Measured the same day, asking the local model again at temperature 0, which gave three of those names the same refusal that night's prompt had: with the key as it is now handed, 50 of the 51 and all 28 passed on the first draft with no figure written past two places, and NVDA, the one name whose facts then carried a filing, passed with its revenue written as 96.22 billion and its growth as 105.9 per cent. The one refused was a company whose name carries a number, Phillips 66.

**The overnight queue is bounded by time, not by a count of names** One pass may take four minutes and another twelve, so a name count does not bound anything. The queue works in priority order until a stated number of hours has passed and then stops; whatever is left is first in line the next night or is written on demand when you open it. A busy night degrades by leaving names for later rather than by running into the morning.

**The overnight run holds the machine awake and reports whether it ran** A laptop left alone will sleep, and a nightly job that silently did not happen is worse than no nightly job, because nothing announces it. The run asserts wakefulness while it works, and the run page states the previous night's outcome including how many queued passes completed and how many were left.

**The overnight queue is bounded by its own limit rather than the night's deadline, and starts no pass once the limit has passed** The queue is bounded by time (see: The overnight queue is bounded by time, not by a count of names), and that leaves two things open a later session could read either way. The first is what bounds it. The night has a deadline of three times its wall clock that it cannot move (see: A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has a two-hour deadline it cannot move), sized for the arithmetic, and a queue held to it would get whatever the arithmetic left of fifteen minutes, so an hour's limit would be cut to the rest of that quarter hour with nothing saying so. So the deadline bounds steps 1 to 16, step 17 is handed none of it, and the queue is bounded by its own limit alone. The second is what the limit stops. A pass is a name's sections, each a call to the local model and a check, and one cut off inside a call leaves a draft written and never checked, which waits on the checker and keeps the next pass from writing that section again. So the limit is read before each pass starts: a pass started before it runs to its end, a name whose turn comes at or after it is left for the next night, and the queue's row names every name it left. The cost is that the queue runs past its limit by at most one pass, which 6.10 measured at 5.13 seconds at the slowest over 12 passes on this machine.

**A night the overnight queue did not run is a traded session with no queue row, read on the run page against the exchange calendar** Section 18 says a night the machine slept writes nothing and the next run reports the gap, and a gap has two readings a later session could take: the nights whose arithmetic ran and whose queue did not, or every session the exchange traded. A machine asleep at the scheduled instant runs neither the arithmetic nor the queue, so the first reading misses exactly the night the row is about. The run page therefore names each session the exchange traded that carries no queue row, from the one after the newest night before the page's own on which the queue ran, up to and including the page's night, and names none before the first night the queue ever ran, since the store's nights from before the queue existed are not nights it failed to run. Nothing is written for such a night: a queue that ran writes its row, a queue that did not writes nothing, and the page reads the absence against the calendar rather than leaving a reader to infer it from an empty region.

**A local model writes prose from numbers and each paid job names the hosted model the operator chooses in configuration** A local model on the operator's GPU writes prose from numbers, which is small and free. A hosted model does research, which is a large-context job a local model does badly. Which hosted model a paid job uses is the profile its configuration names, the operator's to choose, so another is chosen by changing one word and not by superseding an entry here (see: A paid job names its model profile in one word the operator switches, and a profile is priced at its configured rates at its call's own timestamp). The shipped configuration named DeepSeek V4 Flash for research from 6.7, chosen on cost at a few cents a pass. On 2026-09-29 the operator moved research to Claude Sonnet 5.5, a pass priced from the last six DeepSeek passes' own token counts at Sonnet 5.5's rates coming to 0.84 to 1.30 dollars against the 0.04 to 0.05 they cost, an upper figure since DeepSeek counts its reasoning as output; MDT's report on it cost 0.76 dollars, and the same day the operator moved research back to DeepSeek to set the two reports on MDT side by side. The actual cost of each report written on Claude Sonnet 5.5 is recorded against that estimate until five are, over whichever days research names it (see: A report costing more than two dollars is named on the run page the morning after it was written). Every section records the model that wrote it, so a change of model shows on each section written after it and on none written before (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole).

Supersedes **A local model writes prose from numbers and each paid job names its hosted model in configuration**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that it kept research on Claude Sonnet 5.5.

**A research pass is split by section difficulty, not run wholesale on one model** The dividing test is whether the answer sits inside one document or has to be built across several that disagree. Classification and extraction stay on the local model at no cost, and each such call carries only the documents that section needs rather than the whole evidence set, which is what keeps it inside a consumer card's memory. Synthesis goes to the paid model, because a small model given contradictory evidence produces something fluent that says nothing. Figure 12.2 shows where the line falls. The line itself is provisional: the frozen fixture makes it measurable, so each section is run both ways against the fixture and the boundary is settled by that comparison rather than asserted here (owes: The research lane boundary measured against the fixture both ways).

**The local lane calls the one model its settings flag as the default, and a profile it cannot read is the local model unavailable** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-06: "I want us to be able to use gemma4 in LM studio from now on", with every setting of the lane "load from settings", each model "a setting provision" of its own and "a flag maybe like isDefaultModel" to switch between them, "for now default it to gemma4", and the models switched by hand to compare them a day at a time; and, asked whether settings flagging both models or neither should refuse the whole night or stop only its overnight queue, "alternative", the second. The local model is reached over HTTP at an OpenAI-compatible chat completions endpoint on the operator's own machine, which is the interface LM Studio, `llama.cpp`'s own server and Ollama all serve, so the runtime is the operator's choice and not the build's. Each model the operator runs is a profile of its own under the local lane's settings, named by a word and carrying its base address, its model identifier, the per-call timeout, the context it is loaded with and how long its load may take, and the lane calls the one profile flagged as the default. No value has a default in the code, where the address and the model measured on the machine this was first built for once stood: a model the code names is one the operator did not choose, and a switch that left a stale default in place would call it without saying so. So settings naming no profile, flagging none or flagging two, a flagged profile missing a value or holding one that is not a number, and a key the lane read before the profiles, which a lane that passed over it would leave silently not the model called, each leave the lane calling no model, in a line naming what was read. The lane is then the local model unavailable with that line, which is what a runtime not answering already is: the night runs every step and its overnight queue's row says it could not run and why, and a pass on demand writes the paid lane's sections and leaves the local lane's absent with the line. The flags are edited by hand to switch models a day at a time, and a slip in them costing the night's arithmetic would cost far more than the drafts it is about. Gemma 4 is the default from 2026-10-06, with Qwen 3.5 9B, the model this was first built for, kept beside it (see: The local and paid boundary is a property of the machine, not of the design). The identifier is stored on every section the model wrote, which is what makes a comparison between two models a reading of the record rather than a memory of which was running, and why a switch needs no command of its own. A run over a capture calls the profile its recordings were made under, from the capture's own models file, so a switch of the shipped default moves no recorded test, and a capture whose models file cannot be read refuses the run, since that is the suite's own file and not a setting switched by hand. A local call needs no key, and a key configured for the local lane, at the lane or in a profile, refuses the night and the pass before they start rather than being sent, because a local endpoint that authenticates is an endpoint on somebody else's machine and the queue's zero-cost property rests on this lane being free.

**The local lane loads its model at its profile's context before its first call of a night or a pass, and loads again a model held at a smaller one** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-06: "Dont forget for it to wait for the model to load and not just fail while it's loading." A model the runtime has not loaded answers no section until it is, and asked for by name it is loaded on demand inside the call: Gemma 4 took 19.8 seconds so on this machine, at the runtime's own context of 32,000 tokens rather than the 50,176 its profile names, so a call bounded by a section's timeout fails on a model about to answer and one that does answer reads a context the lane did not plan its prompts for. So before its first call the lane reads the runtime's own list of its models and, where its model is listed and not loaded, loads it at its profile's context and waits for the load to answer that it is loaded, which LM Studio sends only once it is, within the allowance the profile states; a load measured at 15.3 seconds against an allowance of 600 is the allowance bounding a runtime that has hung rather than one loading. The list is read once for a pass on demand and once for a night's overnight queue, whose passes share one lane, so a night does not read it again between names. Any other model loaded, whatever its kind, is unloaded first, so the runtime holds one model at a time, which is the operator's standing word on the machine's memory. A model already listed is loaded or loading, since the list names an instance from the moment its load starts, and the list states the context each instance was loaded at. One listed at its profile's context or more, or at one the list does not state, is not loaded again and its first call carries the load's allowance beside its own timeout; a runtime that answers no such list, being another runtime or one not answering it in time, is called the same way. One listed at a smaller context is unloaded after every other model and loaded again at its profile's context, waited for as a model not loaded is, because on the evening of 2026-10-06 Gemma 4 stood loaded at 32,000 tokens from a load by hand, and called as it was it would have refused every prompt past that size, as the captured overflow refusal names the loaded context it ran past. A model the runtime does not hold, a load it refuses and one past its allowance are the local model unavailable, so the pass or the queue leaves every section of the lane unwritten with why rather than failing them one call at a time, and the reason is kept for the rest of the pass or the night: a later round of the pass answers with it at once rather than loading again, since a load that failed once would otherwise be waited out again round after round. An address the runtime cannot be asked at, and a timeout or an allowance past a day, are settings the lane cannot read, as a missing value is.
**The local lane is a configured list of section names, and the prose writer writes whatever the list holds** Which sections the local model owns is a setting, and a setting with no shape becomes whatever the first implementation does (see: The local lane's scope is a setting, and whatever it holds is written for nothing). It is a list of section names in configuration, read once per pass, and the writer takes the list rather than deciding section by section, so moving a section between lanes changes a value and touches no code. On the machine this is first built for the list holds the cause of each large move, what the company sells, the segment commentary and the key under each figure, which is the short-prompt work figure 12.2 puts on the left (see: A research pass is split by section difficulty, not run wholesale on one model). The short version is not in it, and that is the list's value rather than a fact about the component: section 4, figure 12.2's table and section 15.12 each put it in the paid lane, while the prose writer's catalogue row said it wrote that section on the local model at no cost. Neither side was a design fact, so neither is superseded and nothing is corrected except the row, which now names the lane where it named the section. A section in the list the machine cannot hold is refused before the pass starts and left for the paid path, which section 18 already promises, so a list edited past what the card holds degrades rather than failing.

**The fixture comparison moved the cause of each large move into the paid lane on this machine** The lane decisions leave which sections the local model writes to a setting, and say the comparison against the fixture decides whether a section moves (see: The local lane's scope is a setting, and whatever it holds is written for nothing). 6.8 ran it: one name's pass over one evidence set, every section asked of the local model and of the research model, each through the claim checker and its one retry. The research model's sections were accepted for all eight it could write. The local model's were accepted for three, what the company sells, the segment commentary and the key under each figure, and left out for the other five, the cause of each large move among them: handed the documents inside the name's eight moves, it wrote figures from the articles that the facts file does not hold, and wrote them again when told why, in every pass it wrote that section in. So the list on this machine is those three, and the cause of each large move is written in the paid lane, at about a cent a pass. The entry that first stated the list named the cause among the local sections before any comparison had run; its rule, that the lane is a list the writer takes whole, stands, and what this changes is the list's value. The four synthesis sections failed locally as that entry expected. A different machine runs the comparison again rather than taking this list (see: The local and paid boundary is a property of the machine, not of the design).

**A paid model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, and a job never falls back from the profile it names** The endpoint was already configuration and that is not enough, because two providers do not share a request shape, so the choice of provider is a code path even where the address is not. A paid model is one interface with one implementation per wire format: the OpenAI chat completions format DeepSeek serves, and Claude's own messages interface, which the operator's brief of 2026-09-28 chose over Anthropic's OpenAI compatibility layer, since the provider describes that layer as for testing and the labeller needs the structured output the native interface provides. A job's profile names which format answers, it is resolved where the on-demand feeds are resolved, refusing in both directions, and it never falls back to another profile. A fallback would make the model that wrote a section unknowable from the fact that the section exists, and every section is already dated and stamped with the model that wrote it for exactly that reason (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole). A profile naming a key the secrets file does not hold stops that job with a plain line: the research job's pass writes it as its own run log row, which the drain settles the request under and the run page draws, and the drain reads its peak windows from the profile without the key, so a missing key stops each pass rather than stopping the drain where nothing would draw why. A Claude key not scoped to a workspace names its workspace beside it in the secrets file, and the feed sends it on every request. The switch is also what makes the lane boundary measurable rather than asserted, because the recording is keyed on the canonicalised request, and the fixture's recordings are read under the profiles the fixture's own `models.json` names, so a switch the operator makes moves no recorded test.

Supersedes **The research model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, chosen by configuration and never falling back**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that a key missing at startup refuses the whole command rather than the job.

**Research names a profile per section as well as per job, and a Claude profile states its thinking** Ruled in the 12.6 research template on 2026-09-30, from the operator's brief that one model writes every section and nothing lets a section that keeps failing on one model be tried on another. The research job's settings gain a map from each of figure 12.2's nine sections to a profile word, and a section is written by the profile it names, the job's `Use` writing any the map does not hold; it ships naming the job's own profile for all nine, so nothing moves until the operator changes a word. A map naming a section figure 12.2 does not name, or a profile the profiles do not hold, refuses the job at startup by name, as a key the secrets file does not hold does, and no other profile answers for it, for the reason the decision above gives. Each profile's calls go through a spend cap of its own over the one ledger, so the day's and the month's caps hold across them; a pass asks each profile its sections name whether it answers before it starts; a section records its own profile's identity; and the drain waits out the peak windows of every profile the map names, with the queue page stating the same start. A Claude profile states its thinking as off or as an effort level, `low` to `max`, and never as a token budget, because the models this build calls refuse a budget with a 400: captured from Sonnet 5.5 on 2026-09-30, the refusal names adaptive thinking and an effort level in its place. Off is sent as thinking between tools and not as thinking disabled, because the same capture shows Sonnet 5.5 refusing disabled with a 400 that names between tools as the form that turns thinking off, and a request with no tools has nothing between which to think; the answer to that form came back with no thinking. An effort level is sent in the output settings beside the answer's format. A thinking setting on the other wire format, or beside options setting thinking or the output settings themselves, is refused at startup, and a model asked with one is recorded as a different writer, since the same model thinking and not thinking are two writers a reader would want told apart.

**A trial asks a second profile for named sections beside a report, and ships naming none** Ruled in the same pass, and its shipped profile on the operator's word of 2026-09-30. Choosing a model for a section wants the two models' drafts over the same request side by side, and a trial that wrote into the report would change what the page draws on the strength of a draft nobody had yet compared. So a trial names a profile, the sections it is asked for, the reports it runs over and the day it counts them from. After a pass writes one of those sections in its paid lane at the first draft, the trial asks its profile with the request the pass built for that draft, the same facts file and documents and, for the short version, the sections the pass had accepted, and checks the answer in memory by the claim checker's own rules, told once what was refused as a pass's retry is. It writes no research section; it writes a run log row a section under the pass's run holding each round's draft, verdict and price, the outcome and the cost, and its calls stand under a round of their own, which the run page leaves out of a report's count and cost and the spend caps count as they count every call. It stops by itself once it has run over the reports it names, so its cost is bounded by that number rather than by attention, three as shipped. It ships naming no profile, so no report is tried until the operator names one: it first shipped naming Claude Sonnet 5.5 with thinking off for the short version and the two cases, and the operator ruled before any report was tried that report generation asks DeepSeek alone (see: Report generation asks DeepSeek alone, and no research setting names a Claude profile). The operator chooses a section's model by changing that section's word in the map.

Supersedes **A trial asks a second profile for named sections after a report and records its drafts beside the report, never in it**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the profile it shipped naming.

**The run page draws how each report's sections came out and each section's rates over the newest twenty reports, and no trial's drafts** Ruled in the same pass, and the drafts moved off the page on the operator's word of 2026-09-30. The run page draws, in its detail, one row for each report of the seven nights the research region reads, a report being a research pass whose paid model answered at least once, with a cell for each section: passed first time where its accepted version was the pass's first draft, passed on retry where it was the retry, left out with the line the pass's row names it by or the checker's refusal as the name page words it, and not warranted where it stood from an earlier day. Each cell carries what that section's own calls cost, every round, and the report what all its calls cost; a trial's calls and a review's are in neither, being spend beside the report. The industry cycle is read off the theme pass under the same run. The two cases' cell is marked where any of the pass's drafts carried a figure on both sides, the first as well as the retry, since what is counted is what the ask produced and not what the checker let through, and the page states how many of the newest twenty reports that drafted the two cases carried one. Each section's share passed first time and share left out are read over the newest twenty reports that warranted it. Twenty is a display window and not a measured number: one report moves a rate by a twentieth, and a change to a section's model or ask shows within a few weeks of reports. Where fewer have been written the count read is stated beside each rate. The drafts a trial or a review wrote beside a report are drawn on no page: they are written to files by a command, since the operator reads them to choose a model or an ask and not to run the night (see: The drafts compared beside a report and the research template's before and after counts are written to files by a command, and drawn on no page).

Supersedes **The run page draws how each report's sections came out and each section's rates over the newest twenty reports**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the drafts drawn beneath.

**The drafts compared beside a report and the research template's before and after counts are written to files by a command, and drawn on no page** Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-30, asked where the comparisons should go: in files, written by a command the operator's session runs, with no page drawing them. The read surface started with the verb `comparisons` reads every report the store holds through the read API and writes into the checkout's `sampleReports` folder, which the repository ignores, and writes nothing to the store and starts no page. It writes a file for each report a trial or a review asked beside, holding each section with every model's drafts side by side, composed by the marks the run page drew them with, with the app's stylesheet inline and every disclosure open. It writes each section's share passed first time and share left out over the ten reports whose passes started before the research prompt's addendum merged and the ten that started after, once the tenth after has been written, as the addendum asks. And it writes, of the reports whose passes started after the two cases were asked to argue each fact on one side, the first twenty that drafted the two cases and how many carried a figure on both sides, beside the 5 of 8 accepted drafts measured before the ask changed, once the twentieth has been written, which is the report the carried obligation owes. A report is placed by the instant its run is named for, since one day holds several, and a report started at the merge's own second counts as after it. Until a window is full the command says how far it has come and writes no file for it, since a comparison over fewer reports than the ruling names is not the comparison it asked for.

**The research prompt repeats each section's ask after the documents, names its reader and marks each document by kind** Ruled in the addendum to the research template of 2026-09-30, found by reading the prompt every research call sends. Five changes, each for a reason of its own. The section's ask is said again after the documents and the sections already written, under "Now write the section:", word for word, because several long documents put the ask at the top far behind where the model writes; a retry's brief still comes last, so what to fix is the last thing read. The instructions name the reader, someone deciding whether to hold a swing trade in the stock over the next weeks to few months, so a section chooses what could move the stock before and at its next report and leaves out what would not; the section still describes and never prescribes. Beside the rule that no figure is written that the facts do not list, the instructions say what to do instead: what a figure only a document gives shows is said without the number, as that orders rose sharply, and a change no listed figure sizes is described the same way against its base, since a writer told only what to leave out wrote the figure anyway. The sentence was first written as stating such a figure in words, and the recording made under it showed the writer spelling the number out, above two billion dollars and eighty to ninety per cent, which the checker refuses as it refuses the figure; it was reworded and every recording made again. That recording also showed a writer answering the industry cycle with a lone citation marker, which the checker read as a sentence citing a document and accepted, so an answer holding nothing but markers is now no answer, as one holding only an invisible character already was. Each document is listed with its kind, a company filing or release, a news report, or an opinion or promotional piece, read by a stated rule from its address: the filings archive and the release wires are the company's own, sites publishing contributors' and promoters' views and the contributors' part of a mixed site are opinion, and every other address is news; a point rests on the first two, and an opinion piece is cited only for what its writer argues, said to be that. A syndicated opinion piece reads as news by this rule, since a portal's address says nothing of what it carries, and the news labeller's kind is used in its place once the labeller exists. And the two cases and the risks are each given a weak point and a strong one as examples, written with no digit so nothing in them can be copied into a draft or refused in one. Every recording is keyed on the prompt, so every research recording was made again once.

**A review asks a section's model to check its own draft against the section's rules, beside a stated number of reports** Ruled in the same addendum, and its shipped profile on the operator's word of 2026-09-30. After a pass writes the two cases or the risks at the first draft, a review asks the profile it names, as the addendum puts it a second call to the model that wrote the draft, with the request the pass built and the draft handed back, to read it a sentence at a time: a reason rather than a restated figure, the size of a change weighed, specific to this company, and for a risk a confirmation that is what the risk coming true would look like; and to answer with the whole section revised in the form the section asks for. The revision is read by the claim checker's own rules in memory, told once what was refused as a retry is, and written beside the report on the run log and never into it, as a trial's drafts are, under a stage and a round of its own that the run page leaves out of a report's count and cost. It stops by itself after the reports it names, three as shipped counted from 2026-09-30, and its drafts are set beside the pass's own and the trial's where the trial asked for the same section. It first shipped naming no profile; the operator reviewed the addendum's pull request and ruled it on, naming DeepSeek, the model that writes both sections, so the next three reports that write either section are reviewed and then it stops. Whether it earns its call is read off the drafts it writes beside the pass's.

Supersedes **A review asks a section's model to check its own draft against the section's rules, behind a setting that ships off**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the profile it shipped naming.

**Report generation asks DeepSeek alone, and no research setting names a Claude profile** Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-30, on the cost measured that day. The one report written on Claude, MDT's of 2026-09-29 on Claude Sonnet 5.5, took 12 calls for $0.7564: 286,932 input tokens, none read from the provider's cache, at $2.00 a million, and 18,253 output tokens at $10.00. The DeepSeek report on MDT written the same day for comparison cost $0.0490. The operator's Claude balance stood at $0.86 that morning, drawn down by the fixture's Claude recordings rather than by reports. So the research job's profile, every section's word in its map, the trial and the review name DeepSeek or nothing, and the trial, the one setting that still asked Claude beside a report, ships naming none. The Claude profiles and the Claude wire format stay in the settings and the code, since a later ruling may name one; naming one in a research setting is refused by the suite until a decision replaces this one, because a word changed in the settings file moves money without moving any code a review would read.

**The archive declares a contact in its user agent, and a blank one refuses at startup** The filings archive is the first provider here that refuses a request on a transport rule rather than on a credential: it answers a request naming no user agent with a refusal, and its fair-access policy asks that the agent carry contact details. So the agent is a credential path in shape and not in kind, and it is built like one. The header is composed from the tool name, a version read off the assembly and a configured contact, and a contact that is absent or blank refuses at startup rather than at the first request, which is the shape the credential path has had since 2.1: a run that reached the archive and came back refused would read as the archive being down. The contact is a dedicated address rather than the operator's own, and the reason is not privacy in the abstract. The value is configuration in the gitignored secrets file beside the provider keys, and a value that identifies a person sitting in that file is one more thing that must never reach a captured fixture or a committed manifest, so the manifest's credential scan covers the agent and a dedicated address narrows what a leak would be. Declining to send contact details at all was considered and rejected: it trades a real refusal risk for one omitted string, since a caller that declines is one the archive may rate-limit, and the corpus would then have to describe and handle a failure mode it created for itself.

**A stored source is not automatically an admissible one** Requiring that a claim name a stored document proves where a sentence came from, and proves nothing about whether that document is worth believing. A search for a company by name returns machine-generated price forecasts, broker marketing pages quoting contract-for-difference prices rather than the stock, year-old articles carrying a previous fiscal year's guidance with no visible date, and summaries written by other AI systems. Each of those would satisfy a bare provenance rule. So admissibility is a second test, applied before a document is stored: deny algorithmic price predictions, broker and platform marketing pages, AI-written summaries, and quote or hub pages carrying no article; require a publish date and reject anything outside the window the pass asked for; prefer the company's own filings and releases, then established financial press, and make the pass state why it used anything else. A document that fails is not stored, so a claim resting on it cannot be written.

**Gated sources are out of scope, and access is ranked before reputation** Paywalled and login-walled sites cannot be read by an automated pass and no attempt is made to work around them. The order of preference follows access rather than prestige: filings and company releases first, which are free, complete and fetched by ticker rather than searched; then the licensed news feed, where the provider delivers article text under its own distribution agreement so the paywall question does not arise; then the open web, which is where gating actually bites. What is genuinely lost is the analyst-opinion layer and primary industry research. Where the substance of a gated report reaches free outlets as secondary coverage, that coverage is the source and is marked as second-hand rather than presented as the original.

**Two source lists govern the open web, and the licensed feed is a channel rather than a list** Filings and company releases need no list, since they are fetched by ticker. A company-news list governs reporting about a company and an industry list governs theme material, because the publishers who write about memory contract pricing are not the ones who write about quarterly earnings, and splitting them keeps a theme search from returning earnings coverage and the reverse. Every list carries a review date, because sites are acquired, paywalled and abandoned, and a list nobody revisits silently narrows what the system can see.

Two is what the previous entry described and three is what it was called, which is the whole of contradiction J. What is new is where the lists apply. The 1.7 measurement found that the licensed news feed delivers 1,173 of 1,253 articles under one domain, and that the domain is the aggregator that carried the article rather than the outlet that wrote it: the writing outlet appears inside the text where it appears at all. So a domain list over the news feed would hold three entries and would filter nothing, and filtering the feed by domain would discard the whole feed or none of it. The lists therefore govern the search tool, which is where a query can return a football club or a forum post, and the news feed is admitted as a delivery channel with the per-document admissibility test doing all of the work on its articles. (see: Admissibility is judged per document, after the fetch, and never per publisher)

**A list of publishers is a noise filter, never a correctness test** The list decides which sites a search may return; the per-document test decides whether a particular article may be believed. Both are needed and neither substitutes for the other, because a publisher that belongs on any reasonable list also runs promotional opinion pieces and syndicates other people's, and a trial search returned exactly that from a well-regarded name. A list also has to be of sites that are reputable and fetchable, which is a smaller set than reputable: the most authoritative names in this field are the gated ones, so a list assembled by prestige is substantially a list of pages that return nothing.

**Admissibility is judged per document, after the fetch, and never per publisher** A publisher that runs ordinary financial journalism also runs machine-generated price forecasts, so denying the domain throws away the good with the bad and allowing it admits the forecasts. A search tool's domain filter cannot express this, since it operates on the site rather than the article, which means search parameters reduce noise and the admissibility test does the actual work, on the document, after it has been retrieved.

**Each denied category is refused by a marker the document carries, and the domain is never one of them** The decision above says the test is per document and says nothing about what the test reads, and what it reads had to be measured rather than guessed, because a rule over words refuses whatever shares a word. Eight pages were asked for by hand on 2026-09-13 UTC, two per denied category, and read against the four articles and two release exhibits the committed fixture already holds. Seven answered and one refused automated access, which produced section 18's unretrievable case as a measurement rather than a construction.

Four findings decided the rules. The first is that the domain says nothing, measured rather than argued: finance.yahoo.com delivered a page that must be refused, a price prediction piece with a named author, a publish date and twelve paragraphs of prose, and a page that must be admitted, an ordinary earnings preview, and it is the same domain the fixture's four captured articles arrive under. The second is that a price forecast page need not say it is one: the forecast site read carries five prose paragraphs, a table of yearly figures out to 2050 and no statement anywhere that an algorithm produced any of it, so the marker is the title and the address rather than the text, and it is a pairing of a price word with a prediction word rather than either alone, because real reporting pairs forecast with revenue and guidance constantly. The third is that three of the five refusable pages carried no publish date at all, which is why the gates are judged with the kind before the date: a build judging the date first would refuse most of what it refuses for the cheapest reason available and would report almost nothing about the kinds, and what is stored is one verdict. The fourth is that the regulatory risk warning is the one reliable marker of a page that exists to open an account, verbatim in two forms on the page read, and that no article warns its own reader about losing money. The fifth came from the live run this checkpoint made after the rules were written, over 411 real articles from one dated request, and it is the reason the rules were changed rather than confirmed: invitation language does not separate an article about accounts from a page selling one. Three of the 411 were refused as marketing and all three are ordinary consumer-finance reporting. Two tripped on one invitation written twice, a piece on what retirees put off saying sign up and signing up about a benefit, which counting kinds rather than phrases repairs. The third survived that repair, an article on how savers lose a retirement pot saying opening an account and sign up, which are two genuinely different invitations inside an article whose subject is accounts. So nothing rests on invitation language alone, and the second net is a leveraged-product term beside an invitation, measured on the same 411 before anything rested on it: contract for difference appears 0 times, cfd 0, spread bet twice and trading platform three times, and not one of those five carries any invitation at all.

So: a price forecast is refused on a price word paired with a prediction word in its title or its address, on an address whose last segment is the forecast itself, or on a stated algorithmic projection in its text. A marketing page is refused on one regulatory risk warning, or on a leveraged-product term beside one invitation to open an account, the pairing rather than the invitation because a single sign-up line is in the navigation of half the web and two of them are in any article about saving. A summary is refused where it declares that a system wrote it, and only then. A quote or hub page is refused where its address or title says it is a symbol page and no paragraph of it runs to at least 3 sentences and at least 40 words, both halves required. The thresholds are measured against the four captured articles and not chosen: the tightest of them carries exactly one paragraph that qualifies, at three sentences and fifty-two words, so a threshold of four sentences would refuse a real article, and that thin margin is why the shape is a second condition rather than the whole rule.

Two limits are stated rather than left to be discovered. Text a system wrote and nobody disclosed is not something a rule over the text can find, so the marker catches what platforms say about themselves and nothing more; what stands behind it is the preference for primary sources and the claim checker, since a number absent from the facts file cannot reach prose whatever wrote the page it came from. And an article about the regulation of leveraged products, quoting the warning, would be refused: that is one article lost against admitting pages whose whole content is marketing.

The preference for primary sources orders and never refuses, which is what keeps it from becoming the publisher list this corpus has twice ruled out. It ranks by the channel rather than by the host, because the host of a news feed article is the aggregator and ranking by it would put every article in the feed in one place and that place would be the wrong one. (see: Admissibility is judged per document, after the fetch, and never per publisher) (see: A stored source is not automatically an admissible one)

**Theme material comes from a search tool, and per-name material never does** A per-name pass reads filings and news fetched by ticker, which is exhaustive over a date range and cannot return the wrong company. An industry's own pricing cycle is published by research firms and filed with nobody, so that one section needs open search. The tool is Tavily, called as one more fetching tool inside the research loop; its results are stored as documents exactly like a filing or an article, so the claim checker and the admissibility test apply unchanged. Volume is a few hundred searches a year against a free allowance of a thousand a month, so cost is not a factor and will not become one. A self-hosted meta-search is the fallback if that allowance changes, since nothing in the design depends on which tool answers.

**A theme search is scoped by parameter, not by hope** Trial searches showed what an unscoped query returns: for a two-letter ticker, results about a football club; for a company name, a machine-generated price forecast, a broker marketing page, a year-old article carrying a previous fiscal year's guidance, and another AI system's summary. So a theme search names the industry rather than a ticker, is bounded by an explicit date range, restricts results to the industry source list, and requests full page text rather than snippets, because a snippet cannot be stored as the document a claim rests on.

**A theme pass searches each site on the industry list alone, and hands the model a bounded set of the pages they return** Measured at 6.11 over the quarter to 2026-09-08. One search restricted to the whole list of twelve sites returned nothing for any of the fixture's four industries, Consumer Electronics, Scientific & Technical Instruments, Software - Infrastructure and Entertainment, nor for Semiconductors, which had returned ten results over the same list at 6.9. Restricted to four sites it returned nothing; to three, nothing once and eight results once; to two, ten results each of three times, and in two of the three one site took all ten; asked at the tool's advanced depth over the whole list, it returned nothing for one industry and ten results for the other, eight of them from sites the list does not carry. Restricted to one site, each of the four industries drew results from five to nine of the twelve sites, and 63, 34, 47 and 21 of them in that order were on the site with their text and a publish date. So the tool answers a restriction to more than one site with nothing, with one site's pages or with pages from outside it, and the form that returns what each site holds is a search a site. A search a site is not steady either: a quarter of an hour later, capturing the fixture's searches in this form, semiconductors.org returned nothing for Semiconductors, and worldsteel.org returned three pages for Scientific & Technical Instruments from three sites that are not it, which a pass drops. What the form changes is that a search fails a site at a time rather than the whole list at once. The third form the question named, searching only the sites a measurement shows covering an industry, is not taken: the map is twelve searches an industry for every industry the index names before a pass saves one, and it goes stale as the tool's answers move, which the two readings a quarter of an hour apart already show. A pass makes one search for each site on the list, asking each for its first three results, which is the fewest at which each of the four industries had ten pages to hand. It hands the model at most ten of the pages it admitted, every site's first before any site's second, so a site whose pages are about something else does not crowd out the rest. And it carries each page as its first 30,000 characters: that holds 135 of the 172 pages with text the searches returned whole, the median page being 9,785 characters, while a government release of a million and a half characters is carried as its opening, and ten pages at the most come to under 110,000 tokens, inside the shipped model's context and under two cents at its off-peak rate. The page is stored whole, so a citation opens the document rather than the part the model read. A pass costs twelve searches rather than one. A basic search is billed at one credit, so the few hundred theme passes a year the tool was chosen for come to a few thousand searches, which is inside a free allowance of a thousand a month. (see: A theme search is scoped by parameter, not by hope) (see: Theme material comes from a search tool, and per-name material never does)

**A theme page is handed to the model only where its text names the industry** A theme pass's searches return pages from the sites on the industry list that are about the industry and pages that only mention it: the first live pass for Semiconductors handed its call ten pages, and four of them were a national productivity release, the advance report on durable goods, a central bank note on Vietnam's exports and another company's credit rating, each naming semiconductors once to eight times in twenty to forty thousand characters, beside industry pages naming it sixteen to sixty-nine times in every ten thousand. The industry cycle written from them cited all four. So a page is handed only where the industry's words, those of its name less its separators and the words the index uses to qualify an industry rather than name it, appear at least five times in every ten thousand characters of its title and text, the pages kept in the order the pass kept them; over those ten pages that keeps the five about the industry and leaves the five that mention it, the credit rating among them at 3.8. A pass whose pages all fall below it hands the model nothing and writes no cycle, saying the searches returned no page about the industry, which costs a call nothing. The count is a floor and not a judgement: a page that repeats an industry's word without being about it passes, and an industry whose name is a common word keeps more than it should, which costs a call's evidence some focus and never admits a page a site outside the list serves. (see: A theme pass searches each site on the industry list alone, and hands the model a bounded set of the pages they return)

**An industry cycle the model declined is named as declined for lack of industry sources** Measured over the operator's store on 2026-09-30, ten theme passes over nine industries: the two industries whose cycle was written were handed about ten pages about the industry, and the six declined industries, over seven passes, one to five, most of them market stubs, job pages and census tables, since the industry list carried no site for health care, chemicals, defense, autos or insurers. The models then answered with nothing, or with a draft saying the documents did not support a cycle, and the page said the answer was empty. So a cycle is declined where a call was made over at least one page about the industry and both answers came back empty or cut short, or the answer cited none of the pages it was handed, and its line says so with what the searches found: declined for lack of industry sources, how many pages about the industry and from which sites, and, where the source list carries no site of its own for the member's sector, that sector, which is what a review adds sites for. A draft citing none of its pages is not stored and not asked for again, because a retry repeated the decline each time it ran. The theme pass's row keeps each page it handed with its site, its title and how densely it names the industry, so the next reading of a declined cycle reads the row rather than rebuilding what the pass had. A pass whose pages all fall below the density rule, and one storing nothing, name the sector the same way. (see: A theme page is handed to the model only where its text names the industry) (see: Industry research is per theme, not per name)

**A theme search adds its sector's sites, and a site joins the list only where a measurement found industry material on it** The industry list gains sites per sector, searched beside the list's own for a theme whose member the index names in that sector, one search a site as the list's own are searched, the sector read from the membership row the industry is read from. A proposed site joins its sector only by a rule stated before it is measured: for at least one declined industry of the sector it returned at least one page admissibility admitted and the density rule reads as about that industry, measured by the worker's own theme search, admissibility and density rule over the quarter to the day, writing nothing to the store. The operator ruled on 2026-09-29 that the rule decides rather than the operator. On 2026-09-30, over 26 searches for the six declined industries, thirteen of sixteen proposed sites joined, five for Healthcare, three for Industrials, two for Consumer Cyclical and three for Basic Materials, and iata.org and nada.org, which returned nothing, and bea.gov, whose one admitted page was not about the industry, stayed out; `source-lists.json` records each site's result. The other seven sectors carry no site of their own until one of their industries is declined, which the declined line names. (see: Two source lists govern the open web, and the licensed feed is a channel rather than a list) (see: An industry cycle the model declined is named as declined for lack of industry sources)

**The model never fetches; components fetch and hand it documents** The research model has no web search of its own, and this is the right shape regardless: every document entering a pass is stored as it is fetched, which is exactly what the claim checker needs to verify a claim's source. A model that searched for itself would produce claims backed by documents nothing kept.

**A research pass reads a name's news from the last three months alone, inside each stored move and since the company's own filing, and hands each section the documents code picks from it** Supersedes **A research pass reads a name's news inside each stored move and since the company's own filing, and hands each section the documents code picks from it, the company's own filing first**, moved below with its reasoning, which stands but for four things the first live research pass found on NVDA on 2026-09-18. What a pass reads. The span of each stored move and the days from the company's own filing to the night, overlapping spans read once, as before, and none of it earlier than three months before the night, by the operator's ruling that day. The year's moves are ranked by change and a name's largest are often most of a year old, and that pass read 10,709 of NVDA's articles, 7,117 of them from the last three months, to hand its sections a few dozen. A move older than three months is read with no document, so its cause is not written. The company's own release is read as before however old it is, since it is the filing the sections rest on. What the cause of a move is handed. Stored moves whose spans share a session are one run of the price measured from several starts: NVDA's eight moves of the year were three runs. Each run is an episode, its cause is asked for once, beginning with the session its largest move ended on, and it is handed at most two documents published inside that largest move: a title naming the company first, then the newest, then the fewest companies named. The newest rather than the earliest, because a move's first session is the one before it moved: the pass handed NVDA's largest move, from 2026-07-29 to 2026-08-05, articles about that first day's selloff, while the ones published on 2026-08-04 and 2026-08-05 named the cause. Each move is listed with which way it went and by how much, as its change fact is written, and the model is told to write nothing for a move no document beside it gives a cause for in that direction, because the newest documents inside a fall can be about the rise before it: over the fixture, KEYS's fall of 13.98% in the five sessions to 2026-08-24 was handed the two newest articles inside it, both about the record results that lifted the shares on 2026-08-19, and the cause written from them gave those results as the cause of the fall. What the sections built across the evidence are handed. The company's own filing and at most six documents published since it, one from each of six equal stretches of the days from the filing to the newest, each the best of its stretch by the same order, and where a stretch has none, the next best of the rest. Picked by the fewest companies and then the newest, all six of NVDA's were from the pass's last two days of a stretch that began 23 days before. Which documents are about the company. The provider attributes a document to every company its text names, so a launch by another company that mentions this one is attributed to this one alone and ranked first on the fewest companies named: NVDA's calendar section was handed Xeal's product launch. A title naming the company, its ticker as a word or the first word of its name with a leading "The" taken off, ranks first in every choice above. The first word rather than the whole name, because a title writes Nvidia and Keysight and never the legal name; a name whose first word is a common one ranks every title using that word with it, which costs order and never a document. Of NVDA's 4,927 articles since its filing, 1,134 name Nvidia or NVDA in the title. The rest stands as the superseded entry states it: code picks the evidence, because choosing the evidence is choosing what a sentence may say (see: Code owns every number); what the company sells and the segment commentary are handed the company's own release; a window the provider has more of than a query reads is refused on the feed's side and recorded as unread; and a judgement of relevance by the model was rejected, which a title rule is not. (see: The model never fetches; components fetch and hand it documents) (see: A cause of a move rests only on a document published inside that move)

**A name's news is asked of the provider a month at a time, and a window it does not answer in time is read as unread** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03, after a report pass for QCOM, the night's first name, stopped on a news window the provider did not answer inside a request's limit on any try. The provider's time to answer a name's news grows with the articles a window holds: asked the same morning, QCOM's quarter answered just inside the limit and a week of it at once, so a heavily covered name's window since its filing can pass the limit on one night and not the next. The feed asks a window a month at a time, each piece from its first day to the day before the same date a month on, and pages each piece as it paged the window; the pieces share no day, so a pass reads the articles it read before. A window that still times out on every try is recorded on the pass's row as unread, as a window the provider has more of than a query reads already is, and the sections are written from the windows that were read, where the timeout had ended the pass with nothing on its row. Two alternatives were rejected: a longer limit for this one fetch, which keeps one large request, makes every pass wait on a slow provider and adds a figure to section 17; and catching the timeout alone, which writes a heavily covered name's report without the news since its filing.

**Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC** Research rates double during two fixed windows that fall late at night in Eastern time, so interactive reading after the close is never billed at peak. Non-interactive passes are scheduled into off-peak on purpose, in UTC, because a schedule written in local time moves into peak when daylight saving changes.

**A pass starts only where the longest pass the store holds would end before a peak window opens** Before it takes a request the drain asks whether a pass started now could still be running when a peak window opens, bounded by the longest pass the store holds that ran to its end, and where it could, it leaves the request outstanding and waits for the window to end. The queue page states the same start from the one function the drain calls, so the two cannot disagree. On the operator's ruling of 2026-09-24, over pausing paid calls inside a window: a wait before the claim keeps the request outstanding, which the queue screen reads as waiting, and nothing recovers a request left being written, since the claim takes only outstanding rows, a withdrawal refuses one being written and the night skips a name holding one, so a pass paused mid-way whose process died would leave its name blocked for good. The longest pass rather than the median, because a pass's paid calls come at its end: the four passes the store held on 2026-09-24 took 3.55, 3.97, 5.43 and 26.28 minutes by their run logs, and NVDA's first spent about 21 of its 26 minutes gathering sources before any paid call, so a 26-minute pass started under a median bound of 5 makes its calls inside the window. The bound is measured rather than guessed and rises if a longer pass ever ends; a store holding no finished pass has no bound, and only a start inside a window moves. What it costs, with the 26-minute bound: on a weekday no pass starts after about 00:34 or 05:34 UTC, so the night's own report starts at once when it is asked about 00:13 UTC, as after the night of 2026-09-23, and when it is asked about 00:40 UTC, as when the overnight queue uses its hour, it waits until 04:00 UTC, midnight in New York before 2026-11-01 and 23:00 after, on nights of Monday to Thursday in New York; a Friday night lands on a Saturday in UTC, which is not a peak day. The bound states its end: a pass longer than it can still make a call answered inside a window, which the spend records at the price charged and the run page counts where it states the priced passes, and two operating rows carry the first such call and a review of the bound at twenty passes (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC).

**A written section describes the company and never proposes a trade, a holding or a plan for income** Every research model is told, in the instructions every section is asked under, to describe and never prescribe: no section proposes a trade, a holding, adding, trimming or an income plan, because the plan is computed by code and drawn in its own region, and a written plan beside it can contradict it. The short version was asked for what is true, what the market is arguing about and what the plan therefore is, and on the operator's comparison of 2026-09-29 DeepSeek's short version for MDT advised holding the position for the income, adding toward the support band and trimming into the resistance band, while the swing trade the list computed for MDT was a trade of its own; Claude Sonnet 5.5's closed on keeping the position and letting the next report decide whether to add. So the short version is now asked for what the documents and the sections already written show to be true and what the market is arguing about, and nothing else. On the same ruling, three asks are held to what the reader needs rather than what the model finds easy: the segment commentary names the segments that moved most or changed direction, at most four, with what the documents give as the cause, because the numbers table already lists every line; each point in the two cases is specific to the company and its latest documents; and each risk's confirmation is an observable event that tells the risk apart from ordinary movement, stated against guidance or the trend, never a bare threshold and never a figure the next quarter crosses by construction. Stated in the instructions and not checked by code, because whether a sentence prescribes or could describe any company is a reading no rule makes reliably; the operator reads the result, section by section, beside the two reports it replaces. (see: Code owns every number)

**A written sentence carrying the word the corpus bans or an em dash is refused as an uncited one is** The corpus's two prose rules, the one banned word in any form and the em dash, hold for every written sentence of every section as they do for every file the repository tracks: the claim checker refuses a sentence carrying either, the draft is asked for once more with the refusal named, and a section refused twice is left out and named on the name page, as a sentence naming no document is. The finding records the draft's own sentence, so the page names the rule and never draws the word. On the operator's ruling of 2026-09-29, after DeepSeek's short version for MDT framed a counterpoint in the banned word. The instructions every section is asked under say so too, so the refusal is what catches a draft the instruction did not, rather than the only thing standing between the word and the page. (see: Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file)

**A retry names each thing the check refused, and a section refused on its third retry is left out** The operator ruled on 2026-09-30, told that the reports on BDX, MO and IT that day each left sections out after one retry, that a section is asked for again up to three times in a pass. BDX's risks show what one retry lost: the first draft wrote "almost 4,000 pending federal cases", a figure an article gave and the facts file does not hold, and the retry, told to remove it or replace it with a listed figure, wrote "thousands of related product liability cases", which the checker refuses as a number in words, and the section was left out though a third draft saying "many pending cases" would have passed. Each retry is told each thing the draft before it was refused for, a line each, with what to do about it, and never the refused draft itself. Until the operator's research template of 2026-09-29 a retry was told the checker's reason in one sentence, and ECL's short version of that day was refused for one date, then written again carrying six figures the facts file does not hold, several of them from other sections' refused first drafts, and left out. So the refused draft is read again by the checker's own rules over the facts file and the source list it was checked against, which gives the findings the checker stored without a column to keep them in, and each finding becomes a line naming the figure, date, citation or sentence, the sentence it sits in, the rule, and what the next draft does: a figure or a date removed or replaced by one the facts file lists, a number written in words also said with no number and no count word, a sentence citing nothing removed or ended on the marker of the document stating it, each other rule its own instruction. The paid lane, the local lane, the theme pass and the overnight queue retry this way, each pass counting its own retries from its first draft, since a refusal after a fallback or on a later day starts the count again. A refusal at the third retry leaves the section out, and where that retry carries a thing the draft before it was refused for, by the same rule over the same text read without case, its reason says the last retry repeated it, so the name page and the run page tell a writer that ignored the line from one that failed on something new. Three is the operator's bound rather than a measurement: each retry of a DeepSeek section costs a fraction of a cent and the retries end where the section is accepted. A replay of recordings made while a section had one retry is checked under that one, since a further retry would ask for an answer nobody recorded. (see: A claim is a sentence, every sentence in a researched section names the document it rests on, and a window written in words is read as its number) (see: Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file)

**The short version is written last from the sections that passed, and one left out is replaced by a summary code writes** The short version is written after the other sections, handed the ones the checker accepted, and held to the facts file's rule as every section is. The research template of 2026-09-29 asked also that it carry no figure the sections it summarises did not, and the operator withdrew that before it was built, on 2026-09-30: measured over the operator's store, it would have refused 4 of the 6 short versions the checker had accepted, RTX's, DGX's, GM's and MDT's DeepSeek one, each for quoting the close, the averages or a total the facts file vouches for, while the fault it was aimed at, ECL's figures copied from articles, is the facts-file rule's to refuse and the retry's to name. The section read first should never be empty, so where no accepted short version stands for a name's night the name page draws one written by code from computed parts alone: why the name is or is not on the list that night, the state its reported quarters give it with the heading its numbers open on, and the entry, stop and target of the swing trade, the plan the trade gate read where it read a swing plan. No model is asked and nothing is stored, so it follows the night the page draws. Its heading states why it stands there, read from the record the page already reads the section's state from: the model's short version was refused by the claim check where the checker left it out, was not written where the newest pass named it not written or no version stands and research was written for the name, and no research has been written for the name where nothing was, in which case no model is implied. The industry's cycle is its theme's and drawn for every member, so it is not research written for the name. (see: Code owns every number) (see: A written section describes the company and never proposes a trade, a holding or a plan for income)

**The two cases are asked to argue a fact on one side only, and a draft doing otherwise is counted rather than refused** The two cases are asked for reasons and not restated figures, for a change stated with its size so a small change reads as small, from the listed figures at both ends or in words against its base, and for each fact to argue one side only, the closing sentences included. The size is never a difference the writer works out, because no change figure is listed and a worked difference is a figure no fact holds: the template's own example, a two-cent raise on eight dollars of guidance, is refused as written. The research template of 2026-09-29 asked that a figure in both cases fail the section, and the operator withdrew that before it was built, on 2026-09-30: of the 8 accepted drafts in the shape the page draws, 5 carry one figure on both sides, and a figure can rightly stand on both, raised guidance on the bull side and growth slowing towards that same guidance on the bear side. So a draft doing otherwise is counted on the run page, and a refusal comes back only if twenty reports after the instruction show the share has not fallen below that measured 5 of 8 (owes: The two cases' figures on both sides counted over twenty reports). A draft whose case for and case against cannot be told apart, not opening on the bull case or holding no sentence opening on the bear case, is refused, because the page draws the two halves by that cut, which the checker and the page share, and the count reads the same halves. (see: A written section is drawn a claim to a row, and the two cases and the risks are asked for in the parts the page draws)

**Each risk is returned as fields and confirmed by a listed fact or an event of one kind, and no two risks share either** The risks are asked for as a JSON object and not as prose: each risk with the listed document stating it, what would be seen if it came true, and why that means it is coming true with the document saying so. What confirms a risk is a listed fact's name, copied as the facts file lists it, with the direction it moves, rises above or falls below, and the level it crosses, a figure the facts file holds, wherever a fact could show it, so that sharing is an exact match and code can later read whether the risk came true. A risk no fact can show, a lawsuit, a regulator's decision or a competitor's launch, names an event instead, given one kind of seven, legal, regulatory, competitive, acquisition, management, supply and other, and no direction or level. No two risks name one fact and no two event risks share a kind, because three risks confirmed by one miss of guidance cannot be told apart, and a real risk with no number is still listed. Code composes the fields into the paragraph a risk the page already draws, opening on its ordinal with what would confirm it in a sentence of its own citing the document the fields named, and stores the fields beside the prose, which the checker reads: an answer not returned as the fields, a fact the file does not list, a level it does not hold, a kind outside the seven, an event carrying a level, and two risks sharing a fact or a kind are refused, each named to the retry. The ask states that a valuation risk is confirmed by the multiple falling and not by it staying high. These are the operator's answers of 2026-09-29 to the research template's second item. (see: Code owns every number) (see: A written section is drawn a claim to a row, and the two cases and the risks are asked for in the parts the page draws)

**A label's reason is one sentence holding no digit, and an answer code cannot read is asked once more and then kept as unreadable with its cause** Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file, and a label's reason is prose: a reason that sales beat by some percent carries a figure no facts file holds. So the instruction asks for a reason with no figure, date or number, and code refuses a reason holding any digit, as it refuses a kind outside the nine, a direction outside the three, a reason that is not one sentence, an answer that is not JSON, one cut off at its budget and one the provider refused. An answer failing a check is asked for once more under a round of its own; failing twice, the article is stored as unreadable with the second answer's cause, so it is not paid for a third time under that profile and the instruction's version, and the run counts the causes. The instruction is a versioned constant, so a change to its words is a new version under which every article is asked again. The share refused for a digit is read after the labeller's first twenty nights, and above one in ten the alternative, a number allowed only where the article's own stored text carries it, goes back to the operator. Ruled on 2026-09-29 with correction 2 of the 12.6 ruling.

## Data

**The bar store holds one year for every night's work, and the history pulled beside it is read by measurements alone** The operator ruled on 2026-09-28, asked where the eight years pulled on 2026-09-26 should live, that they stay in the operator's store in the two tables of their own the pull wrote, rather than moving to a research copy. The bar store keeps its year for the reasons it always did: every level in the report comes from a sixty-session window, so a longer history was never needed to produce one, and a year covers the 200-day average and gives the level window headroom. The fetcher drops the bar store's oldest session every night, so the year rolls forward, and the gap check reads it against the exchange closure table, which covers the store's own years. The pulled history, back to 2018 for every name the index held since, is what the trade gate's six pairs were measured over, and what a later measurement of the filter reads. Nothing a night runs reads it: no stage, listing, gate, forward return, candidate's record or page, so no count or outcome over those years can reach the list or the edge clock, and a measurement over them states what it read and is shape evidence alone. The night's deleter drops the bar store's oldest session and never a pulled row; the pull's own verb removes a pull whole, and only on the operator's command. What it costs is the file's size, about half a gigabyte with the pulled rows where the year alone is about ten megabytes, which is still one file copied and backed up by hand. This supersedes the ruling that a year of bars is all the store holds, whose reasoning stands for the bar store and whose words no longer describe what the operator's store holds.

**The history pulled before the store's year sits apart from its bars, marked by the pull that wrote it, read by no night and removed whole by that pull** Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-26, asking for a larger sample to measure the swing filter's trade gate over than the store's year gives: a session is readable only once two hundred sessions stand before it, so the year replays about fifty sessions. The operator asked for the older history on the store itself rather than on copies of it, marked so it can be removed later. It cannot go into `bar`, for three reasons, each a failure rather than a preference. The night's gap check reads each name's whole stored series against the exchange closure table, which covers a stated range and refuses a weekday outside it, so a series reaching years before the table's start is one the table cannot place and every name would go unchecked; extending the table back moves the swing filter's code pin and stops the next night. The fetcher drops the bar store's oldest session every night, so older rows would be dropped by the next night. And removing a pull is a delete no stored bar may take (see: Bars are never interpolated). So a pull asks every name the index held on any session from a date the operator names to tonight, the members that have since left among them, for its daily bars over the whole span in the form the bar store holds, one request a name, and the earnings calendar once for each calendar month of the span, and stores what comes back in `pulled_bar` and `pulled_earnings`, each row carrying the run id of the pull that wrote it. The span reaches tonight rather than stopping where the bar store begins, because that store's first session moves forward every night and a pull stopping there would leave a hole between the two within a week. No night, listing, score or page reads either table, so a purge naming a pull removes its rows whole and moves nothing any of them read, and a second pull adds only the sessions no earlier pull holds, so each row belongs to one pull. A session one name misses against the others is stored as the provider sent it and named on the run log, because nothing computes from these tables on their own and the reader of the series decides what a hole stops. A session is a day at least half the names whose series span it hold, since the exchange's closure table covers the store's own years alone and the names that traded a day are what place it; a day fewer hold is named apart and is no one's missing session. Until the 12.2 correction of 2026-09-26 a missing session was read against every date any pulled name held, and the first pull named 621 names missing a session where 10 miss one: JWN's bars on 2024-11-28, 2025-01-01 and 2025-01-09, three days the exchange was closed, read as three sessions every other name spanning them missed. What this entry rules is unchanged, so it is corrected in place. This leaves the bar store's year standing (see: The bar store holds one year for every night's work, and the history pulled beside it is read by measurements alone): no level, reading or night reads further back than the store's year. The cost is one weighted call a name and one a calendar month, taken only on the operator's command and never by a night.

**The surprises pulled before the store's year sit beside the pulled prints and are read by no night** Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-30, choosing to pull the earnings calendar's surprises back to 2018 for the sweep's earnings beat condition rather than read the stored estimates, which start at report dates in 2023-08 for today's members alone. The pull, `history-pull --surprises --from <date>`, asks the earnings calendar once a calendar month of the span, as the bars' pull asks it, about a hundred requests at a weight of one from 2018, and stores each print of a name the index held over the span in `pulled_surprise` with the report date and its timing, the earnings figures as filed, kept as text since nothing computes with them, and the provider's surprise in per cent, a statistic, null where the print carries no estimate, no actual or no surprise; each row carries the pull's run id and is removed whole with it, and a second pull adds only the prints no earlier pull holds. No night, listing, score or page reads the table, for the reason none reads the pulled bars.
