The block is the corpus's unit of a record (see: A candidate is judged by a sign-flip test over blocks of 63 sessions, with at least eight blocks), about a quarter, where a year would have held a declined idea off the page for up to twelve months after the evidence turned.

## The list

**Tonight's list is built from stated conditions, not a score** The length of the list is itself the reading, and a ranked cutoff is the same length every night so it cannot tell you whether anything happened. A condition is also a sentence you can check against the chart, where a score is a formula you would keep tuning.

**The page shows twenty and states the true count** A hundred-name night says a hundred.

**Condition thresholds are calibrated from your own nights, not from a backfill** The run page records what fired each night, so the distribution that sets the thresholds is the one you actually live with. That names no checkpoint and cannot: nights accumulate while the system runs and no checkpoint accumulates them, so the calibration is an operating obligation with a numeric trigger rather than a deferral to a phase (owes: The six reason thresholds calibrated from the nights they fired on).

**Every forward-return figure is shown against the universe base rate** Most names are higher after a month regardless, so a bare hit rate reports market drift as a finding.

**The base rate is over every name-night, and never over the listed ones** The population is every `listing` row for the window, being one per index member per night whether or not a reason fired, and not the rows where one did. `.claude/rules/writing-tests.md` states the same rule about this tool's own verification, and it is the same arithmetic either way: a figure computed over listed names only is a figure over the wrong population, because a listings row exists for every name. Comparing a reason's hit rate against the rate among names that fired would compare a signal against itself and report zero for a reason that works. The window is the horizon the figure is stated at, so a five-session return is shown against the five-session base rate and a twenty-one-session return against the twenty-one, and the population, the filter and the source are stated with the figure rather than beside it.

**The `setup` horizon has no universe base rate, and the column is null for it** Target before stop is a question about a plan, and a name with no plan that night has no answer to it, so there is no universe-wide figure of the same kind to pin beside it. The column is null for that horizon, as `return_pct` already is, rather than being computed over the name-nights that happened to carry a plan: that would be a base rate over a different population from the one the row is about, which is the thing this pair of decisions exists to refuse. What a setup is judged against is the break-even its own plan demanded (see: A condition is judged against the break-even its own plan demands).

**`base_rate` is stored per row on purpose, and the reason is that the row is what gets read** It is a property of the night and the horizon and it sits on a table whose grain is one row per listing per horizon, so one night's figure is repeated across the index. That is deliberate rather than a grain error. The row is what the run page reads and what a later session scores, and a figure that has to be joined for is a figure that can be shown without it; storing it beside the return is what makes "no forward-return figure without its base rate" a property of the store rather than a habit of the page. The cost is one REAL per row against a table already keeping every listing forever.

**Sessions to a dated event are counted on the exchange calendar and never on stored bars** A dated event is ahead of the night and the store holds no bar for a session that has not happened, so a count of stored bars after the night is 0 for every future event. The shortlist builder counted that way from 5.4 until its correction, and earnings soon fired for every name with any date on file however far off: 399, 477, 474 and 476 rows over the four whole-index nights of 2026-09-09 to 2026-09-14, all at a count of 0, where 30, 31, 28 and 28 names had a print on file within twenty sessions. The universe screen had counted the same fact on the calendar since 5.8, and the count now lives in one place both read. It is the sessions strictly after the night up to and including the event's own day where that day is a session, so 0 is tonight's print, and it still fires: a print filed after the close moves the next session, and tonight's list is the last surface to show the name before it does. A print filed before the open and dated tonight has its move in tonight's close already, which the calendar's `timing` column tells apart and this reason does not read. Where the lower edge sits is a condition threshold, so it is set from the operator's own nights by the calibration of the six reasons, which reads the share of earnings soon's fires at a count of 0 split by timing, and an edge it moves moves the ladder's second book and the name page's next dated event with it (owes: The six reason thresholds calibrated from the nights they fired on). An event past the last date the exchange closure table covers has no count: the reason does not fire, its values say beyond the exchange calendar, and the listings stage names such names on its run log with the table's end. That can hide a print inside the horizon only within about twenty-eight days of the table's end, and the night's closing line names that end on every session within ninety days of it. A member the night evaluates over nothing is not counted either (see: A member the night evaluates over nothing keeps the dated event the calendar holds and says no count was made). Rows written before the correction are kept as written, since a listing records what that night listed. What tells the two apart is the row's shape, a version marker rather than a stamp because the listing has no version column: earnings soon's values carry `next dated event` from the correction on, and breakout on volume's carry `previous close`. The run page's record counts each of those two reasons only off rows carrying its marker, and states the nights each record stands on counted the same way. Over the seven sessions written before the correction, 2,522 rows as the store held them after the night of 2026-09-16, that removes 2,306 fired from earnings soon's record, 46 won, 142 lost and 97 never entered among them, and nothing from breakout on volume's, which fired on none of them. Every surface that draws such a session says so beside it: the tonight, run, universe and name routes, and the report exported from a name page. Not taken: re-running the nights, because the shortlist builder writes the newest night each name holds and a replay of an older session would rewrite the newest rows rather than the replayed ones. Until the 5.4 correction of 2026-09-16 this entry named the tonight, run and universe routes alone, gave its removal as 99 wins and 23 losses, over the six sessions held before the night of 2026-09-15, and said a count of 0 fires without saying why. What it rules is unchanged, being the count's basis, the rows kept as written, the marker and a line wherever those rows are drawn, so it is corrected in place rather than superseded.

**Breakout on volume reads resistance at the previous session's close** A band is resistance to a breakout where its low edge was at or above the previous session's close, and tonight's close above that band's high edge, on volume above the fifty-day average, fires. The role the level builder stores is set against tonight's close, so every band it calls resistance sits at or above tonight's close, and a reason asking whether the close is above one could never be true: from 5.4 until its correction it fired on none of the 2,522 rows the operator's store holds from those nights, and the suite's recompute read the same role and agreed. The bands are tonight's and the side is last night's, which is how crossed a level already reads a band, and clearing the high edge rather than crossing the low one is what separates a breakout from a close that entered a band. Not taken: last night's stored band set with its stored roles, because a band set rebuilt tonight is the one the plan and the other reasons read. Until the 5.4 correction of 2026-09-16 this entry gave 2,018 rows, the six sessions held before the night of 2026-09-15; the rule is unchanged, so it is corrected in place.

**A member the night evaluates over nothing keeps the dated event the calendar holds and says no count was made** A member with no bar for the session, a gap in its stored series or no bar stored at all is evaluated over nothing and fires no reason, which keeps a figure computed across a hole, or off a session the member did not trade, off tonight's list. Five of the six reasons read a bar or a band such a member lacks, and what their values state stands as the 6.0 ruling on a stale member's values left it. Earnings soon reads the calendar and the night, and the calendar holds the member's date whether or not it traded, so its values carry the date the calendar holds on or after the row's session, and in place of the count, not counted with the reason: no bar for the session with the last session stored, the gap's date, or no bar stored. The path handed earnings soon no date at all until this was decided, and a row said not on file for a member whose calendar held a print, which a later reader of the stored values would count as a missing date: a member whose last stored bar is 2026-08-17 stored that on each of the five nights from 2026-09-10 to 2026-09-16, and on the last of them the calendar stage had stored its print for 2026-10-21 before the listings stage ran. Not taken: counting such a member's sessions to its date, because a stale or gapped member with a print inside the horizon would then reach tonight's list with an empty plan and nothing saying why, which is what evaluating it over nothing exists to refuse; and restating all six reasons as not evaluated, because the five that read a bar are already stated truly and a second vocabulary for them would split the stored rows into two shapes with no reader that needs it.

**The swing filter's starting settings are ruled from shape counts before tonight's list switches to it** Phase 12's filter was proposed with every threshold set before any night was measured, and a planning estimate over the eleven stored nights from 2026-09-09 to 2026-09-23 listed no name on nine of them and one on each of the other two, with the market gate at a breadth of 50 percent closed on the last. The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 that the filter is built and every gate result stored for every member while tonight's list stays as it was, and that the code's own counts come back first: each night in gate order with what each step removed, each gate relaxed alone with every other held, the trade gate read from the ladder's first tranche and from a swing trade's own stop and target, and the same counts over the stored year wherever the bars, the stored swings, the indicators and the membership give the readings as of each session, the market gate read at 45 and at 50 percent. The operator rules from those counts the starting settings, which reading the trade gate takes and the five variants, and the list switches after the family registers. The counts read no outcome: a count of names is what the shape clock moves, and nothing in them says whether a name went on to rise. The year's counts inform the settings the list starts on and nothing else; the calibration the shape clock runs at its trigger reads the gate results the filter stored night by night (see: Condition thresholds are calibrated from your own nights, not from a backfill).

**The swing filter's trade gate reads section 10's plan for the swing trade, and the plan at the nearest bands is the variant in the reward to risk variant's place** The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, reading six stop and target pairs measured over the pulled history, that the live trade gate reads section 10's rule applied to the swing trade: entered at the night's close, stopped on a close below the setup band's low edge, or below the low edge of the next support band beneath it where the setup band's is less than one typical day's move below the entry, and won at the lowest low edge of a band at least two typical days' moves above the entry. Section 10 places the report's plan by the same two facts, that a band closer than two typical days' moves is inside the noise and so is a stop closer than one, so the list and the report no longer disagree about where one stock is wrong. The plan it replaces, stopped at the setup band's low edge and won at the nearest band above the close, put its stops a median 0.69 typical moves below the entry over the 1,996 sessions from 2018-10-16 to 2026-09-25 and nine in ten under 0.94, where section 10's plan puts them at 1.18 and never inside a band, and it lists a name on 78.3% of the 1,628 sessions the market gate opened against section 10's 82.8%. With no evidence on either side of whether either makes money, the live rule is the one the architecture already reasons for, and the departure from it is what a variant tests: the plan at the nearest bands takes the place of the variant at a reward to risk of 2, which asked a weaker form of the same question. The step to the band beneath is taken once, as the pair was measured and ruled, so a stop can still sit under a typical move where the band beneath is close as well, and the gate's distance floor decides those. The family is the live filter at version 3's settings and five variants, each a whole rule with every threshold stated and one thing moved: the plan at the nearest bands, a pullback 1 to 3 typical moves deep, the market gate off, strength in the top third and one-session arrival, six tested at 0.05 over 6 (see: A variant of the swing filter is registered as a whole rule and runs on unchanged when the live settings move). Each variant is registered anew against version 3 under a name saying so, because each now reads section 10's plan and the 45% market floor, which makes each a rule of its own, and a name stands for one rule. A breakout's setup band is the band it cleared, and its plan is read the same way. Every setting but the plan stands as version 2 holds it (see: Version 2 of the swing filter lowers the market floor to 45% before the list's first live night, and nothing else moves), and the switch is taken as a rule correction (see: A rule correction taken before the family's first scored night opens a filter version and registers the family again at one instant, and is no shape acceptance).

**Version 2 of the swing filter lowers the market floor to 45% before the list's first live night, and nothing else moves** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, after version 1 listed no name on the evening of 2026-09-24, that the market gate's floor is 45%, every other setting version 1's. Evidence: version 1 replayed over the 53 stored sessions from 2026-07-13 to 2026-09-24, reading the store and writing nothing, listed a median of 3 names a night, at most 8 and none on 5 sessions at 45%, and none on 7 at 50%. The two floors differ only on 2026-09-23 and 2026-09-24, breadth 49.8% and 47.6%, the only sessions of the 53 on which it fell below 50%, where at 45% the replay lists four names and three; every name the replay listed was a pullback. The gate still stands aside in a market clearly below its long average and no longer on one hovering at the line (see: The swing filter opens loose on the swing trade's own plan, and each of its five variants moves one setting to its other side). Version 2 opened at 2026-09-25T14:04:07Z through the shape command, before the family's first night, so it restarted no block: the live candidate registered for version 1 was retired and version 2's registered at that instant, and ten candidate conditions have now ever been registered. The five variants keep the 50% floor they were registered with and run on unchanged, so on a night breadth sits from 45% to 50% the live filter can list where the four variants that hold a market gate cannot, and the run page's edge half names the floor as a setting the live filter has moved since (see: A variant of the swing filter is registered as a whole rule and runs on unchanged when the live settings move). This was the acceptance version 1 had left while the list is live, so every further one, the shape proposal at sixty ordinary nights included, states the blocks it restarts (see: A shape acceptance restarts the live filter's edge clock, and after one acceptance while the list is live each further one states the blocks it restarts). Section 17's 50% stands as the proposed value the code carries.

**Arrival is a trigger that first fired within the last three sessions, and the trade is read from tonight's close** A pullback's trigger is a close above the previous session's high, or a close back inside or above the band after a session below its low edge; a breakout's is the first close above the band it cleared. The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 that arrival is the trigger not having fired the night before, and on 2026-09-25, from the counts, that it counts inside a window of three sessions: the trigger passes where its event happened tonight or on either of the two sessions before, having not happened on the session before that one, each read off that session's stored gate result, so a name whose trigger first fired yesterday lists tonight as well. The counts showed arrival as the gate that binds: at the loosest settings measured, the list's median over the stored year was one name a night with the one-session window, three with the window of three, and seven with arrival set aside. The window admits no stale entry, because the trade gate reads the swing trade's own plan from tonight's close, so a name whose trigger fired two sessions ago and has since run from its band fails reward to risk on tonight's price. A session in the window with no stored result, where the answer turns on it, cannot say, and the gate fails and names it rather than passing on the absence. The window is a setting, three proposed, and the one-session window is one of the family's variants, so the loop measures whether same-day arrival does better rather than assuming it. It was settled before the family registers, because after that a change to the trigger's code moves every candidate's pin and restarts about two years of each one's clock. For a breakout the rule is the one it already had, and the pullback's own window, a high made within the last twenty sessions, still bounds how long a name can sit in its zone.

**The swing filter reads pullbacks alone, and a breakout returns only as a registered candidate built from its measured record** The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, reading the breakouts the gates read over the pulled history's 1,996 sessions from 2018-10-16 to 2026-09-25 under filter version 2's settings: 81 breakout setups in all, 78 named as breakouts, the trend and strength gate removing 62 of them, and the 12 that reached the trade gate listing none on the nearest bands' plan or on section 10's, 6 with no band above the close and 6 with none two typical moves away. "About five names a year, on about three nights a year, is too few for the edge clock ever to judge. Every path in the filter has to feed the loop, and a path the loop can never measure is code to maintain with no way of learning whether it works. The fix would also bring in a measured-move target, which is a new rule section 10 does not have." So the setup gate reads a pullback into an anchored support band and nothing else, the trigger is the pullback's, the plans are read off the pullback's band, and the base's tightness and the breakout's volume are no settings of the filter's; a version opened before it names both and they are passed over, the tightness is still stored with the swing readings as the reading the name page draws, and a night stored before it keeps the breakouts it named. The measurement and the fix it would take are recorded whole in PROGRESS, the base read to the session before, a close through a band's top counted and a measured-move target, 41 names over eight years at a median reward to risk of 2.2, so a breakout comes back, if it does, as a registered candidate built from that record and scored like any other, and is not rediscovered. The reason breakout on volume is untouched: it is still computed for every member every night and drawn beside each row as context (see: Breakout on volume reads resistance at the previous session's close). This narrows three entries rather than superseding them, since everything else each says still describes code that runs: the shape proposer no longer holds the base's tightness or the breakout's volume among the settings it does not move (see: The shape proposer moves one setting a gate, nearest first, and never applies what it proposes); no setup band is a band a breakout cleared, so no plan is read off one (see: The swing filter's trade gate reads section 10's plan for the swing trade, and the plan at the nearest bands is the variant in the reward to risk variant's place); and the trigger has no breakout's first close above its band (see: Arrival is a trigger that first fired within the last three sessions, and the trade is read from tonight's close). Narrowed in turn on 2026-10-01, by the operator's ruling of the setup families: the filter still reads pullbacks alone, and a breakout of another shape, a close above the year's high and not a base broken through a band, is a family of its own beside the filter, on provisional settings until its sweep and registered at its freeze, so it is still scored like any other before it counts (see: A breakout is a close above the year's high on heavy volume after its ranges narrowed, sold on a trailing stop with no target).

**The swing filter's results are replayed for the sessions before its first stored night for the trigger's arrival alone, and removed once no night can read them** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, after version 2 opened at a 45% market floor with 2026-09-24 the only night the filter had stored. A pullback's trigger arrives where it first fired within the arrival window, read off the stored results of the sessions before the night, and the sessions before the filter's first stored night held none, so every pullback on the list's first nights failed its trigger for want of them. The `filter-history` verb replays the filter for sessions before its first stored night, from the bars, bands and plans the store holds as of each session and under the open version's settings, through the replay the filter counts run, and stores each member's result under the version `replayed`, which is no filter version. The trigger's arrival reads a replayed result as it reads a night's, and nothing else reads one: no clock counts a replayed session, the shape clock keying its window on the open version and the edge clock and the near misses on theirs, no page draws one, Past picks reading only a session the filter listed, and the filler scores no replayed plan. A session that already holds results and the night's own session are refused whole, and the night that draws a session replaces whatever the session held with its own. Rejected: writing a trial copy's seeded results into the store, which no declared writer wrote; and running the older nights again, which the night refuses because its corporate action refetch would cut a refetched name's later bars (see: Arrival is a trigger that first fired within the last three sessions, and the trade is read from tonight's close). The operator ruled on 2026-09-28 that the replayed results come out once no night reads them: "Nights the list never showed must not appear on Past picks or count in any shape window." The same verb with `--remove` takes out the replayed results of the sessions it names and nothing else, a night's own results on those sessions staying, and writes one run log row by hand naming each session it removed with its count of results and the names among them that passed. A session a night can still read is refused whole, and so is a range holding no replayed result, and a refusal writes nothing. The trigger's arrival reads each member's sessions before the night as far back as the open version's window or a standing candidate's reaches, three on version 2, and the night after the newest session the store holds reads that many of each member's newest sessions while the newest night run again reads that many behind its own, so every session among each member's newest bars that far back and one more is kept until a later night is stored. Removing them does not change what any page or clock read before, since none read them; it makes that hold by construction rather than by every reader remembering to pass them by, and the replayed results were only ever an answer to the list's first nights, which have now passed.

Supersedes **The swing filter's results are replayed for the sessions before its first stored night, for the trigger's arrival alone**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that the replayed results stay in the store.

**Tonight's list is the swing filter's with improving businesses drawn first, and an evening is listed and ordered by the rule that listed it** The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 that tonight's list becomes a swing filter, on 2026-09-25 opened its first version on the swing trade's own plan, and on 2026-09-27 ruled that the names it passes are drawn improving businesses first. From the first night whose filter step records its session, tonight's list is the names the swing filter passes: every gate passed, the trigger arriving inside its window and no exclusion applying. They are drawn by the state the night's readings of their reported quarters give them, improving first, then steady, then the members reading no state, being not enough quarters and no fundamentals yet, then deteriorating (see: Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night by rules the measured split settled, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone), and within a state by the trade's reward to risk, higher first, then relative strength, then the setup band's strength, then the ticker, each a fact about the chart, the plan or the company's reported quarters and none an opinion about the company. The state orders the names and removes none, since no gate reads it, and a member reading no state is drawn third rather than left off. The order is applied where the list is read, by one rule over the night's stored readings that every reader shares, and the swing filter's own rank stays the trade's order, so no pin moves. A night that stored no readings, which is every night before the first that did, is drawn in the filter's own order wherever it is drawn, tonight's page, the overnight queue, the night's own request and Past picks alike, so an evening keeps the order it was drawn in as it keeps the rule that listed it and a later rule reorders no earlier night. Each row carries its gates with the values that decided them and its state beside its trend, and the six reasons are still computed and stored for every member every night and stand beside each row as context. A night the market gate closed draws no row and one line with the breadth and the gate's floor, and a night no name passed draws one line with how many reached each gate, so an empty list says why it is empty. The night records the rule for its session in the swing filter's step, once the filter has stored its rows and before any later step can stop the night, and only where the filter stored rows; the night close is its only writer. An evening with no such record was listed by any of the six reasons firing, which is every evening before the switch, and it is drawn as it was listed, in the order it was drawn in (see: Tonight's list breaks a tie in fired count by the plan's reward to risk, and a row with none is drawn after every row with one and says why). A night run again over a session draws that session again and records its rule, so a session is listed by the rule of the night that drew it last. Every surface that says whether a name was on the list reads each evening by its own rule and names it: tonight's list and its header, a name's page, its listing history and its walk, the universe's last evening and strip, the run page's overlap, the facts retention, the overnight queue, which takes the list's names first in the order it is drawn in, and the night's own request, which asks for the first name drawn on the list and for none on a night none passed. This narrows three entries rather than superseding them, since each still describes code that runs: the list is still built from stated conditions and not a score, the five gates being the conditions and the state an order among the names they pass (see: Tonight's list is built from stated conditions, not a score); the page still draws twenty and states the true count, the count the filter listed (see: The page shows twenty and states the true count); and the fired-count order still draws every evening the reasons listed. The ruled filter passed a median of three names a night over the stored year, so the list will rarely approach twenty, and that is its selectivity rather than a fault. From the operator's ruling of 2026-10-01 the page's list is drawn from setup families, and this entry is narrowed rather than superseded, since it still describes code that runs: the names the swing filter passes, in this order, are the pullback family's, and every evening the filter listed before the families is still drawn as this entry rules (see: Tonight's page is drawn from setup families, each a rule of its own listing at most five a night).

Supersedes **Tonight's list is the swing filter's, and an evening is listed by the rule that listed it**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the order the names passing are drawn in.

**A member that missed exactly one gate and no exclusion is drawn close to a buy point nearest first, and recommends nothing** The operator asked on 2026-09-26 for a second list on tonight's page and on 2026-09-29 gave its specification, approved as written. On an evening the swing filter listed, "Close to a buy point" draws beneath the list every member whose stored result failed exactly one of the five gates, market, trend and strength, setup, trigger and trade, and carries no exclusion. Two failed gates keep a member off, and so does any exclusion, an earnings date inside the holding window, a series in doubt or a missing day being a reason not to trade rather than a check narrowly failed. It is ordered nearest to qualifying first by one distance for every gate, how far the deciding value sits from its bar as a share of the bar: a strength, a breadth, a depth, a dry-up, a reward to risk or a stop outside its bounds reads as its shortfall over the bar; a trigger that first fired on the Nth session back, tonight the first as the arrival window counts, against a window of W reads (N - W) / W, so a firing one session outside a window of three reads a third; a condition not met at all counts as one whole bar, being no firing among the stored results, no band to set a target or a stop at, or a close outside an anchored support band; a trend that is not an uptrend adds half a bar for a range and a whole bar for a downtrend; shortfalls within one gate add; and a tie is drawn in the list's own order, the state the member's reported quarters give it, then the trade's reward to risk, then strength, then band strength, then the ticker. Each bar is the setting of the filter version the member's result was stored under, and a missed trigger's first firing is read off the member's own stored results on the sessions before the night, the replayed results among them, since those are what the trigger's arrival reads. Each row carries what a row of the list carries, with the gate it missed in place of the gates, what it had against the bar it needed in plain words, how far short that is, and the trade its plan states. The two lists share the twenty rows the page draws, the list's first, each stating its true count. On a night the market gate closed the list is empty by design, and this one draws the members short of the market alone beneath one line stated once that they would qualify if the market turned, with the breadth and its bar. It is drawn for every evening the swing filter listed, the first being 2026-09-24. It computes nothing a gate did not and stores nothing, so the edge clock and Past picks, which read the stored rows of the names the filter passed, never count a row of it, and its key says so; a name on it opens to its page, whose Why it is here names the gate it missed and by how much; and the overnight queue drafts the list's names first, then these in the order they are drawn, then every other member. The rule and the distance live in one place that the page and the queue both read, so the two cannot order the names differently. A shortfall as a share of its bar needs no unit and no scale set by hand, since every bar here is a quantity with a natural zero, so a tenth short reads the same on a strength, a reward to risk or a count of sessions; a count of the members lying between a value and its bar was rejected, because it moves a stock's distance when other stocks move. This narrows one entry rather than superseding it, since the page still draws twenty and states each list's true count (see: The page shows twenty and states the true count). It is named "Close to a buy point" because the watch list already names the operator's own stocks.

**A stock holds one open trade on each rule's list, and it is free the night after its trade ends** The operator ruled on 2026-09-30, after the live list listed BDX again on 2026-09-29, two nights after listing it, with the trade of 2026-09-25 still open. A trade is the one listed: bought at that night's close with that night's stop and target, and open until a close reaches its target, a close falls through its stop or its sessions run out. A stock with an open trade is not listed again, not even for a different setup, and is eligible again from the night after the trade ends, a stop's night included, because a stop reached at the close frees nothing before the next session. Each rule keeps its own open trades: the live list and every candidate walk theirs apart, so one rule's trades never block another's and a candidate is judged on the listings it alone would have made. A stock whose live trade is open appears in neither the list nor "Close to a buy point", and frees its place for another stock. The trigger stays fresh for several sessions, so one setup was listed on consecutive nights and each listing was scored as its own trade, one move counted two or three times in the live record and in the sweep's history, and a record that counts one move several times overstates both its trades and its evidence. A trade whose outcome row is missing is read as open until its cap's sessions have passed, since a row that is not there says nothing either way. The rule reaches the pages first, with no pin moving, and joins the filter's code at the freeze, sharing its remedy; every trade before the freeze is outside every checkpoint, so a repeat before it is never judged (see: Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and a candidate's picks are shown on the Run page while its outcomes wait for a look). From the operator's ruling of 2026-10-01 the page's list holds one trade a stock across every setup family, which the family lister applies as it draws the list (see: A stock holds one trade across every family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order); this entry is narrowed by that and not superseded, since each registered rule still walks its own open trades for its own record, as it rules.

**Tonight's page is drawn from setup families, each a rule of its own listing at most five a night** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: "setup families, built and shown first, each swept and frozen after", because they want each family's picks on screen every night and a rule needs a sweep to be frozen, not to exist. A setup family is one kind of buy point with a rule of its own: it reads every member every night, passes the names its gates pass, orders them in its own order and keeps its own picks, variants, record and checkpoints. The families share the night's machinery, the market check, the open trade rule and the page, and nothing else: no family reads another's gates. The pullback is the first family and is the swing filter as it stands, its names the ones the filter passes, improving businesses first, on the plan its trade gate reads. After the swing filter has stored its rows the family lister draws the page's list from what each family passed, the families in the page's order, and stores it, recording the session as one the families drew whether or not any stock is listed, so every reader of tonight's list, the page, the overnight queue, the night's requests, the news labeller, the night's count and the facts retention, reads one stored list; a session the lister did not draw is read as it was before, by the swing filter's passing names on a night it listed and by the reasons before that, so no earlier evening is redrawn. Each family lists at most five a night, the first in its own order, so a card is the night's best of one kind of setup and not a count of how busy the night was; a name past the five is held back and named under the card. The five is the operator's and a constant section 17 pins. The swing filter's own code is not edited for any of this, so no registered rule's version moves: the list is drawn from the rows the filter already stores.

**A stock holds one trade across every swing family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order** The families the operator's ruling of 2026-10-01 named were the three swing families, and the sector heavyweights, a rotation held for months that phase 14 added, stand outside it. The brief of 2026-10-03 brought the operator the fork between one trade a stock across all four cards, option A, and each card keeping its own, option B, measured over 2019-01-02 to 2026-10-02 on the pulled data: under A a held heavyweight would have hidden 113 of the 4,759 swing picks, whose results were better than the rest's, and held back 83 of 479 heavyweight entries; under B 66 stocks stood on two cards at once on 1,235 of the 1,949 sessions, at most 6 on one. 14.3 built option B, the recommendation, while the operator's ruling on the fork is awaited, and a ruling for A writes the decision superseding this one. So a stock the heavyweights hold is listed by a swing family as any other, a stock a swing family holds a trade on is bought by the heavyweights as any other, and neither card holds back what the other holds. Among the swing families the ruling stands as given. Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: "One stock, one trade, across all families: a stock with an open trade from any family is listed by none. A stock qualifying under two families on one night is listed once, under the first in the page's order, carrying both labels, and counted once." A trade is the one the page listed: bought at that night's close on the listing family's plan, and open until that plan's exit or its sessions run out, read by the rule that already reads an open trade, a stop's night included and a missing outcome read as open until its cap's sessions have passed (see: A stock holds one open trade on each rule's list, and it is free the night after its trade ends). The lister reads every trade a list made on an earlier night, the pullback's from the nights the swing filter listed before the families among them, and lists no stock whose trade is still open, storing for each family that passed it a row naming the family and the night that listed the open trade, which that family's card says in a note. A stock two families pass on one night is listed under the first of them in the page's order that has a place left, its row carrying the other's label; the later family stores it as listed under another and gives it none of its own five places, so the night's count of buy points counts it once. A stock past the five of an earlier family is not listed by that family and may be listed by a later one it qualified under, since it is on no card. What this rules is the page's list. A family's own record, the one its checkpoints read, counts every trade its own rule would have made, the ones the page gave to another family among them, each rule walking its own open trades: a record that depended on what another family listed would move every family's record whenever one family's rule changed.

Supersedes **A stock holds one trade across every family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands for the swing families, the sector heavyweights keeping their own book on option B while the fork's ruling is awaited.

**A family lists on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: each new family goes live with provisional settings taken from the published evidence, "marked as not yet frozen", and its sweep over the stored history then proposes the starting point that replaces them at its freeze, on the operator's go. Until a registration stands for a family its card says, in the ruling's words, "provisional: not yet frozen; its record starts at the freeze", its picks are listed, scored and recorded like any other, and no trade of it counts toward any checkpoint, since a rule nobody registered was never pre-registered and its settings were chosen before any sweep. A family whose live rule stands registered draws the day that registration was made and the variants registered beside it, read off the register as it stood at the night's end. The freeze registers the family's rule and its variants at one instant, as the swing family's registration does, and a family whose sweep finds no setting meeting its floors keeps listing on its provisional settings, its card saying so in one line, until the operator rules on it (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word).

Supersedes **A family runs on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that a family whose sweep finds nothing steady is set aside.

**The market check closes every swing family's list together, and the sector heavyweights read none** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01 for the swing families and on 2026-10-03 for the sector heavyweights: "No market check for the heavyweights." The night's breadth, the share of the members closing above their own 200-day average, is read against one floor, the open filter version's, and a night it falls below that floor lists no stock under any swing family: every swing family's card is drawn and says the check closed every list, with the breadth and the floor. One check for every swing family rather than one a family, because the evidence it rests on is about the market a trade is opened in and not about a kind of setup, and a page on which one setup is open and another shut on the same breadth would be read as a view on setups the night does not hold. The sector heavyweights read no market check: a rotation held for months among the largest companies already reads its own trend gate on each stock and sells at a close under the stock's own 200-day average, so a breadth check on the night of a rebalance would decide a month's holding on one evening's breadth, and its card is drawn whatever the check read, holding what it holds.

**Each index's market check closes its own swing lists together on its own breadth** Ruled with phase 15's plan, approved by the operator on 2026-10-05: each index runs every family as rules of its own, and "each index's own breadth at the 500's floor" is one of the provisional rules' settings. The S&P 400's and 600's swing lists each close together on a night their own index's breadth, the share of its members holding a close and a 200-day average whose close stands above it, is under the floor the S&P 500's filter reads, and open on every other; neither index reads the S&P 500's breadth, nor the S&P 500 theirs, so a night the large companies fall and the small ones do not closes the S&P 500's lists alone. The sector heavyweights read no market check on any index. It narrows, without superseding, the S&P 500's own check, which stands for the S&P 500's lists (see: The market check closes every swing family's list together, and the sector heavyweights read none); each index's sweep reads the S&P 500's breadth in place of the index's own as one of its switches.

Supersedes **The market check closes every family's list together**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands for the swing families, the sector heavyweights joining the page after it was ruled and reading none.

**The new families freeze at their sweeps' proposals, the breakout's provisional setting and the drift's wider stop registered beside them as variants** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-02 after the three sweeps' reports. The breakouts freeze at a close above the highest high of the 126 sessions before it, on volume 1.5 times its fifty-session average, after the newer ranges narrowed to 0.85 of the older, with the stop and the trail 1.5 typical moves beneath: over the scored history 1,009 trades at an edge of 0.082, against the provisional setting's 1,658 at 0.041. The earnings drift freezes at a reaction bought within 3 sessions, up half a typical move on twice its volume's average, aimed at the nearer of the band and 2.5 times the risk: 2,243 trades at 0.122 and above nothing in 6 of the 8 years, against the provisional setting's 0.234 above nothing in 4. Neither proposal is an isolated point, checked on the operator's correction before either froze: two of the breakout's five one-step neighbours meet the floors, ranges no wider at an edge of 0.060 and volume 2 at 0.064, each above nothing in 6 of 8 years, and two of the drift's five, a rise of one typical move at 0.119 and a target at 3 times the risk at 0.096, each in 6 of 8. The sector leaders freeze as no family: their one setting meeting the floors has no neighbour that does, and they are registered as a variant of the pullback (see: The sector leaders are a variant of the pullback's starting point and not a family of their own). Each family registers its proposal's one-step neighbours as variants, and the operator added two: the breakout's provisional setting, a year's high with ranges no wider and the stop 2 typical moves beneath, and the drift's proposal with its stop no closer than one typical move under the buy, the stop moved down to that distance where the reaction's low sits nearer and the target placed on the wider risk as the rule places it. The second answers the drift's near stops: 40 per cent of the proposal's trades held a stop under one typical move, and a result counted in risks grows as the stop nears the buy. No family passes eight rules, so no neighbour is dropped for them. Neither edge is far from its standard error, so a freeze claims no edge: it fixes each rule so its trades from the freeze count toward its own checkpoints. This narrows the breakout's and the drift's own entries to the numbers frozen here and supersedes neither, each having said its settings were provisional until the freeze.

**A registered family rule is evaluated every night at its own settings and keeps its own list, its trades stored with their benchmark when they end** Ruled with 13.9 on 2026-10-02, the operator choosing that the four freezes land together once the three new families' rules are measured as the pullback's are. A registration fixes a rule before its evidence and the record is what tests it, so a family's rules are measured from the night they register, as the swing family's are. Each new family's rule reads its settings, the live rule at the frozen starting point and each registration at its own parameters, so a variant is the rule itself at other numbers and never a second implementation of it. The family evaluator evaluates every family candidate standing when the night started over every member it evaluates, at that candidate's settings, and stores each verdict with the trade the candidate's plan places on the member's row beside the live rule's answer, a missing or moved evaluator failing the stage as it fails the filter's. Each registered rule keeps its own list as the page keeps the live one, at most five a night in its own order and one open trade a stock, its stock free the night after its trade ends and never held by another rule's trade, so its record counts the trades it alone would have made. Each trade it keeps is stored the night it is made, and once the trade ends its result in multiples of its risk and the benchmark of the same plan entered at the close on every member the index held that night with a bar and no gap, the reading its sweep proposed it on, both written once and never recomputed, because the bars they are read from are kept a year and a record is read over many. The record is the trades' edge, the result less the benchmark, over blocks of 63 sessions from the night the rule registered, read against the test its sweep's report fixed before the freeze.

**The pullback's freeze opens its base as the next filter version and registers the swing family again at one instant** Ruled with 13.9 on 2026-10-02. The pullback's freeze moves a setting and the code every swing family evaluator pins: its base raises the reward-to-risk floor to 2, and the code it lands, the fundamentals candidate, the open trade rule and the new families' evaluators, moves every evaluator's version. A shape acceptance replaces the live candidate alone and leaves its variants running on the code they registered under, and a rule correction opens no setting, so neither fits a freeze. The freeze is a form of the shape command of its own: the open version's settings with the reward-to-risk floor at 2 opened as the next filter version, every standing swing family candidate retired and the eight the code writes for that version registered, the live filter, its five variants, the fundamentals candidate and the sector leaders, all at one instant, stating the non-empty blocks the live candidate has run, which it restarts. It is no acceptance of a proposal or of ruled settings, so the bound on acceptances does not count it, and it is taken once: a second, finding the open version opened by a freeze, is refused with nothing changed.

**The sector leaders are a variant of the pullback's starting point and not a family of their own** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-02, correcting the morning's answer before the freeze merged. The leaders' sweep found one setting of its 9 meeting the floors, the top quarter of the top 3 sectors: 1,021 trades at an edge of 0.070, above nothing in 6 of the 8 scored years, 0.038 without its five largest results. None of its three one-step neighbours meets them: the top 2 sectors read 4 years of 8, the top third of each sector 5, and the top 4 sectors 4 with an edge of 0.016. So the setting is an isolated point and not a plateau, and a family frozen on it would be a family fitted to one corner of a grid. The rule uses the pullback's setup, trigger and trade, with sector leadership in place of the trend and strength gate, so it is a pullback rule with one thing changed. It is registered as "the pullback in the top 3 sectors, top quarter of each", a variant of the pullback's base beside its other variants, read by the swing filter's own evaluator over each member's standing in its sector, ranked by the night's own rule: the sectors by the median of their members' long returns, a member by its own inside its sector. The sector leaders' card leaves Tonight, the family evaluator evaluates them no longer, and a leader the page listed before is still named as one. Its record carries the caveat its sweep stated: 319 of the 827 names in the scored history carry no sector, most of them names the index has let go, so the sweep read mostly the stocks that stayed, which flatters it. This supersedes the sector leader family's own entry, whose reading of the rule it keeps.

**The pullback's ninth rule keeps the night's best three in the list's own order, and the family is registered again whole to add it** The operator ruled on 2026-10-02: "The ideas' starting point: do not freeze it. One idea of 13 passed against a luck figure of 1.7. Register "the night's best three, in the list's own order" as a variant of the pullback, through the remedy script, now while the freeze's records are a day old; state what restarts." The rule is the live filter at the open version's settings whose list keeps, of the members it fires on a night, the first three in the list's own order: reward to risk on the plan its trade gate reads, then strength, then the setup band's strength, each higher first, then the ticker, once its own open trades have kept a stock off. A member past the three is no trade and holds its stock on no later night, as the ideas' run walked its idea c. The count is a parameter of its registration, read where its record is read, because a member's verdict is its own and the night's order belongs to the list. It joins the family by the family registered again whole at one instant under the version already open, so it is first evaluated on the same night as every rule beside it, its level divided across them, and it is defined against the live filter registered at its instant. Nothing measured restarts: no night had scored the freeze's rules, the eight are registered again under their names and settings, each stating that its list keeps every member it fires on, and no filter version opens, so the shape clock's count goes on.

**The pullback's sector leaders' rule is retired and the analysts' revisions variant registered in its place** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04, answering "Yes, both" to retiring the pullback's sector leaders' rule and registering the analysts' revisions variant, and on 2026-10-03: "Make room by retiring the pullback's sector-leaders rule (the isolated point with a survivor-flattered history) rather than raising the cap." The leaders' rule was the one setting of its sweep's nine meeting the floors with no neighbour meeting them, over a history missing a sector for 319 of its 827 names, most of them names the index had let go (see: The sector leaders are a variant of the pullback's starting point and not a family of their own). The revisions variant is the pullback's base, the live filter at the open version's settings, firing only where a member's analysts raised their estimate for its current fiscal year over the thirty days before the night (see: A member's estimates are raised where its current fiscal year's consensus earnings estimate stands above its level 30 days before), in the operator's words of the brief: "Analyst estimate revisions: estimates raised over the last 30 days. The provider holds no history of this, so it is a live variant only, recorded from tonight's snapshots." It has no sweep and no history to sweep, since the provider files the trend as it stands today and none from before, so its record starts at its registration and is read as every variant's is. Revisions stand in for the guidance no rule can read (see: Six context checks are not adopted, each for the reason it states), and stocks whose earnings estimates analysts were raising have gone on to beat those whose estimates they cut (Chan, Jegadeesh and Lakonishok, 1996). The family is registered again whole at one instant under the filter version already open, the leaders' rule retired on the evidence and every other rule registered again unchanged, as a rule joins it (see: The pullback's ninth rule keeps the night's best three in the list's own order, and the family is registered again whole to add it); the leaders' rule keeps its record as a retired rule does, and the family stays at nine. The night asks for each estimate the variant reads as a carve-out of its own (see: The night asks for the estimates of each member a rule reading them passes on everything else, once a member a night).

**The breakout and the earnings drift each register a variant listing only on nights its market switch is open, each family registered again whole and its records replayed** The operator ruled on 2026-10-03, from the three reports of 2026-10-02 and 03: "register two variants through the remedy script, stating what restarts: breakout: only on nights the index closes above its 200-session average (edge 0.082 to 0.100, held without the five largest, cut 2022's loss); earnings drift: only on nights the VIX closes under its close ten sessions before (edge 0.121 to 0.221, and 0.159 without the five largest). Both failed only on yearly totals because they sit out nights, which the live record will weigh." Each variant is its family's live rule at the frozen settings with one market switch and nothing else moved, read as the ideas' run on the frozen families read it: the index's close above the average of its 200 closes ending on the night, and the VIX's close under its close 10 sessions before, each on the store's own sessions and closed on a night it cannot be read. A switch is a setting of the registration, a count of sessions and nought where the rule states none, read after the family's own gates as a gate of its own, so every registration of either family states both, and the fourteen rules the freezes registered are the same rules stating none. The night asks for both series to read it (see: The night asks for the index's and the VIX's daily closes once a series, and keeps them apart from the members' bars). The switch moves both families' evaluators, so each family is registered again whole at one instant, its seven retired and eight registered, the seven under their names and settings and the eighth the switched variant, as the pullback's family was to add its ninth rule. What restarts is read by the replay each family's registration now runs (see: A family rule registered again keeps its record from its first registration where a replay of its stored nights reproduces every trade, and restarts at the change otherwise): 2026-10-02 is the one night the freezes' rules were scored on, the breakout's seven kept no trade that night and two drift variants kept one each, and each rule's record counts from that night where a replay over it reproduces every trade, and from its registration where it does not. Registered on 2026-10-03, before any replay was run, each counted from 2026-10-05 until the next registration replayed it. Each family's rules are tested at 0.05 over eight in place of 0.05 over seven, and no pullback rule, filter version or clock moves. The ideas' test had failed each on more than its totals: the breakout's switch was better in two of the eight years and the same in five, and its 963 trades fell under the 1,000 the test asks, which the family as frozen clears by nine; the drift's switch lowered its plain result a trade from 0.278 to 0.241, its edge rising because the benchmark on the nights it kept fell from 0.157 to 0.020. The live record weighs each as it weighs every variant.

Supersedes **The breakout and the earnings drift each register a variant listing only on nights its market switch is open, and each family is registered again whole to add it**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the restart its rules' records took, which the replay now reads.

**A family rule registered again keeps its record from its first registration where a replay of its stored nights reproduces every trade, and restarts at the change otherwise** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03 with phase 14's plan, choosing that a rule's record be read by its name with a guard over it. A family rule is registered again whenever a change of code moves its family's evaluator or a rule joins its family, and until this a rule registered again counted its record from the new registration, so a change that altered nothing a rule kept still threw its record away. Before registering, `register --moved` and `register --family-again` replay each family rule they register again, at its settings under the code as it then stands, over every night the store holds since its record began, through the night's own inputs read as of each night, the night's shadow and the list the night keeps, and compare each trade the replay keeps with the one the record stored: the stock, the night, its place on the list, the stop and the target as distances from the buy to a millionth, since a split or a dividend since the night rescales the store's closes and a distance does not move with the scale, the session it ended and its result. Where every trade is the same the rule's record carries on from where it began; where one is not it restarts from the registration, and the row the replay writes on the run log names the first trade that differed, which the run page states beside the rule. A rule registered once was never replayed and counts from its registration, which is its first. A registration made before any replay, as the market switches' of 2026-10-03 was, is replayed from the rule's first registration by the next one. An input the store has replaced since a night, a print the calendar restated or bands written at another scale, is read as it stands now, and a difference it makes restarts the record rather than carrying a trade the night did not keep. For a rule the replay carried, "the night the rule registered" in the decision on a registered family rule's record is read as its first registration (see: A registered family rule is evaluated every night at its own settings and keeps its own list, its trades stored with their benchmark when they end).

**A sector heavyweight's trade is scored by its percent return less the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03. A heavyweight is held while it leads its sector, with no stop and no target, so a result in multiples of the risk its plan stated, the measure every swing family is scored in, has nothing to divide by. Its trade is its return in percent from the close it was bought at to the close it was sold at, on adjusted closes, less the equal-weighted return over the same sessions of the companies it was chosen from, the five largest of its sector at the setting of five, so its record asks whether leading picked well among the largest rather than whether the largest did well. Being large is itself a bet that moves by year: over 2019 to 2026 the five largest companies of each sector returned, against every member weighted equally, -2.6, -2.4, -2.9, -0.4, +3.4, +7.3, +6.1 and +2.9 points a year, so a benchmark of every member would have scored the size cut's own years as the rule's skill. Every member's return and the index's are drawn beside it as context and never as its benchmark. Its checkpoints read blocks of 63 sessions, a trade counted in the block it ends in.

**The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-07, handed the phase 14 sign-off review's corrections, which recommended a rebalance that waits over a store keeping 252 sessions. Supersedes **The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month** and **A heavyweight's beta is read over 251 daily returns against the index**, moved below with their reasoning, all of which stands but the premise both stated, that a return over 251 sessions or a beta over 251 daily returns reads no close older than the year of bars the store keeps. The family was ruled on 2026-10-03 as the fourth, on provisional settings until its sweep proposed the ones its freeze registered: "the largest companies in each sector that are outperforming their sector, held while they keep leading. A months-long rotation, not a swing trade, so it needs its own entry, exit, scoring and record." Its universe is the index's members on the session, each in the GICS sector the provider files (see: A company's sector on a session is the GICS sector the provider files, with the fourteen moves of 2023-03-17 read by date), and its size cut a sector's largest companies by value as it stood (see: A company's value on a session is the newest share count filed before it times the session's close on the count's split basis) (see: Companies are ranked by CIK with one listing held, the class that traded the more dollars over fifty sessions). Its look-back is counted in sessions, three, six and twelve months read as 63, 126 and 251, six months the provisional setting, and where a setting reads it a stock's beta is the slope of its daily returns on the index's over the 251 sessions to the rebalance, on the operator's words of 2026-10-03: "Beta: not required. High-beta stocks earned less for their risk (Frazzini and Pedersen 2014), so beta of at least 1 is a variant, not a rule." A return over a number of sessions reads the close that many sessions before the night and a beta over a number of daily returns the closes as many sessions before it, so each needs that number of closes and one more: 252 at the live setting and at each of its three variants, and 127 at the provisional setting, which reads no beta. The store keeps every session from the date a year before the night (see: The bar store holds one year for every night's work, and the history pulled beside it is read by measurements alone), and that year holds 252, 251 or 250 closes as the calendar falls: 251 on a night whose date a year back was no session, every Monday and some nights near Good Friday, and 250 on 2027-03-29. A rebalance at a setting needing more closes than the store's year holds to the night waits: it reads, sells and buys nothing and stores no row of its month, the book's row names the sessions held and needed, and the next night tries again, while each holding is carried and sold under its average where the setting reads that and at its last close as a member, as on every night. A rebalance at which no ranked company of any sector reads a lead, or, where the setting reads a beta, none reads a beta, waits the same way and says why, whatever the cause, a hole in the index's closes inside the beta's window or a store refetched short among them, so a month is never recorded as read with every holding sold on readings that are none; and a month counts as read only where a row of it read a lead. From 2026-10 to 2027-12, 8 of the 15 months' first sessions hold 251: seven wait one session and April 2027 three, from 2027-04-01 to 2027-04-06, and none goes unread. The book reads a rebalance on the first night of a month it runs that holds what its readings need, so a month whose first night did not run, or could not read, rebalances on the night after rather than not at all, and its first night reads one so it holds from then. The sweep reads each month's first session over the pulled history, which holds every close a reading needs, so the live rule rebalances up to three sessions after the session the sweep read; a slow rotation read a few sessions later moves little, and the sweep is not run again for it. A store keeping 252 sessions was weighed and not taken: a longer store changes what about 25 readers of the bars see on Mondays, and the backfill and the missing-session refetch read the same year, so members would read as missing a session. The four registered books' rebalance of 2026-10-05, 829 rows that read nothing on 251 sessions, stays as written, and on the operator's answer of 2026-10-07 to rerunning the heavyweights' judgement, "Re-read October tonight", it is read again on the night of 2026-10-07, which holds 252. The S&P 400's and 600's books wait the same way, design (b) on the sessions of its own window. A month rather than a week because a rotation among the largest companies turns slowly, and the sweep tries both. Its card is drawn after the swing families', with its holdings, the last rebalance's entries and exits, the next rebalance's date, the first session with none read in its month whose stored year holds the live setting's need, a line saying the month's rebalance waits on a night it has not been read, and held while leading in place of a stop and a target, and the night asks for a report on what a rebalance buys, a buy point of the night after every swing family's picks, and never on a holding carried from an earlier month, on the operator's words of 2026-10-03: "A new entry is eligible for the night's reports under the cap of six; a holding carried over is not." (see: The night asks for a report on the first six names its page draws). No model reads, chooses or orders anything it holds (see: Rules choose the stocks, and the AI writes the reports).

**A heavyweight is bought where it leads its sector above nothing and passes the trend gate, and sold where the rule would not buy it** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03: "Leadership: of those 5, the highest 6-month return minus the sector's 6-month return, above zero. Trend: close above the 50-day average, the 50-day above the 200-day." and "Exit, whichever is first: no longer the leader at a rebalance, or a close below the 200-day average, sold at that close." A sector's leaders are its largest companies whose lead over the sector's return is above nothing and whose close sits above its 50-day average with that above its 200-day, the largest lead first and the ticker settling a tie, one a sector at the provisional setting. No longer the leader is read as not among those the rule would buy on the rebalance's session, whatever the reason: another company leads, its lead fell to nothing, its trend gate failed or it left the five. One rule for buying and for holding, because a holding kept where the rule would not buy it is a second rule nobody wrote and nobody tested. A close under the 200-day average sells at that close on any night, a rebalance's or not; a stock holding too few sessions for either average passes no trend gate and is sold by no average it lacks.

**A sector's return is the mean of its members' own returns over the look-back** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03: the benchmark is "the equal-weighted average of the sector's own members. A few companies make up most of a concentrated sector ETF, so comparing them with it is partly comparing them with themselves. The ETF is a variant." Each member of the sector on the session stands in the mean once with its own return over the look-back, the largest companies among them, and a member holding too few closes for the look-back is left out rather than read as nothing, so a new member does not pull its sector toward nought. A lead is a company's own return less that mean.

**A heavyweight leaving the index is sold at its last session's close as a member** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03. The book reads the index's members on its night, and the store asks for no bar of a former member after it leaves, so a holding whose stock is no longer a member ends at the close of the last session it was carried to as one, its result and its size cut's return read to that session. A member of a size cut leaving the index stands in the cut's return at its last close as a member, as if sold there, so the cut's return asks what its members made while members.

**A heavyweight's result is the product of its daily close ratios since its buy, carried each night** A holding's growth and each of its size cut's are carried every night by tonight's adjusted close over the adjusted close of the session they were last carried to, both as the store holds them tonight, and at the sale its result is the growth less one and its size cut's return the mean of its members' growths less one (see: A sector heavyweight's trade is scored by its percent return less the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from). Carried rather than read from the buy's close at the sale, because the store keeps a year of bars and replaces a name's whole year after a corporate action, so the buy's close may be gone, or restated on another basis, by the day of a sale months later; each night's ratio is read on one basis, so a dividend or a split between two nights is in it as the provider adjusts the closes, and the product over the nights is the adjusted return from buy to sale. A stock with no close tonight or none on the session it was last carried to keeps its growth where it stood until it has both.

**The night values a member from its newest fetch, tonight's close brought to the count's basis by the fetch's own close** The quarters step already asks for each member's whole fundamentals answer after its report, and stores from it each balance sheet's share count with the day the sheet was filed and the member's company, CIK and sector, with no request of its own (see: A member's reported quarters are fetched on the night after it reports, and asked for again on the five nights after and weekly after that until the quarter is posted). On a rebalance the night values a member as the newest count filed before the session times tonight's close brought to the count's split basis by the fetch's own close on its newest session over the store's close on that session tonight. A split since the fetch moves both closes alike and leaves the value where it was, which a count restated to the fetch's basis needs. A dividend or a spin-off since the fetch moves the store's adjusted close on that session too, so a company that paid one is valued above its count times its close by that adjustment, about a quarter's dividend for a payer, until the fetch after its next report; the history's reading uses the session's own close and the plain splits alone, and carries none of it (see: A company's value on a session is the newest share count filed before it times the session's close on the count's split basis). A member whose newest fetch filed no count, or whose fetch's session the store no longer holds, is not valued and stands in no rank.

**The heavyweights' sweep replays the book over the pulled history across its settings and proposes the best edge among those meeting the family sweeps' floors** Ruled with phase 14's plan on 2026-10-03, which sets the sweep's settings, its scoring and its report. The sector heavyweights rotate on rebalances rather than list a night, so their sweep walks a book of its own rather than the swing families' nightly lists. It reads the history the sweep history reads with the companies, share counts, splits and sector funds the history pull stored, values each member on every session a rebalance reads as the history's reading values it (see: A company's value on a session is the newest share count filed before it times the session's close on the count's split basis), and reads each sector's largest companies, leads and leaders by the night's own rule. Its 432 settings are every combination of a size cut of 5, 10 or every company, a look-back in sessions of 63, 126 or 251, 1 or 2 leaders a sector, a sector's return from its members' mean or its fund's over the same look-back on the fund's own sessions, a beta of at least one read or not (see: The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need), a rebalance on the first session of each month or of each week from its Monday, and an exit on no longer leading at a rebalance, on a close under the two-hundred-day average, or on whichever comes first; the provisional setting is one of them. The fund that holds Communication Services began on 2018-06-19, so at the twelve-month look-back that sector reads no fund's return, and buys nothing, until mid-2019. The walk holds one holding a stock and sells as the book sells: at the close of its last session as a member where the stock leaves the index, at a close under its average where the exit reads it, and at a rebalance's close where the exit reads leading and the rule would not buy it; a leader not held is bought at the rebalance's close with its sector's size cut, and one with no close that session is not. Each holding is scored by its return in percent less the equal-weighted return of its size cut over the same sessions (see: A sector heavyweight's trade is scored by its percent return less the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from), every member's and the index's returns beside it as context, and it counts in the year of the session it was sold on; one still held at the history's end counts in none, as a setup not yet resolved counts as nothing (see: An unresolved setup is never a win), and each setting states how many. The proposal is the best edge among the settings with at least 300 trades and an edge above nothing in at least 6 of the 8 years, the family sweeps' floors, ties to the fewest dials moved from the provisional setting and then the key, and the report states every setting one step from it, the size cut and the look-back a level either way and each other dial at each of its other levels, the exit's two others among them. An edge with no effect stands above nothing in 6 of 8 years in 37 of the 256 ways the years can fall, so luck alone would put about 62 of 432 independent settings there; settings one step apart hold mostly the same stocks, so the neighbours, and not that count, say whether the proposal stands on its own. The replay at the provisional setting is held to every rebalance the night's book stored, each sector's companies in their places, each lead within a billionth, the trend gate and the leaders, and every sector it reads differently is named: the night values a member from its newest fetch and the sweep from the pulled counts and splits, so a dividend since a fetch or a count filed after the pull can move a rank. Nothing is registered: the family's freeze is the operator's.

**The sector heavyweights freeze at their sweep's proposal, the proposal's three passing neighbours registered beside them as variants** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04, answering after the sweep's report: "The proposal (Recommended)". The night holds the sector heavyweights at the setting the sweep proposed, the best edge among the 32 of its 432 settings meeting the family sweeps' floors: each sector's ten largest companies, a look-back of 251 sessions, two leaders a sector, a sector's return read from its fund over the fund's own sessions, a leader's beta at least one, a rebalance on the first session of each month, and a holding sold at a rebalance where the rule would no longer buy it and at its last close as a member, with no sale at a close under its 200-day average. Over 2019 to 2026 it made 374 trades at an edge of 1.34 points, above nothing in 6 of the 8 years.
