Three of its nine one-step neighbours also met the floors, and each is registered beside it as a variant at one instant with it: sold on either exit, the sector's return read as its members' mean, and every company of a sector in the size cut. It narrows three entries written for the provisional setting without superseding them, each describing what a setting reads: the size cut, the look-back and the leaders the family's own entry names are the frozen ones (see: The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need), a sector's return is its fund's at the live setting and its members' mean in the variant reading that (see: A sector's return is the mean of its members' own returns over the look-back), and a close under the 200-day average sells only where a setting reads it, the variant selling on either exit (see: A heavyweight is bought where it leads its sector above nothing and passes the trend gate, and sold where the rule would not buy it). The night asks for the eleven funds' closes (see: The night asks for the market series' daily closes once a series, and keeps them apart from the members' bars) and reads each stock's beta against the index's closes it stores (see: The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need). A rebalance reading the funds or the betas waits for the first night the store holds the night's close of every fund and of the index, so a series the night could not fetch moves no holding, and the book's row names what it waits for. Each rule is judged by the test its registration states, its holdings' edge over blocks of 63 sessions by the sign-flip test at 0.05 shared among the family's own rules (see: Each registered sector heavyweights rule keeps a book of its own beside the page's, its holdings scored in percent against their size cut). The card draws the page's book at the live setting, with the day its live rule registered and the variants beside it.

**Each registered sector heavyweights rule keeps a book of its own beside the page's, its holdings scored in percent against their size cut** Ruled with the freeze on 2026-10-04. The page's book holds at the live setting the freeze names, so the card reads the family's rule whatever the register holds, and each heavyweights rule standing registered when the night started keeps a book of its own beside it, in tables of its own, at its own registration's settings, read and held as the page's is: its rebalances, each sector's largest companies with their values, returns, leads, trend, beta and leaders, and its holdings, each carried every night and, once it ends, its result and its size cut's return in percent, written once. A book of its own rather than the page's read at other settings, because a variant's holdings part from the live rule's wherever their settings part, and a record read off another rule's holdings is that rule's record. The live rule's book holds what the page's holds, and each card keeps its own one trade a stock (see: A stock holds one trade across every swing family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order). A rule's record is its holdings' edge, the result less the size cut's return, summed over blocks of 63 sessions from the session it registered, a holding counted in the block of the session it ended on and a block whole once its last session has passed, read at the looks the register's candidates are read at by the sign-flip test at the level the family's own Holm graph gives it over its own rules alone (see: Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family) (see: A sector heavyweight's trade is scored by its percent return less the equal-weighted return of the size cut it was chosen from), and the run page draws it beside the swing families' rules'. A rule whose evaluator a change of code moves is not replayed, since the guard replays the swing families' nightly lists and no replay of a book is built: its record restarts at the registration again, the row the replay writes for it says so, and the run page states it beside the rule (see: A family rule registered again keeps its record from its first registration where a replay of its stored nights reproduces every trade, and restarts at the change otherwise). A rule the code carries no evaluator for at the version it stands at is kept by no book, and the book's row names it as a failure.

**Six context checks are not adopted, each for the reason it states** Weighed in phase 14's evaluation and ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03. Gating on positive guidance: many companies give none, the provider keeps no history of it, and analyst revisions stand in for it where a rule wants the market's changing view. Rising ownership by institutions, read from their quarterly 13F filings: each is due 45 days after its quarter, so it reads up to four and a half months stale, and the change is small for companies institutions already mostly hold, which is most of the index. A quality gate applied before any testing: a gate joins a rule only with evidence from the history, and the pullback's rule that leaves off a business whose reported quarters read deteriorating measures it on the live record. Short interest: heavily shorted stocks have gone on to underperform (Asquith, Pathak and Ritter, 2005), but the provider holds today's figure alone, FINRA's free files keep five rolling years, from about October 2021, and the one source documented back to 2017-12-29 is a subscription of $29 a month, so nothing is built until the operator buys a history. The size of a revenue beat: the provider's estimate is one figure a period with no date it was made on, a fiscal year's end holding the year's figure and one quarter in four holding none, and over 12 names its earnings estimate matched the one standing before the report within a cent in 23 of 2019's 36 quarters, so a beat as it stood before a report cannot be read. The news check on the pullback: ruled to read only the provider's sentiment, and only if VADER over the article's text reproduces it; over 200 articles drawn evenly across 2019 to 2026 the best of six readings reproduced all four of its figures to three decimals on 42 against a bar of 190, so the score is not shown to be a fixed word list's and the check is not run. Each is taken up again only on evidence that answers its reason.

**A wider universe is tested first on today's members, and widened only where a family's edge improves even so and holds on membership as it stood** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04, after the S&P 400 and 600 were asked again with historical=1 and answered today's members alone, 400 and 603, with no span of membership: "Part C, a first test on today's members, after phase 14's report", nothing bought and no paid model call. Today's members of the two indices are stored by a members pull, one request an index, and their eight years of bars, earnings prints, surprises and splits by the history pull's own pulls given that index, each into the pulled history's tables and read by no night. The sweep history reads the 1,500 as the S&P 500's history as stored with those members beside it, each read as a member on every session, since nothing says when one joined; a name the S&P 500 never held is a survivor. Each swing family's rule as frozen, the pullback's base, the breakout and the earnings drift, is replayed over the 1,500 and over the 500 alone, and the 1,500 is judged against the 500 by the test the ideas' run judges an idea by, on the edge (see: A new idea is added to the base one at a time and kept only where it is better in six of eight years), with what luck alone passes over the three tries. A history of today's members holds the companies that stayed in or grew into the indices and none that fell out or failed, which flatters the wider universe, so every figure the run states for the 1,500 says it holds survivors only. Where no family's edge improves even so, the widening is dropped and recorded as not adopted. Where one improves, nothing is adopted until the result holds on membership as it stood, read from the provider's add-on or from the iShares IJH and IJR funds' holdings by date, since a gain survivors alone show may be theirs and not the rule's. The heavyweights stay on the 500.

**The S&P 400 and 600 join the universe with rules of their own, since the first test showed only that the 500's settings do not carry to them** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04 with phase 15's brief: "The Part C test showed the S&P 500's frozen settings do not transfer to mid and small caps; it did not show the 400 and 600 cannot give good picks." The first test replayed each swing family's S&P 500 rule as frozen over the 1,500 as one pool, its strength ranked, its market check read and its edge benchmarked over the three indices together, so its figures are over a mixed population and answer whether the 500's settings carry to the wider universe, not whether the 400 or the 600 can give good picks under settings of their own. They stand as the evidence that each index needs its own settings: over the 1,949 nights from 2019-01-02 to 2026-10-02, survivors only, the pullback's base made an edge of 0.100 over 1,903 trades on the 500 alone and -0.017 over 5,354 on the 1,500, the breakout 0.082 over 1,009 and 0.067 over 2,454, and the earnings drift 0.121 over 2,243 and -0.222 over 3,809, none passing the ideas' run's test where luck alone passes about 0.40 of the three tries. So the S&P 400 and 600 join the universe from 15.1, each running every family as rules of its own with settings, variants, records and checkpoints of their own, and the S&P 500's rules stay as frozen. Every pulled table stays under the pull that wrote it, read by no night. It narrows, without superseding, the rule of the first test, which governs widening the S&P 500's frozen rules to more names: they stay on the 500 as frozen, and the 400's and 600's rules are rules of their own (see: A wider universe is tested first on today's members, and widened only where a family's edge improves even so and holds on membership as it stood). The published evidence for why each index needs settings of its own is in section 11.4, each finding with its source and what is judgement.

Supersedes **The wider universe is not adopted, since no swing family's edge improves on it even on survivors**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands as the test's record but that the universe stays the S&P 500: the operator ruled that the test answered whether the 500's settings carry to the 1,500 and not whether the 400 and 600 can give good picks.

**The 400 and 600 rules start provisional with liquidity floors and a profit gate before any testing** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04 with phase 15's brief: "a minimum price of $5, a minimum 50-day dollar volume of $10 million for the 400 and $5 million for the 600, the quality gate on (profit over the last four quarters), each index's own breadth, and the S&P 500's settings otherwise". A 400 or 600 member passes the floors where its close is at least $5 and its mean of close times volume over the 50 sessions to the night is at least $10 million on the 400 and $5 million on the 600, and the profit gate where its net income over the four newest quarters filed before the session sums above nothing, as S&P's own admission criterion reads earnings; a member with fewer than four quarters filed does not pass. Measured over today's members with the pulled bars, survivors only: on 2026-10-02 the floors leave out none of the 363 400 members read with 50 sessions and 7 of the 541 600 members, 4 of them under $5, and on 2019-06-28 23 of 324 and 111 of 480. Over 30 members drawn with a stated seed, 6 sum to a loss over their four newest quarters, 2 of 15 on the 400 and 4 of 15 on the 600. It narrows, without superseding, the quality gate's clause of the six checks not adopted, which stands for the S&P 500's rules: on the 400 and 600 the gate rests on the published evidence and the operator's ruling rather than on the history, and each family's sweep on each index reads it at four levels, off, profit, profit and coverage, and all three, before any freeze (see: Six context checks are not adopted, each for the reason it states). Evidence: Asness, Frazzini, Israel, Moskowitz and Pedersen (2018) find the size premium stable, and not held in the smallest companies, once low-quality firms are controlled for; S&P admits a company to the 400 or 600 only on positive earnings over its last four quarters and its latest, which is why few of today's members fail. Judgement: the $5 and the two dollar volumes, which no study sets, each a dial of every 400 and 600 rule's sweep.

**A 400 or 600 trade pays the published effective spread for its size and price, and its pass tests read the edge after it** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04, answering phase 15's planning pass: "Published spread table (Recommended)", in place of the brief's spread estimated from daily highs and lows (Corwin and Schultz 2012). Measured read-only on the store's bars over 2025-09-26 to 2026-09-25, the daily-bar estimators put the median member's round trip at 0.663, 0.790 and 0.877 per cent for the S&P 500, 400 and 600 by Corwin and Schultz, 0.390, 0.489 and 0.444 by Abdi and Ranaldo, and 0.348, 0.500 and 0.514 by EDGE (Ardia, Guidotti and Kroencke 2024) run from the authors' own code, EDGE negative for 22 to 32 per cent of names over the year and its 2019 median for the 500 below nothing. An S&P 500 trade pays about 3 basis points (Hagströmer), so the estimators overstate a large company's cost ten to twenty times and barely separate the 600 from the 500; at a stop 1.5 typical moves beneath, Corwin and Schultz would charge 0.17 to 0.19 of a trade's risk against the families' edges of 0.08 to 0.12, and every rule would fail for the estimator's noise. The round trip a trade pays is half the mean relative effective spread of its company's value band and its price band at entry and half at exit, from the SEC's table of 2013's trades (Collver 2014, Table 4): the value bands $250 to 500 million, $500 million to $1 billion, $1 to 2 billion and $2 to 5 billion, a company above $5 billion read in the last, and the price bands $2 to 6, $6 to 10, $10 to 20, $20 to 40 and $40 and over; a company with no count is read in the $1 to 2 billion band and the page says so. It is stated at the table's value and at double, since the table is 2013's and no measurement of the store's own years stands beside it. A 400 or 600 rule's record stores each trade's result before and after it and its pass tests read the edge after it; the S&P 500's rules show the edge after it beside their frozen figures, their records unchanged. Evidence: Chordia, Goyal, Sadka, Sadka and Shivakumar (2009) find costs take 70 to 100 per cent of a drift strategy's paper profits in the least liquid stocks, and Collver (2014) measures the spreads. Judgement: the doubling, and reading a company above $5 billion in the $2 to 5 billion band, which overstates its cost.

**A trade's cost comes off the trade and not its benchmark** Ruled with phase 15's plan on 2026-10-05. The benchmark is what the same plan made entered on every member of the index that night, the market's drift in the plan's shape, and nobody trades it, so it pays no cost: the edge after costs is the edge before them less the trade's own round trip. Taken off both, the edge after costs would measure only whether a rule picks dearer names than the average member, which is not what a trade costs.

**The 400's and 600's earnings drift lists from the first night with the evidence against it on its card** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04, answering phase 15's planning pass: "Picks from night one". The drift lists on the 400 and 600 from the first night, provisional like every new rule, and its card carries one line: "Published evidence finds this drift gone outside microcaps (Martineau 2022), and the 14.8 test measured -0.222 on the 1,500." Martineau (2022) finds prices now take in an earnings surprise on the day it is announced, the drift gone from large companies since 2006 and more recently from smaller ones, and the S&P 400 and 600 hold no microcaps; Chordia et al. (2009) find what drift there was sat in the least liquid stocks, where costs took most of it. The card states the evidence so a reader weighs a pick against it, and each index's sweep reads the drift after costs; the evidence keeps nothing off the page (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word).

**Analyst coverage is stored from each fetch and waits for dated counts before any rule tests it** Ruled with phase 15's plan on 2026-10-05. The provider's fundamentals answer carries the analysts' rating counts, strong buy, buy, hold, sell and strong sell, AAPL's 23, 7, 16, 1 and 1, and 29 of 30 sampled 400 and 600 members carry at least one. They are today's counts alone with no dated history, so no sweep can read coverage as it stood on a past session, and one reading today's counts back over eight years would read the future. From 15.2 the night stores the five counts' sum from each fundamentals answer the quarters fetch asks for, dated by the fetch, and the name page draws it as context; no rule reads it until the store holds four dated fetches for 90 per cent of the 400's and 600's members (owes: Analyst coverage tested as a dial once dated counts exist). Hong, Lim and Stein (2000) find momentum works better where coverage is low, mostly among past losers, which a rule that only buys does not hold.

**A member's coverage is read on the night only over quarters whose fetch read their interest expense** Made with 15.2's second pull request on 2026-10-06. The quarters step stored no interest expense before 15.2, and the coverage reads a company filing no interest expense on any of its four newest quarters as passing, so a quarter stored before would read as a company owing nothing and pass every member the night has not fetched again since. Each stored quarter carries whether its fetch read the interest expense, and the night stores a member's coverage only where every one of the four quarters it reads carries it, and none otherwise, which the name page says; each member is fetched again on the night after it next reports, so every member's coverage is read within one reporting season. The sweeps read the pulled income, which carries the interest expense as filed, and meet no such quarter. Judgement: reading none rather than reading the older quarters as passing or as failing, since either would be a figure no filing states.

**The 400 and 600 each sweep two heavyweight designs and keep the stronger after costs** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-04 with phase 15's brief. Design (a), leaders within the index: on the first session of each month, each sector's largest members of the index that lead their sector's members, with the profit gate and the floors, held while they lead; it starts as the provisional book at the S&P 500's frozen settings within the index, the ten largest, 251 sessions, two leaders, a sector's return its members' mean since no fund holds an index's sector, and a leader's beta at least one against the index's fund, IJH or IJR. Design (b), followers of the heavyweights: the industries whose S&P 500 members' value-weighted return leads the S&P 500's over 21 sessions or over 63, then that index's members in those industries by their own strength within the index and the gate, held while the industry's lead lasts. Each is scored in percent against the equal-weighted members of the same sector in its index, after costs, and the stronger meeting the family sweeps' floors is kept; where neither does, the provisional book keeps listing (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). Hou (2007) finds big firms lead small ones within an industry, mainly in taking in bad news; design (b) buys on good news and so rests on the weaker side of it, which its card states.

**The S&P 400's and 600's sector heavyweights are searched over six settings a design registered before the run** Made with 17.1's second pull request on 2026-10-08, on the operator's brief of that day: "at most six settings per design, pre-registered in the entry before the run, each justified from the evidence ... and only those tested, so the luck figure stays near one." The sweeps of 15.4 read 648 settings of design (a) and 72 of design (b) on each index, and none beat what luck alone passes of so many: 47 and 80 of design (a)'s met the floors against about 94, and 7 and 3 of design (b)'s against about 10. Six settings a design are read instead, each written with its evidence in a ruling entry committed before the run, the same six on both indices: design (a)'s provisional book, twelve months' strength skipping the latest month (Jegadeesh and Titman 1993; Asness, Moskowitz and Pedersen 2013), the profit gate with the interest cover (Novy-Marx 2013; Asness, Frazzini and Pedersen 2019), a rebalance on each quarter's first session (Novy-Marx and Velikov 2016 on what turnover costs), the beta floor off (Frazzini and Pedersen 2014), and the second, third and fourth together; design (b)'s base, the 10 industries leading SPY most over 63 sessions and one member an industry, with the same moves, two members an industry in the fifth place. Each is walked and scored as the sweep walks and scores its grid, and luck alone puts about 0.87 of six above nothing in six of the eight years. A setting meeting the floors passes and is brought to the operator; nothing is frozen and every family keeps listing (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). Judgement: twelve months' strength read on the calendar's closes 252 and 21 sessions back, none where either is missing; design (b)'s base at its grid's longer window and middle count, since the design holds no provisional book; and design (a)'s fifth place the beta floor off, since its provisional book holds the two leaders a sector the plan named there.

**The S&P 400's and 600's breakouts and drift read an entry on the first pullback within ten sessions as a level of their second stage** Made with 17.1's second pull request on 2026-10-08, from the operator's brief of that day: for the breakouts "a close in the top quarter of the day's range on the breakout day, and entry on the first pullback to the breakout level within 10 sessions", and for the S&P 400's drift "entry on the first pullback after the reaction day within 10 sessions", beside its "minimum surprise of 5% or 10%", hold limits of 10, 21 and 42 sessions, the revenue growth behind the beat and the other index's fund against SPY. Each is a level of the second stage, tried alone on the ten strongest settings and kept where it survives on six, as every level is. A breakout's pullback is the first session within ten after its listing whose low reaches the high it broke, read over the sessions its setting reads, and whose close stands above it, bought at that close with its stop and trail the setting's typical moves in that session's own; a drift's is the first session within ten after its reaction whose close falls below the close before it, bought at that close with the reaction's low still its stop and its target the nearer of the band and the setting's multiple of the new risk. Where that first session finds the market check closed, the member out of the index or holding a gap, or the drift's close at or under its stop, there is no trade, and the walk reads no later session. Every other level's test reads the listing as the rule made it, the floors and the gate are read on the session bought, and a day with no range closes in its top quarter. Judgement: no study tests either entry, So and Wang (2014) and Savor (2012) bearing on the drift's only from aside; the stop read again on the new session for the breakout, whose plan is a distance in typical moves, and left at the reaction's low for the drift, whose plan is that low.

**An index's pullback search looks a second time beyond a grid end and names the dial that binds its depth** Made with 17.1's second pull request on 2026-10-08, from the operator's brief of that day: "S&P 600 pullback: depth high and freshness extended past their grid ends, depth measured again." The S&P 600's search of 15.4 looked two values beyond its depth's high end and its freshness's, to 12 typical moves and 14 sessions, and its proposal's depth stayed at nought, the dial binding it carried and never printed. The extended grid holds two values more on each, 14 and 16 moves and 17 and 21 sessions, the arrival window reading 21 sessions back; an S&P 400 or 600 search looks beyond a grid end a second time where the end still binds the depth and the extended grid holds values past it, and every setting its report reads states its depth with the dial and the direction binding it, and whether a grid end does. The S&P 500's search looks once, as it did. Judgement: two more values on the two dials the brief named, at the spacing of the last two tested, and the arrival window widened with the freshness, since a freshness of 21 reads a trigger arriving 21 sessions back.

**The S&P 400's drift passes a search only where its edge over 2025 and 2026 together stands above nothing** Made with 17.1's second pull request on 2026-10-08, from the operator's brief of that day: "S&P 400 drift (positive over eight years, negative in 2025 and 2026) ... Extra pass condition: positive over 2025 and 2026 combined." A setting of the S&P 400's drift search, a grid setting or a crossing of its second stage, passes only where it meets the floors and its edge after costs over the history's last two years, each year's edge weighted by its trades, stands above nothing; the report lists every setting meeting the floors with that edge, and the answer the run states reads it. Before the run none of the grid's 81 settings and none of the second stage's 170 tries holding 300 trades met it, the best at a loss of 0.053 and of 0.015 risks a trade. The condition is the S&P 400's alone, as the brief names it.

**A 400 or 600 swing pick may be kept off where its industry's S&P 500 heavyweights fell, a dial its sweeps test** Proposed with phase 15's plan and taken with it on 2026-10-05. Hou (2007) finds the lead of big firms over small ones within an industry runs mainly through bad news, and a rule that only buys uses that side by keeping off a small company whose industry's large ones have just fallen: each 400 and 600 swing family's sweep reads a dial keeping off a pick whose GICS industry's S&P 500 members' value-weighted return over the 21 or the 63 sessions before fell below nothing, off at its provisional setting. Thomas and Zhang (2008) find investors overreact to early reporters' news for late reporters, corrected at the late reporter's own report, which is why the drift's peer reading is read after the member's own report and not before it. Judgement: the two look-backs, a month and a quarter.

**Membership as it stood is rebuilt from the funds' quarterly holdings filed with the SEC, matched by ISIN and then by name** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05, answering 15.0's data checks: "Free, two keys (Recommended)". iShares no longer serves its funds' holdings for a past date: on 2026-10-04 the IJR page offered 2026-10-01, 2026-09-30 and 2025-12-31 alone, and the dated file answered the product page for 2019-01-31 and 2022-12-30. Each fund files its quarter-end holdings with the SEC, IJR as series S000004313 and IJH as S000004307 of iShares Trust: 28 public N-PORT filings each from the quarter to 2019-09-30, N-Q filings to the quarter to 2018-12-31, and the annual reports, each holding named with its CUSIP and ISIN and no ticker. Matched by ISIN alone against the provider's US symbols, current and delisted, the snapshots of 2019-09-30 reached 92.5 per cent of IJR's 604 equity holdings and 95.3 of IJH's 401, short of the 98 per cent the rule stated before the run, and those of 2024-12-31 98.0 and 99.0; the company's name as a second key reached 98.7 to 99.5 on all four, though 22 of IJR 2019's 37 name matches hit more than one symbol. So a holding is matched by ISIN and then by its name, an ambiguous name settled by which symbol has bars on the snapshot's date, 30 name matches are checked by hand at 15.3, and a holding matched by neither is listed and left out, every figure saying so. A name is a member from the first snapshot holding it to the last, so a join or a leave is dated within a quarter, and the quarter to 2019-06-30 holds no public snapshot. Nothing is bought.

**A holding matched by name is kept only where its code traded at the quarter's end** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05: "Name matching: keep a name match only where its ticker traded at that quarter end. Re-check the same 30 by hand after the fix and state the count." Of 30 name matches checked by hand after 15.3's remedy, 24 were the holding's own company, 1 uncertain and 5 another security the name was later carried by, most of them a code that held no bar in the years the holding stood: Aaron's Inc of 2019 read as the company spun off in 2020, McDermott International as the shares issued after its 2020 bankruptcy, and Noble Corp plc of 2019 as the company formed in 2022. A name names a company and not a security, so a match by it is kept only where its code traded in the six days to the snapshot's quarter end: a bar the history pull holds for it in those days or, where it holds none, a session the provider's own daily prices for those days carry, asked once a code a quarter, and no name match is made where which codes traded is not known. Several codes trading are settled by a main exchange before another, a code still listed before a delisted one, and the code itself: the indices admit only stocks listed on a main exchange, and the provider's prices answer for any code a name could be read as, so another company's line over the counter sharing the name, as Noble Group's do Noble Corp plc's, is not read before the index's own delisted listing. A company renamed since, whose old name the provider no longer carries, is read by the name the fund's own filings carry beside an ISIN, after the provider's names; and a non-voting class's mark and what follows a slash, a place or a state the filing names the listing by, are no part of a name. A quarter an earlier pull stored is matched again by the next pull that reads it, each holding kept as first stored and its code and key changed where the rule as it stands reads another, so no pull is removed to be pulled again, a removal the operator keeps for their own word (see: The history pulled before the store's year sits apart from its bars, marked by the pull that wrote it, read by no night and removed whole by that pull). Ruled again by the operator the same day: "Unmatched names: before a name is left unmatched, search the provider's delisted symbols by company name and dates for the code it traded under at that quarter end (for example McDermott's and Noble's pre-restructuring listings), applying the same traded-that-quarter rule." And: "Yes to the value-per-share check: a match stands only where the filing's value per share is within a stated tolerance of the code's close at that quarter end, which separates common stock from units and preferred lines. State the tolerance and the matches it changes." So a name match stands only where the value per share the fund filed is within 5 per cent of the code's close on the quarter's end, as the provider sends it or with the splits it files after that day undone, the 5 per cent measured against that close; ruled again by the operator on 2026-10-05, this sentence having stated the 5 per cent against the value a share where the code reads it against the close: "Correct the decision's sentence to match the code: the 5% is measured against the stock's close." Commerce Bancshares' holding is the one the two readings part on, 5.00007 per cent from its close and 4.76 per cent from the value a share. Over the 26,976 holdings of the 56 N-PORT filings matched by ISIN, 99.4 per cent sit within 1 per cent of the close as sent; the name matches that sat outside it did so by more than 20 per cent, a company's units, as Aqua America's at $60.60 against the $44.83 the fund valued its shares at, and the codes another company took with the name after a merger, as Caesars', Coherent's, Cadence's, Dime's, Safehold's and Gannett's. The provider sends some codes' closes already divided by a split made later and others as they traded, which is why both are read. A name that neither the provider's names nor the funds' own filings carry word for word is read against the listed and delisted symbols sharing its first word and at least half the words the two names hold between them, the five closest, under the same two tests; and the names any holdings pull stored with their ISIN's code, both funds' alike, are read beside the pull's own.

**A holding's code is kept only where its closes move in step with the fund's values a share, and a match by name is held to the level at each quarter end as well** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05: "(A): keep a ticker for a holding only where its quarter-end closes move in step with the fund's reported value per share for that holding, quarter after quarter, as proposed. Before any of the eight wrong-price tickers is left unmatched, try the company's correct delisted listing (for example BBBYQ for Bed Bath & Beyond) under the same check, and report which recovered." And on 2026-10-08, the go for "the 15.3 correction (the level test against the close for name matches)". After the remedy of 15.3's correction, eight codes the provider files under a holding's own ISIN were found carrying another security's prices, over 61 holdings: COR_old, filed under CoreSite's ISIN, carrying AmerisourceBergen's; EE_old, FG_old and GOV at a few cents; and ARCH_old, BABY, BBBY_old and RPT_old. An ISIN names a security, and a match by it read no price, so nothing refused them. So every code a holding is matched to, by its ISIN or by its name, is read over the quarter ends it is matched at: the value a share the fund filed over the code's close, as the provider sends the close or with the splits it files after undone, whichever sits nearer the same ratio at the quarter end before, holds within 5 per cent from one quarter end to the next, or steps to a level it holds at the next quarter end as well, as the provider's closes divided down for a corporate action step and then hold. A code read at a single quarter end stands within 5 per cent of its close there, as a name match must, and a code read at none, which only an ISIN can match, is kept. A code failing it is matched to none at those quarter ends, and the holding's codes by its name, its ISIN set aside, are read in its place under the same test and one more: a code a holding's name reads over several quarter ends stands within 5 per cent of the fund's value a share at each of them, as it must at one, as well as moving in step across them, because a ratio that holds steady off the level is another security's price moving with the holding's and not the holding's own. The sign-off review of phase 15 found GrubHub matched by name to GRUB at five quarter ends from 2020-03-31 to 2021-03-31 at a steady ratio of about 6.5 between the fund's value a share and the code's close, which the in-step reading alone kept. A delisted listing the pulled history holds no close of is asked of the provider once a quarter, and each code is kept at the quarter ends it holds a close at. A code matched by ISIN is held to the in-step reading alone, since the ISIN names the security and a constant distance from the value a share is the provider's adjustment of the company's own closes for a corporate action. Over the stored holdings before the in-step rule, the eight wander from one quarter end to the next, BBBY_old's ratio in the S&P 600's fund running 0.373, 0.206, 0.370 and 0.440; six codes step at a spin-off to a level they hold, XPO's at 2.893 for eight quarter ends, 1.683 for five and 1.000 for fifteen, and VGR's, ZD's, BERY's, ENSG's and SITC's once each; and every other code read at two quarter ends or more holds within 1 per cent at every step. A name's code off the level is kept where the value a share over its close is one ratio at every quarter end it is read at, two or more, within half a per cent, ruled by the operator on 2026-10-08 when the first rerun showed the level test refusing Providence Service's eight quarters at a ratio to PRSC of exactly 3.000 at each, the provider's own closes divided by a factor for a corporate action it files as no split: "Keep exact ratios", that is, a name's code off the level stands where its ratio is identical within 0.5 per cent at two or more quarter ends. Another security's price moving with the holding's does not hold one ratio: GrubHub's GRUB, a listing that began trading on 2020-03-25, ran from 4.53 to 6.71, a spread of 48 per cent, and Dime Community's schedule quarters read Bridge Bancorp's closes at 0.666 and 0.639, 4.2 per cent apart, and both stay refused. A name's code off the level at no one ratio, which the rule before this kept, is matched to none and the holding named with it. Supersedes **A holding's code is kept only where its closes move in step with the fund's values a share from one quarter end to the next**, whose reading stands for a code by ISIN and for the step a code may take, and whose last sentence kept what this refuses.

**A code the fund held by ISIN beside a holding at one quarter end is another holding and never that holding renamed** A company renamed since its holding was filed is held by its fund under its old ISIN at the quarter ends before the rename and under its new one at the quarter ends after, and never under both at one quarter end, because one security is one line of a fund's schedule. So the codes read for a holding still matched to none under the renamed company's rule leave out every code the fund held by ISIN, as another holding, at a quarter end this holding was held at: such a code is that other holding's security, whatever its close reads against this holding's value a share. The constructed fund that holds the correction's level test found the case: a holding valued at $50 whose name's code sat a fifth under it at every quarter end fell through to the renamed company's rule once the level test refused that code, and another holding's code, held by ISIN beside it at every one of its quarter ends and closing at $50.00 at two of them after a step the in-step reading allows, was equal to the cent twice and in step, and so was taken as the holding renamed. Without this a holding the level test refuses can be matched to whichever of its fund's other holdings happens to close at its value a share.

**A holding of a schedule filed with no identifier carries the code the next coded quarter's holding of the identical name matched, where the closes move in step across them** The N-Q of 2018-12-31 and the annual report of 2019-03-31 file each holding by its name alone, so their holdings are keyed by name and never meet the same company's quarters keyed by ISIN, and a name the symbol list does not carry word for word is matched to none at those two quarter ends though the next quarter's N-PORT matched the identical name by ISIN. So a holding of either schedule still matched to none after its name is read carries the code the next later quarter's holding of the identical name, read with the corporate suffixes taken out, matched by ISIN or by name, where that code holds a close at the schedule's quarter end, asked of the provider once where the pulled history holds none, the fund's values a share over its closes move in step across the schedule's quarter and the coded quarters joined, those being the coded holding's own security's quarters read by its key across any later rename, as the in-step reading over the coded quarters reads them, and the join itself holds the ratio: the schedule's quarter is in step with the first coded quarter read alone, within the 5 per cent, since the step the in-step reading allows once across a series would otherwise let a schedule's quarter at any ratio join a later company that took the name. It is stored under the key `carried`. A holding whose next coded quarter's identical name matched no code, whose code holds no close on the day, or whose quarter stands at another ratio than the first coded quarter's stays matched to none. The first rerun over the S&P 400's store on 2026-10-08, before the join was held, carried the old Caesars Entertainment Corp.'s quarter to 2019-03-31 to CZR, the ticker Eldorado took with the name in 2020, and Coherent Inc.'s two to COHR, II-VI's since 2022, each at a ratio nowhere near the one the coded quarters held; and it left XPO's quarter matched to none, since its coded quarters filed as XPO Logistics Inc end at 2022-12-30 with one quarter at a ratio of 1.000 after five at 1.683, a step at the last quarter of the name, before the company files as XPO Inc. Joined by the security's key and held at the join, the reruns carried eight S&P 400 quarters, Apergy's two to CHX, Commerce Bancshares' quarter to 2019-03-31 to CBSH, which the evaluation read as already matched and was matched to none, Worthington Industries' two to WOR, j2 Global's two to ZD and XPO's one, and twelve S&P 600 quarters, two each of Cabot Microelectronics to CCMP, Lexington Realty to LXP, Office Depot to ODP, Glatfelter to GLT, Regis to RGS and Universal Forest Products to UFPI.

**A holding the symbol lists carry under no code is mapped to the tickers it traded under through OpenFIGI, each held to the checks a name's code is** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-08: "OpenFIGI: proceed without a key; ask only if the keyless limit slows the run." The provider's symbol lists carry a security under its ISIN only while the provider files it, so a holding whose company was acquired, renamed or delisted is found by neither its ISIN nor, where the provider's name differs from the fund's, its name, and 27 of the live store's holdings matched to none on 2026-10-08 carried an identifier. OpenFIGI maps an identifier to every listing it knows, free, keyless and at twenty-five requests a minute of ten identifiers each, read off the answer's own rate limit headers. Asked under the US composite's exchange code it answered no identifier found for all 27, since a delisted security's composite listing is withdrawn; asked with its unlisted flag and no exchange code it answered 23 of them, 15 with the ticker the security traded under on a US venue, as COHR for Coherent and COR for CoreSite, beside a placeholder of digits and a closing D on the venues it no longer lists, and 8 under placeholders alone, and found nothing for the two contra lines, Leslie's and Xperi Holding. So a holding still matched to none after its name is read, whose key carries an ISIN, is mapped once, ten identifiers a request at the keyless pace, and each ticker answered on a US venue, the placeholders left out, is read against the holding's quarters exactly as a name's code is: asked of the provider for its close at each quarter end, kept at the quarter ends it holds a close at where it moves in step over them and sits at the level at each, and stored under the key `figi`. The mapping names a ticker and never a price, and a ticker the provider now files another security's prices under, as CADE for BancorpSouth's history or COR for Cencora's, is refused by those checks as any other code is. A request refused or that cannot be read is named on the pull's row with the identifiers it carried and the pull goes on, since the mapping adds matches and its absence takes none away. On 2026-10-08 the mapping matched 4 S&P 400 holding-quarters, Cabot Microelectronics' to CCMP, and 28 S&P 600 ones, Actuant's to EPAC, Cabot Microelectronics' to CCMP, Indivior's to INDV, Lexington Realty's to LXP, Office Depot's to ODP, Regis' to RGS and Universal Forest Products' to UFPI, while Coherent's COHR, CoreSite's COR and Cadence's CADE stood at no level and no one ratio and were refused. The identifiers are mapped and not the names, because OpenFIGI's name search ranks by likeness and a likeness is what the matcher already reads; and the pull asks the keyless limit's pace rather than a key, on the operator's word, at one to three requests a pull, far under it.

**A renamed company is matched to a code its fund held by ISIN within a year where its close equals the value a share to the cent at two quarter ends** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05: "(B): match a renamed company to a ticker the same fund held by security code within a year, accepted only where its close equals the fund's value per share to the cent on two or more quarter ends, as proposed. Apply (A)'s tracking check to (B)'s matches as well." A company renamed since its holding was filed, and given a new ISIN with the name, is found by neither key: the provider files its code under the new ISIN and the new name, as it files Essential Utilities' and none under Aqua America's. So a holding's quarter ends left matched to none after its ISIN, its name and the tracking check, two of them or more, are read against the codes the same fund held by ISIN at a quarter end within 366 days of one of them, off the pulled history alone and with no ask of the provider, and matched to the first such code in the codes' order whose close equals the fund's value a share to the cent, as sent or with the splits filed after undone, at two of those quarter ends or more, and which moves in step over every one of them it holds a close at; the holding is stored under the key `held`. Read at a single quarter end, as the proposal measured and the operator did not rule, the same reading matched three holdings to their own company and two to another's.

**The funds' holdings before their first public N-PORT are read from their N-Q of 2018-12-31 and their annual report of 2019-03-31** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05: "The first nine months of 2019: do not read them on the 2019-09-30 holdings, since that counts companies added during 2019. Use the 2018-12-31 N-Q and the 2019-03-31 annual report as planned; if they cannot be read, start the 400 and 600 history at 2019-10-01 and state it on every figure." Both are read. The N-Q filed on 2019-03-01, 0001193125-19-059323, is one document of the trust's schedules for dozens of its funds, IJH's naming 400 holdings of common stock and IJR's 602; the annual report filed on 2019-06-07, 0001193125-19-167652, carries in its shareholder report a summary of IJH's schedule, its largest holdings and the rest as other securities, and the complete schedule further on, 400 holdings, and IJR's complete schedule, 602. A schedule names each holding by its company's name with its shares and value and no identifier, so each is matched by name, and kept only where its code traded at the quarter's end (see: A holding matched by name is kept only where its code traded at the quarter's end). The funds filed no N-Q after the one of 2019-03-01 and their first public N-PORT is for the quarter to 2019-09-30, so the quarter to 2019-06-30 holds no snapshot: a name held on 2019-03-31 and 2019-09-30 is a member between them, and one held on only one of them joins or leaves inside those six months. A name the N-Q holds is read as a member from the history's start, which the sweeps read as the year before the first session they score.

**The S&P 400's and 600's members are read each night from their funds' own holdings files** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05, answering 15.0's data checks: "The funds' daily files (Recommended)". The provider's member lists for the S&P 400 and 600 carry tickers it never updated after a rename: against the funds' own holdings as of 2026-10-01, 9 of the 400's and 33 of the 600's differ, GPS where the company trades as GAP, NYCB as FLG and RCII as UPBD among them, and 42 of the 1,003 names the provider listed on 2026-10-04 hold no pulled bar since 2026-09-25, so the night could price none of them. IJH and IJR hold their indices whole, and iShares serves each fund's current holdings as a file of tickers with their GICS sectors, so from 15.1 the night reads each index's members from its fund's file, one request a fund a night at no weight on the provider's allowance, and a file it cannot read keeps the members of the night before and says so on the run page. The S&P 500's members stay the provider's, whose answer carries each span of membership.

**The 400's and 600's provisional picks are computed on the night by the sweep's own code into tables of their own** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05, answering the question put with the sweeps on membership as it stood: "Cards: yes. Compute each index's provisional picks on the night with the sweep's own code over the night's stored year of prices, into tables of their own. Confirm they still feed one trade a stock across every card, the reports taken in turn, Past picks and forward-return scoring, each per index." The plan built each index's cards through the night's own S&P 500 stages widened to the 400 and 600, and two things stood against that once 15.1's night was built: those stages are pinned and replace their session's rows each night, so they cannot run once for each index, and the listings widened to three indices would evaluate the S&P 500's registered candidates on the 400's and 600's members and mix their records. So each night every family's provisional rule on the 400 and on the 600 is read over the year of bars the night stored by the code that family's sweep reads it with, its floors, its profit gate, its strength ranked and its market check read among that index's own members, and the night writes each member's answer under each rule, passing or not, and each pick with its plan into tables of their own: a card lists what its sweep measures at the provisional setting, no S&P 500 rule, stage or pin moves, and the ladder is not widened. The picks feed, each per index, one open trade a stock across every card of every index, the night's six reports taken in turn, Past picks, and the scoring of each pick's forward returns. The S&P 500's listings, candidates and records read none of it, so the S&P 500's listings row is written for every S&P 500 member as before, and every 400 and 600 member's answer stands in the new tables every night.

**Each index runs every family as rules of its own, ranked and benchmarked on that index's members alone** Approved by the operator with phase 15's plan on 2026-10-05, which names it among the decisions the phase writes and has the S&P 400's and 600's families "ranked and judged against their own index", each with "an edge benchmarked on the same plan entered on every member of that index that night". The first test of the S&P 1500 read the three indices as one pool, one strength rank, one benchmark and one breadth, a figure over a mixed population that says nothing about any one of them. So each family on the S&P 400 and on the S&P 600 is a rule of its own: a member's strength is ranked among its own index's members, its market check reads its own index's breadth, and each trade's edge is read against the same plan entered on every member of that index on the night; a sweep of an index reads that index's members alone, on membership as it stood where the store holds its fund's snapshots, and the S&P 500's rules, records and figures read none of it.

**A failure in the S&P 400's or 600's part of the night is caught and named, and the S&P 500's night is built regardless** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05, before 15.1's cards merged: "Any failure in an S&P 400 or 600 step (picks, books, rebalance, reports) is caught, named on the night's run row with its cause, and that index's cards say "not computed tonight". The S&P 500's list, books and reports build regardless." The index families read each index's part of the night in a transaction of its own, after the S&P 500's list and books are drawn in the same step, and before this a failure there, a stored value the book could not read among them, would have failed the whole step and stopped the night before its facts files, its forward returns and its reports. A failure in one index's part now undoes that index's writes of the night and writes its night row naming the failure, its type and its message, where no earlier try of the night computed one; the next index is read; and the stage's row is written with the outcome "not computed" and the failure in its detail, a word the run page draws among the stages a person has to look at and no resume reads as a stop, so pressing for the rest of a night never runs the S&P 500's step again for it. A failure outside any one index's part ends the step with every index not yet read named the same way. The S&P 500's list reads the 400's and 600's open trades and holds nothing back where they cannot be read, its row saying so, and the night's report request passes every turn of an index whose list cannot be read, its line saying so. On the page, that index's Tonight opens on "Not computed tonight" in place of its market line, every card of it saying the same and listing nothing, and its Run page names the failure. The night's own cancellation at its deadline is the one failure passed on, as for every step.

**A rule of the S&P 400's or 600's swing families is registered as the family on its index and evaluated by their step alone** Made with 15.5 on 2026-10-06, which builds the freeze command with no freeze given, the operator having ruled that none is made on the S&P 400 or 600. A freeze of a swing family on the S&P 400 or 600 registers its live rule and up to eight variants at one instant or none, each at settings its index's sweep read: the family's own dials on its sweep's grid, the breakout's four, the drift's four and the pullback's nine, and the levels its second stage read beside them, the dollar volume floor at half, once or twice its figure, the lowest close at $5 or $10, the quality off, the profit gate or the gate and the interest cover, the hold at the family's own cap, at 21 sessions, at 42 and, for the drift alone, at 63, the industry's fall read over 21 sessions, over 63 or not at all, the index's fund against SPY over 126 sessions, over 252 or not at all, the credit fund above its average and rising, the S&P 500's breadth in place of the index's own, and the breakout's nearness to its year's high, that high's recency and a higher volume, and the drift's stop floor, wider window and peers' surprise. A value its sweep did not read is refused naming it, as is a variant stating a rule given before it and a family already standing on its index, since a first freeze is the form built. Each registration names its family's evaluator on its index, whose family is the family on its index, "breakout on the S&P 400", so its correction for luck counts that index's rules of the family alone, at most nine (see: Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family). That evaluator is a family rule's that fires on no S&P 500 member, so the S&P 500's family evaluator, its recorder, its replay and the listings' shadow, each reading its own families by name or leaving a family rule to the family's stage, never read it, and no S&P 500 source or version moves; its version pins its family's sweep files, the files the index night reads it through and every family rule's shared files. On the night the index families' step reads each rule standing registered when the night started at its registration's settings through the night reader's own functions, its levels off the member readings, the switches and the S&P 500's market reading the night stored. Each keeps its own list, five a night in its family's order with one open trade a stock of its own (see: A stock holds one open trade on each rule's list, and it is free the night after its trade ends), and its trades with each one's result, its round trip at the published table and, once its cap's sessions have passed, the benchmark of the same plan on every member of the index that night, its record read after each trade's own round trip (see: A trade's cost comes off the trade and not its benchmark). A family's live rule draws the index's page in the provisional rule's place, so its card's line saying its sweep found no setting that passed goes from the night of the freeze (see: No family on any index is set aside or hidden by a test result without the operator's word). A rule whose evaluator moved since it was registered, or whose settings its sweep's grid no longer holds, is not read, and the night's row names it. Judgement: an evaluator a family on each index rather than one an index, since a family's dials and records are its own, and the S&P 500's family code left unmoved, since its versions name the S&P 500's records.

**A rule of the S&P 400's or 600's sector heavyweights keeps a book of its own in either design, read by the index families' step** Made with 15.5's second pull request on 2026-10-06, the operator having ruled that no family on the S&P 400 or 600 is frozen yet while each index's sweeps found settings of both heavyweights designs meeting the floors. A freeze of the sector heavyweights on the S&P 400 or 600 registers its live rule and up to eight variants at one instant or none, each in either design at settings its sweep read: design (a) at a size cut of 5, 10 or every company, a look-back of 63 sessions, 126 or 251, one leader a sector or two, a beta of at least 1 or none, and sold on no longer leading, under its 200-day average or on either, a sector's return its members' mean; design (b) at a window of 21 sessions or 63, the 5 or 10 strongest leading industries or every one, and one member an industry or two; each at the quality and at the dollar volume floor once or twice its figure, as its sweep read them, a design's own dials nought on the other's registration. Each rule standing registered when the night started keeps a book of its own in tables of its own, as the S&P 500's registered heavyweights rules do (see: Each registered sector heavyweights rule keeps a book of its own beside the page's, its holdings scored in percent against their size cut): its holdings carried every night, one whose stock left the index sold at its last close as a member, one closing under its 200-day average sold there where its rule reads that exit, and on its own first night and the first night of each month a rebalance read by the sweep's own reading at its settings, design (a)'s leaders with their size cut and design (b)'s followers with their sector's members in the index as theirs, each holding the rule would no longer buy sold where it sells on that and each stock it buys and does not hold bought at the night's close. A rebalance waits for a night holding what its design reads, the index's fund's close where design (a) reads a beta, and SPY's closes and the industries' returns the member readings stored for design (b), so a series or a reading the night could not store moves no holding. A holding's result is its growth less one, with its round trip at the published table beside it and never in it, its company valued as the member readings read it on its buy, and a rule's record is each holding's result less its round trip less its size cut's return, in percent, counted in the block of the session it ended on. The index's own book keeps its provisional setting whatever the register holds, and a family's live rule the night read draws the heavyweights' card from its own book in that book's place. Judgement: a new rule rebalancing on its own first night, as the S&P 500's do, rather than waiting for a month's first; and design (b)'s leads read off the industries' returns the member readings store each night rather than computed a second time.

**An S&P 400 or 600 heavyweights holding keeps the lead it was bought on** Made with the 15.1 correction on 2026-10-08, after phase 15's first sign-off review found every S&P 400 and 600 holding's lead over its sector drawn as not read: each book's rebalance read the leads and stored none. The S&P 500's book stores each rebalance's whole reading, so its card draws a holding's lead at the last rebalance on or before the night it is drawn for. The S&P 400's and 600's books store none of their readings, so each holding a design (a) book buys stores on its own row the lead it was bought on, its return over the look-back less its sector's members' mean within the index, and the card draws it with the session it was bought; a lead read again at each later rebalance into that one column would put on an earlier night's card a figure read after that night. A design (b) rule's holding stores none, since that design buys within the industries leading the S&P 500 and reads no lead over a sector, and its card says so; a holding bought before the books stored a lead keeps none, and the card says it was bought before its book stored one. Judgement: the lead at the buy rather than a table of every rebalance's reading, which a later change may add where the newest lead is wanted.

**A breakout is a close above the year's high on heavy volume after its ranges narrowed, sold on a trailing stop with no target** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01 as the second setup family: "close above the 252-session high on volume at least 1.5 times the 50-session average after daily ranges tightened over the prior 20 sessions", with a "trailing stop 2 typical moves below the highest close since the buy, never lowered, no fixed target, 63 sessions". Three readings of those words were put to the operator with the plan and built on their word to start. The store keeps 252 sessions a name, tonight's among them, so the close is read against the highest high of the 251 before it, and a close at that high is not above it. "Tightened" names no number: the mean daily range, the high less the low as a share of the close, of the 20 sessions before tonight is no wider than that of the 20 before them, which over the pulled history lists a breakout on 53 per cent of the nights the market check is open, 1.6 a night, against 31 per cent at 0.85 of it; the family's sweep settles the ceiling. And a breakout names no target, so it has no win, no loss and no break-even: a trade sold at a close under its stop is stored as trailed and one ended by its cap as unresolved, each with what it made and what its plan put at risk, its result is read in multiples of that risk, the figure the sweep counts every trade in, and the family's record is the average of it, its checkpoint test fixed in its sweep's report before its freeze. The names passing are ordered by volume against its average, largest first, since the volume is the part of the rule that says somebody was pushed to pay the new price. The evidence: George and Hwang (2004) on nearness to the 52-week high, Gervais, Kaniel and Mingelgrin (2001) on the premium after unusually high volume, and Dai, Marshall, Nguyen and Visaltanachoti (2021) on trailing stops, which cut downside risk for a lower average return; the 1.5, the reading of tightened and the 2 typical moves are judgement no study sets. Every setting is provisional until the family's freeze (see: A family lists on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint). The swing filter's own removal of its breakout setup stands, and this is the form it returns in: a family of its own with its own record, not a setup inside the filter (see: The swing filter reads pullbacks alone, and a breakout returns only as a registered candidate built from its measured record). The freeze of 2026-10-02 set its numbers at its sweep's proposal, the highest high of the 126 sessions before the close, volume 1.5 times its average, the newer ranges no wider than 0.85 of the older and the stop and the trail 1.5 typical moves beneath (see: The new families freeze at their sweeps' proposals, the breakout's provisional setting and the drift's wider stop registered beside them as variants).

**The earnings drift buys a beat with a strong reaction within five sessions, stopped under the reaction session's low** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01 as the third setup family: "the last report beat its estimate, the reaction session closed up at least 1 typical move on volume at least 1.5 times average, bought within 5 sessions of the reaction, stop below the reaction session's low, target from the next band or 2.5 times the risk, whichever is nearer, 60 sessions". As built: a member passes where the market check is open; its newest print's reaction session is tonight or one of the four sessions before, counted over its own stored sessions; the provider's surprise for that print is above zero; the reaction session's close is up at least one typical move of the session before it, the move as the indicators stored it; that session's volume is at least 1.5 times its fifty-session average; and tonight's close is above the reaction session's low, since a reaction given back to its low is not drifting. It is bought at tonight's close and stopped at the reaction session's low, read on closes as every stop is, and its target is the nearer of the lowest band whose low edge sits two typical moves or more above the close, the distance section 10's plan asks of a target, and 2.5 times the risk above it; where no band is far enough or the night stores no typical move to measure one by, it is the multiple. The names passing are ordered by the surprise, largest first, since the drift grows with it, and over the pulled prints the family passes more than five on 37 per cent of the open nights it passes any, so its order decides its card in a reporting season. A print the calendar carries no actual for yet has no surprise and passes nothing until one is filed, which the five-session window is wide enough to wait for: a print before the open carried its actual on the night of 2026-09-30 and one after the close did not. The evidence: Bernard and Thomas (1989), the highest tenth of surprises beating the lowest by 4.2 per cent over the 60 trading days after, in 41 of 48 quarters, known here from a secondary account; and Brandt, Kishore, Santa-Clara and Venkatachalam (2008), sorting on the announcement's own return making 7.55 per cent a year, more than sorting on the surprise, and 12.5 with both. That evidence is about the largest surprises and a spread of long against short, not about any beat bought alone, and the one typical move, the 1.5, the five sessions and the 2.5 are judgement no study sets; the family's sweep settles them, and every setting is provisional until its freeze (see: A family lists on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint). The reactions it reads are the ones the move annotator already stores (see: Each print's reaction is read from the nightly calendar and the stored bars, and the earnings drift is the one rule that reads it). The freeze of 2026-10-02 set its numbers at its sweep's proposal, a window of 3 sessions, half a typical move up on twice the volume's average and a target at 2.5 times the risk (see: The new families freeze at their sweeps' proposals, the breakout's provisional setting and the drift's wider stop registered beside them as variants).

**A repeat listing made before the rule reached the filter is marked and counted once** Until the freeze the filter still lists a stock whose earlier trade is open, as it listed BDX on 2026-09-29. On tonight's page the stock stays on the list where the filter put it, marked as listed again while the trade from its earlier night is open, and "Still open" names it with where the price stands against that trade's stop and target, so the page never disagrees with what the night stored or with Past picks. On Past picks and a name's "On the list before" the repeat is drawn as a row with its mark and left out of every count and share, with a line saying how many are drawn and not counted, so one move is counted once. The operator chose both on 2026-09-30: over a page that applied the rule early, which would have drawn a list different from the stored one until the freeze, and over counting every listing, which counts one move twice. On the stored nights one listing of seven is a repeat, BDX of 2026-09-29, and the card counts six trades where it counted seven (see: A stock holds one open trade on each rule's list, and it is free the night after its trade ends).

**Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night by rules the measured split settled, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone** The operator asked on 2026-09-27 for a business state beside each name on tonight's list and a summary of what the numbers say at the head of the report's numbers, and approved the plan with five corrections. Every night, after the swing readings and before the listings, the fundamental reader works out four readings for every member, by arithmetic and no model, from the quarters its newest fetch before that night stored, so a night run again later never reads a quarter from its future. The trajectory is sales against the same quarter a year earlier and the operating margin against a year earlier, over the two newest quarters. The record is the actual earnings a share against the analysts' estimate over the eight newest quarters carrying both, an actual within a cent or 1% of the estimate, whichever is larger, reading as met, stated where four or more count; a quarter with no actual has not reported and is never read as met. Earnings quality is operating cash flow over net income, summed over the four newest quarters: below 1.0 the cash fell short of the reported profit and earnings ran ahead of cash, 1.0 to 2.0 is in line, and above 2.0 they were more than backed by it, and a total at or below nought is named a loss and not compared. The valuation position is tonight's multiple placed in the thirds of the range of the quarters' own multiples, stated where eight or more carry one (see: Reported quarters are stored per fetch, so every quarter a reading reads shares one fetch's per-share basis). The state is one word read from the trajectory alone: improving where sales stood above a year earlier and the margin wider in both quarters, deteriorating where sales stood below and the margin narrower in both, steady otherwise, not enough quarters where the trajectory cannot be read, and no fundamentals yet where no quarter is stored. A year earlier, so the seasons fall out; the operating margin, because it is the part of profit the business itself runs, before interest, tax and one-off items; two quarters, so one quarter's one-off cannot move the word; and both signals, so the word says something only where sales and margin agree. The record, the quality and the valuation are drawn beside the state and never move it, so improving can stand beside earnings that ran ahead of cash, and the heading says so in its own words: "What the numbers say: {state}, read from sales and operating margin alone". The report's numbers open with that heading and one sentence per reading, written by code from the fixed patterns section 4 places, each describing the business and none advising a trade (see: Code owns every number). The state orders tonight's list and gates nothing: no gate reads it, a member reading none is judged and listed like any other, and absent is never a failure (see: Tonight's list is the swing filter's with improving businesses drawn first, and an evening is listed and ordered by the rule that listed it). The cent is the smallest gap a figure filed to the cent can show, and the provider restates per-share figures to the cent after a split, so a restated figure's cent is a larger share of it: over NFLX's committed capture an exact comparison read 6 beats in eight quarters, where the tolerance reads 2 beats, 4 meets and 2 misses, its quarter to 2025-06-30 reading 0.72 against 0.71 after its 10:1 split. The same capture reads sales 13.4% above a year earlier in the quarter to 2026-06-30 and 16.2% in the quarter before, the margin 33.4% against 34.1% a year earlier and so narrower in the newest quarter and wider in the one before, which is steady, and operating cash flow 0.87 of net income, below the cash its profit reported. No study sets the tolerance, the quality's cut points or the state rule. Each was proposed in section 17 and settled by the operator on 2026-09-27 from the members' measured split, once every member held quarters: over all 503, worked by the shipped readings at 2026-09-25's close, the states read improving 172, steady 317 and deteriorating 14, and either signal with the other not pointing the opposite way read 173 and 14, since two signals read to six places are almost never level, so the state keeps both signals. The record over 3,994 quarters read 2,905 beats, 553 meets and 536 misses with the tolerance and 3,174, 165 and 655 without it, so the tolerance stands. Operating cash flow runs above net income for most companies, depreciation being added back, so the ratio's median over the 477 compared was 1.51 and cut points either side of one put 350 in the upper band; at 1.0 and 2.0 the bands read 59, 284 and 134, the lower naming a company whose cash fell short of its reported profit, and the operator moved them there. The ruling before these readings, that no reading of the fundamentals fires a reason, gates a tranche or draws a panel, gave way because its reason was the path rather than the merit of the readings: the fundamentals were fetched only when a name was opened, so a reading of them covered the names the operator happened to open. The quarters are now fetched for every member on the night after it reports (see: A member's reported quarters are fetched on the night after it reports, and asked for again on the five nights after and weekly after that until the quarter is posted), so every member is read by the same rule on the same night, and the state reaches no reason, gate or plan: it orders the list and draws the summary.

Supersedes **Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but earnings quality's cut points, which moved from 0.8 and 1.2 to 1.0 and 2.0, and its three proposed values, which are now settled.

**The dated screens open from the swing filter's first night, and no evening before it is drawn** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, reading the evening of 2026-09-23 drawn with the 340 names its reasons fired: "Lets start from 24th as real night", with the evenings before it made unavailable on tonight's page and on the run page. On a store the swing filter has listed, both screens open from the first night it listed: a date before it draws one line saying the record starts on that night, with a link opening it, and the calendar on either screen holds the nights from that one on. A store the filter never listed draws every evening as before. Nothing a night wrote is deleted. Every night's row for every member stays, which the reasons' records, each name's listing history and the scored setups are read over, and the name page's earlier evenings and listing history are unchanged. The swing filter's replayed answers for the sessions before its first night were written by a command and not by a night; they are read by the trigger's arrival alone, drawn by no page, and removed on the operator's command once no night can read them (see: The swing filter's results are replayed for the sessions before its first stored night for the trigger's arrival alone, and removed once no night can read them). The evenings before the first night were listed by a rule the list no longer uses, and drawn beside the filter's they read as the filter's own history (see: Tonight's list is the swing filter's with improving businesses drawn first, and an evening is listed and ordered by the rule that listed it).

Supersedes **The dated screens open from the swing filter's first night, and an evening before it is not drawn**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that the replayed answers stay in the store.

**A pick's card advises on the trade and removes no pick, and code computes every figure on it** Ruled by the operator with phase 16's brief of 2026-10-05: "Rules choose the stocks. The card is computed by code from stored data; no model output enters it. The AI research report is unchanged. Every reading is as it stood that night, and every figure names its index. The card advises and never removes a pick from a list." The card gathers, beside each pick, what a decision on it needs: a checklist of six lines, each a tick or a warning with its reason in words; the record of the rule that picked it; the plan in the operator's money; what could hit the trade before it ends; and the operator's own record of the trades taken. A warning is advice and nothing more: no list, pick, order, queue, report request or record reads a card or a trade the operator took, so a card can be wrong without moving a pick, and whether a warning is worth acting on is the operator's judgement and never a gate's (see: Rules choose the stocks, and the AI writes the reports). Every figure on it is computed by code, read from the store as it stood on the night and naming its index, but for the plan in the operator's money, which reads the account settings the operator keeps outside the store, and none is asked of a model (see: Code owns every number). Its values sit in one settings block with defaults in code, each stated on the card in its own words, and each card stores the values it was read with, so a value changed later never rewrites an earlier card. Its first line is a tick on every card, since a pick passed every gate to be listed, and it is drawn as words, "passed every gate", rather than as a signal it cannot give.

## Presentation

**The momentum panel is context a reader weighs, and nothing computes with it** The panel draws the relative strength index and the moving average convergence readings beneath the chart with their neutral rules, and no component decides anything from them: no reason, no band, no plan, no gate. It is the only part of the technical half with no other purpose, and it keeps that one. Its neutral rules, fifty on the index and zero on the histogram, are reading conventions the page draws rather than thresholds any component applies, so they are not constants a check pins or figures a measurement settles. The one reader outside the name page is the facts file, which copies every indicator the engine wrote, so prose may state a reading and section 5's convention for it and may never present one as a signal or a reason to act. What is given up is that no momentum state can fire a reason or gate a tranche; giving the panel a job later is a candidate condition like any other, registered before it is scored and scored in shadow before it is shown. Ruled at 8.0.

**One application, not a file per report** The nightly run stores data and renders nothing. Reports are rendered from stored data when read, so a layout change costs nothing and no file on disk goes stale.
