**A press writes a request and starts the worker's drain as a process of its own, and every pass waits for the off-peak hours** A press on a screen writes one row to the request store, as it did, and then starts the worker's `drain` verb as a process of its own and returns, so the read surface still writes the ask and never the research. The operator ruled on 2026-09-23, "I dont want nightly script to do it. I want the queue to be processed as soon as i queue them", after their first request waited from 19:01 to 19:09 UTC for a drain typed by hand; offered a watcher that finds a request, a task that looks every five minutes and the page starting it on the press, they chose the page. Every reason the superseded entry gave for a queue survives, because the request is still written first: the queue screen lists it, a request nobody has claimed can still be taken out, and a press that started nothing is told apart from one whose pass died, since the row says which. The surface holds no reference to the worker and writes nothing a pass writes: it starts the worker's own verb by path, as the runbook shows it run by hand, with the store it reads named in the environment. The drain runs from a copy of the worker's build output made for it, so a drain still writing when the night builds that output again holds none of it and cannot stop the night, and a second drain started while one runs takes only what the first has not claimed, since a claim is one write only one drain can make. A drain started inside a peak window, or reaching one between passes, waits for the pricing's first off-peak instant before its next pass, on the operator's ruling of the same evening, "off peak hours it is for everything" (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC). What is given up is that a press now sets a paid pass going with no second step. Supersedes **A request the page writes and the worker drains is what starts a pass, and the read surface writes the ask and never the research**, moved below with its reasoning.

**A request a drain left being written is put back as outstanding by the next drain, and a pass that fails settles its request as refused** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03, after a drain run by hand claimed QCOM's request and ended on an error its pass did not catch, which left the request being written with no drain to finish it: no drain takes a request being written, and a name holding one is asked for neither by a press nor by the night. One drain runs at a time, under its lock, so a request being written when a drain takes the lock was left by a drain that ended before its pass did; the drain puts every such request back as outstanding before it claims, and takes it in its turn. A pass that ends on an error it wrote no run for is settled as refused with the error, and the drain goes on to the next, so only a drain whose process was stopped outright leaves one, and putting it back cannot loop on a pass that fails every time. Settling a left request as refused was the alternative, rejected because the request was never answered and a press would be needed to ask again. (see: A press writes a request and starts the worker's drain as a process of its own, and every pass waits for the off-peak hours)

**A drain that stops on an error writes a row of its own, and the queue page states it until a pass starts after it** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03, joining phase 14 as a 9.2 correction from the phases 12 and 13 sign-off's carried items: the night's drain of 2026-10-02 ended at 00:26:16 on "database is locked" and wrote no row any page reads, so it was found in the machine's application log and the queue page read QCOM's request as starting now. The two causes known then were removed, and the next drain puts back a request a drain left being written, but a drain ending on any other error still left nothing on a page until another drain ran. The drain verb runs its put-back and its queue inside a guard that catches an error escaping either, writes one run log row under a run of its own named for the instant the drain started, the stage `drain`, the outcome `failed` and the error's type and words with no path a machine roots, and exits failing. The queue page states the newest such row, the time the drain started and the error, while no pass has started after it, since a pass started after it is a later drain at work; the run page's checklist names it on the night its row fell on. Restarting the drain on the error was the alternative, rejected because an error the drain does not understand would repeat, and a press or the next night already starts the next drain. (see: A request a drain left being written is put back as outstanding by the next drain, and a pass that fails settles its request as refused)

**A pass is started only by a request carrying the name page's own header** The control spends money, and the read surface answers anything that can reach the machine's address, which includes a page on any other site a browser on this machine opens: a form that page submits to the control's address arrives looking like a press. So the page's own script sends the press with a header of its own, and the surface refuses a request without it before it reads the name. A form submitted from another site cannot carry a header, and a script on another site that tries to add one makes the browser ask the surface first whether that origin may send it, which the surface never answers, so the request is not sent. The spend cap still bounds what any pass can cost, and that is the reason this is a refusal at the door rather than a matter left to the cap: a pass the cap allows is still a pass nobody asked for. A token issued with the page was the alternative, and it defends against the same request with state the surface would have to keep, where the header needs none. (see: The name page's control starts the worker's research verb, and the read API writes nothing it starts) (see: The spend cap is a stop, not an allowance)

**A regenerated report is written whole by the paid model from the company's figures as they stand on the day it runs, once a name a day** The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, told that a regenerate wrote from the company's figures as they were first fetched: "regenerate report should do a full paid report with current date. There is no point in regenerating a report from 30 days ago using stale data. However, the condition is that it regenerates only once per day". Asked how the figures that move with the price should be made current where the company has filed nothing new, they chose a fresh copy each fetch. A name whose research stands or has gone stale, and for which no pass has run on the day in New York the page is read on, is offered Regenerate Report beside its other controls, the cost of research so far stated before it is pressed. A press is a request carrying the ask, and the drain answers it with a pass in which the paid model writes every section, the local lane's among them, whatever lane the request names, and every section is written again rather than only what is missing or stale. Before it writes, the pass fetches the company's figures again whatever the store holds. A filing made since is stored as a row of its own, as every filing is (see: Fundamentals are stored with the filing date they came from). The parts that are as of the fetch rather than as of a filing, the earnings bases, the multiples, the market value, the analysts' ratings, the dividend and the next report, are stored as that fetch's own copy, since a filing's row is never updated and they sit on the newest one as they stood when it was first fetched. The facts file and the numbers read the newest copy in place of the newest row's, and the night's facts file is assembled again after the fetch, so every figure the prose may state is that day's. The prices, the bands and the plan are the newest night's, because the night is what fetches bars. A regenerate runs once a name a day: it is offered only where no pass for the name has run that day, and a request the drain reaches for a name a pass already wrote that day starts nothing, fetches nothing and says why on its row, so a second press does not pay for the same report twice. It is the one ask held to the day: the page's rewrite of what went stale and its option to have the paid model write the local lane's sections still start a pass whatever ran that day (see: A name opened again on the day its research pass ran starts no second pass unless the page asks for one). A fetch weighs ten of the provider's 100,000 daily calls. It is the operator's own ask, which the rule on expiry names beside the three triggers (see: Nothing expires on a timer), and the request carries it because the drain answers a press later and from the row alone. Supersedes **A report is regenerated whole on the operator's ask once the day it was written has passed**, moved below with its reasoning.

**A regenerate on the day a report was written writes only the sections that report left out, and a report the connection cut is not the day's** The day's report, which a second plain open and a second regenerate on the same day are both held to, is a pass that ran to the end, and a pass the cap paused or the model did not answer never counted as one. Two rulings of 2026-09-30 narrow what that holds a regenerate to. First, a pass the model answered for most of its calls and could not be reached for on one or more of the report's own calls counted as the day's report, because its outcome read as written; that day the connection to the provider was cut on every answer taking longer than a minute (see: A paid model on the OpenAI format is asked to stream its answer, because a connection silent for a minute is cut), BDX, MO and IT each lost the two cases, BDX its risks as well, and the operator chose to lift the rule for the three. It is lifted by what the three share rather than by their names: a pass whose run log holds a call of the report's own that could not be reached is not the day's report, so a regenerate or a plain open the same day runs. A call a trial or a review made is no part of the report, and one that could not be reached leaves the pass the day's. Second, regenerated again, the three were each left with sections the claim checker refused at their one retry, and the operator ruled that a regenerate fill those too: on the day a report was written, a regenerate writes the sections that report left out, and none it accepted, so the second pays only for what the first did not buy, and where the report left nothing out a regenerate starts nothing, as the once-a-day rule has it. A plain open the same day still starts nothing where the day's report ran to the end, since it asks for nothing the report did not. (see: A regenerated report is written whole by the paid model from the company's figures as they stand on the day it runs, once a name a day) (see: A name opened again on the day its research pass ran starts no second pass unless the page asks for one) (see: A retry names each thing the check refused, and a section refused on its third retry is left out)

**The watch list is the operator's own, up to twenty names of the index, on a page of its own** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, asking who makes the watch list, which section 15.7 drew above tonight's list and no store could fill: "I think it would be a good idea to have a watchlist section where users can add stocks from sp500", then "I am thinking watchlist should be an entire new page", holding up to twenty. The watch list is a store of its own, a name and the instant it was added, which the read surface writes on the operator's press alone, from the watch list page or beside a name on its own page, each press refused without the page's own header as every press is (see: A pass is started only by a request carrying the name page's own header). A name is added only while it is a member of the index and while fewer than twenty are held, and a refused press writes nothing and says why. The page draws each name for the newest night from that night's own rows, whether or not the list holds it, with what the swing filter said of it, and tonight's page draws one line counting the names with a link to it. It decides nothing: the night, the list, the queue, the report the night asks for and every record read no row of it, so a name watched is a name followed and never a name promoted.

**A single report can still be exported as a self-contained file** Handing one report to someone outside the tool is a stated use, and the exporter renders through the same code path.

**A screen reads and renders, and computes only the plan in the operator's money and a pick's open trades in its sector** No screen calls a model, makes a network request, or works out a level, a stop or a rate. Every number on every screen is already in the store, but for two a pick's card draws: its plan in the operator's money, being the shares, the dollars at risk, the position's value and its share of the account and the round trip in dollars, and its sixth line, the operator's open taken trades in the stock's sector. Neither can be in the store. The account's size, its risk a trade and its cap live in a file of their own that no store row may hold, and shares times the stop's distance gives the risk in dollars back, so a stored plan would be the account stored; and the taken trades change with every press. So each is worked by one function in the core where the card is drawn, from the plan, the round trip a share and the sector the night stored, and no screen works out either a second way. A rendering layer that computes is a second implementation of the same arithmetic, and two implementations of one rule disagree eventually; these two have one implementation each, an export draws neither, and a screen can still be rebuilt or restyled with no chance of moving any other number. Ruled with the phase 16 plan on 2026-10-05, the evaluation's third finding.

**Marks are defined once and every screen draws from that list** Seven named marks, each a function from stored values to an SVG string, with no script needed to render one. Nothing on any screen is a one-off drawing. That is what lets the exported single report carry the same pictures as the application, from the same data through the same code, rather than two renderers drifting apart.

**Support and resistance own two hues and nothing else uses them** Green is a level below the price and orange is one above it, on every screen and in every mark. A day's price change in particular does not get them, because a green day beside a green support band is one colour meaning two things. Direction is carried by the sign on the number and by which side of a zero rule a bar sits on, and every band carries its role in words as well, so hue is never the only channel.

**Not yet measured is drawn as a dashed outline, never as a pale value** Anywhere a figure has not earned a number, the space carries its count against its minimum inside a dashed outline. A pale number reads as a small one, and the distinction between a small value and an absent one is the difference between a screen doing its job and a screen with no evidence.

**Every figure carries a plain-language key** A short paragraph beneath each picture explaining how to read it is the reason someone outside the domain can follow it.

**The plan figure is one vertical price column with the current price marked in it** Everything above the marker is a sale and everything below is a purchase, which is legible without reading a caption.

**Distances are stated as typical days' moves** "Two typical days away" is understood immediately where an ATR multiple is not.

**Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section when it was written rather than which model wrote it** Supersedes **Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when**, moved below with its reasoning. Computed tonight, fundamentals as of a filing date, research as of the date it was written, and the key under each figure as of the close it explains. The model that wrote a section stays in the store and on the element the section is drawn in, and the page's words do not name it: the operator ruled on 2026-09-18 that no model's name is shown on a page, and on 2026-09-19 that a page says only when a section was written. A section is read for what it says, and which model wrote it is a setting of the lanes rather than a fact about the company; where a local draft is not good enough, the page's option to have the paid model write it again is what serves the reader.

**A written section is drawn a claim to a row, and the two cases and the risks are asked for in the parts the page draws** Supersedes **A written section is broken into parts only where its own prose says where each part ends**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but what it drew where the prose states no part. The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, reading HUM's two cases drawn as one block: "I need this as a bulleted point or a rows separated by lines like in the second image for both bull and bear. I dont need this to be so condensed", then "This should be an overall template change" and "Even the risk section needs that change", the second image being a table of risks each beside what would confirm it. The two cases and the risks are drawn a claim to a row: a row is a sentence as the claim checker reads it, which is the unit the checker accepted and ends on the marker of the document it rests on, each paragraph read on its own so a paragraph's end is a row's, and the rows joined back up are the section as it was written. The two cases carry their labels where the prose opens on the bull case, the case against starting at the first sentence opening on the bear case, in whichever paragraph that sentence stands, and running to the end; where the prose opens on neither, the rows carry no label, since a boundary the page guessed at would put one case's words under the other's. The risks are a table of two columns, the risk and what would confirm it, where every part the prose states opens its confirmation in the words the writer is asked for; a list, a risk to a row with its confirmation set beneath where it has one, where the parts are stated and a confirmation is not; and a claim to a row where the prose states no part. The writer is asked for those shapes: each case a paragraph opening on its own name with a point to a sentence, and each risk a paragraph opening on its ordinal and followed by a sentence opening "That risk would be confirmed by". A section written before the ask, or by a writer that answers in another shape, is still drawn a claim to a row. Measured on 2026-09-26 over the four names the operator's store holds research for: HUM's two cases were one paragraph whose case against opened mid-way, drawn whole under the superseded entry and now eight rows for and seven against, and the risks of HUM, RTX and GM were one paragraph each naming no ordinal, now fourteen, fourteen and twenty rows. Ordering the risks by how severe each one is is still not done: a severity is a judgement the writer states or nothing does. (see: Code owns every number) (see: A figure is drawn at the places it is read at, and its element carries the stored value whole)

**A refused draft's own words are kept on the row and drawn on the evidence page, and never on the name page** A section the claim checker refused twice is stored with the reason, which carries one part per refused sentence: the rule that refused it, and the offending text after it. For most rules that text is something the checker extracted, a figure, a date, a window or a citation marker, and it is the most useful thing on the line. For two of them, a sentence naming no document and a cause naming the session of no move the facts file holds, it is the draft's own sentence, and on the name page it is not drawn: a reader there is asking what is missing and why, and the paragraph a model wrote and the checker threw away answers neither. Repeats are collected under their rule rather than drawn again, since one rule refusing six sentences is one thing wrong six times. The row keeps the whole reason and the run page draws it whole, that being the evidence page (see: Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section when it was written rather than which model wrote it). Measured on 2026-09-19 over the nine sections then reaching a page: eight were unchanged by this and the ninth fell from 407 characters to 147.

**Every region is a card that states where its figures came from and how to read them** Each region of a screen is drawn as a card on one paper palette. A region computed from the nightly store is ruled across its top in slate and stamped with the night its figures are from. A section written by research or taken from a filing is ruled in plum-grey with the day it was written or filed in a column at its left, because a filed figure or a written paragraph can be weeks older than the chart beside it and a reader has to see that before reading it. Every picture has a key beneath it that says how to read it and closes on what to take from it. The app and the exported report carry one stylesheet, which opens with the rule each colour token is held to, and both palettes are designed rather than one inverted, the choice stamped on the root element and remembered between visits with the machine's preference as the default. A card holds a region's markup as the marks and the regions write it and adds words and layout only, so a card can be restyled without a figure in it moving. Adopted on 2026-09-19 from the design the operator approved. (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing) (see: Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section when it was written rather than which model wrote it) (see: Every figure carries a plain-language key)

**Tonight's list breaks a tie in fired count by the plan's reward to risk, and a row with none is drawn after every row with one and says why** The operator ruled on 2026-09-22 that tonight's list orders by how many reasons fired, then by the reward to risk the night's own plan computes from its first tranche, higher first, then by ticker in code-point order. A row whose plan computes none, having no tranche, no stop on its first tranche or no traded exit, is drawn after every row with one in its fired-count group and states why in the plan's own words; on the four nights 2026-09-16 to 09-21 that was 593 of the 1,463 fired rows, read off each row's own night's plan. The ratio is read off the plan the listing kept that night and worked by the ladder's own arithmetic, rounded as the ladder stores it, so an earlier night is ordered by that night's plans and the figure a row draws is the figure the night's plan holds. Reward to risk is a fact about the chart and not a probability of anything, so the rule that no screen shows a number beside a ticker that could be read as its probability of rising stands, and no screen orders names by anything read off the company. The order of the whole list is measured rather than assumed (see: The order tonight's list is drawn in is compared against the order it replaces, declared before any record is read). Research does not settle which tiebreak is better: Chung and Bellotti (2021) find that levels with more prior bounces are more likely to bounce again, decaying with time, while Osler (2000) found published strength ratings bore no consistent relation to bounces. Supersedes **Tonight's list orders by the reasons that fired and then band strength, which are facts about the chart**, moved below with its reasoning.

**The masthead carries the last stored close and the session it is from** The design's masthead shows a name's price, and no screen fetches, so the price is the last close the store holds, stated as of the close of the session it closed on, beside its change on the day off the two newest stored closes and the company's name, sector and industry as the membership row holds them. A live quote would be a request a screen made and a figure the store never held (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing). Ruled on 2026-09-19.

**The sizing arithmetic states the risk a share carries and holds no account of the reader's** The approved design drew the sizing from an account and a risk per plan labelled as the reader's settings, with a share count worked from them. The store holds neither, and a plan places a position and never sizes one (see: The plan places a position and never sizes one), so the page states the risk a share carries from the entry to the stop and says the budget divided by it is the reader's, and draws no account, no budget and no share count. That is what keeps the name page's refusal true, that it does not say how much to buy. Holding the reader's settings would be a store the read surface writes, and it writes none. Ruled on 2026-09-19.

**A reason's record is drawn in two states, and a bare number beside a ticker is a prohibition rather than a third** Section 15.11's table has four rows, and the last of them, never shown, is not a state a record can be in: it is what no screen draws. So a record is drawn in one of two states, a dashed outline carrying its count against the minimum, or the share, the number resolved and the break-even together, and its unresolved setups are the track's own segment in either. Ruled on 2026-09-19. (see: A reason's record is displayed, beside the reason and never beside the name) (see: Not yet measured is drawn as a dashed outline, never as a pale value)

**Tonight's reason totals are counts, and a reason's record is the run page's** Section 15.7 drew tonight's reason totals with the reason track, whose segments are outcomes, and a setup listed tonight has none yet, so the track drew one dashed segment per reason and said nothing a count would not. Tonight's totals are each reason's count of tonight's fired names, drawn in the track's own geometry as a bar out of the fired count with the count written on it. How a reason's setups have ended is its record on the run page, read against the base rate. Ruled on 2026-09-19.

**Selecting a row draws its plan beneath the list and is no navigation** Section 15.7 draws the selected name's plan and levels so that checking a plan needs no navigation. At the page's width the region cannot sit beside twenty rows, so selecting a row, anywhere on it but its links, puts the name in the hash, draws its plan beneath the list on the same page and brings it into view, and back returns to the row. No second screen is opened and the list is not left, which is what the rule asks. Ruled on 2026-09-19.

**A figure is drawn at the places it is read at, and its element carries the stored value whole** A screen draws a stored figure rounded to the places a reader reads it at: a price, a ratio and an amount per share to two places, an amount of money and a count of shares in its scale, as $96.2B and 190.3M, a fraction as a percentage to one place, as 75.0%, and a multiple to one place. The element a figure is drawn in carries the stored value whole in a data attribute, and the checks read that, so every figure on a screen traces to a value the store holds and no rounding becomes a value of its own. Rounding a figure to be read is not the arithmetic a screen is barred from, since nothing is worked out that the store does not hold, and the places are the ones the claim checker already reads prose at, where $109.4 billion is a rounding of 109417000000 (see: Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file). A number inside a sentence the nightly store wrote, an event setup's trigger among them, is read the same way. Prose a model wrote is drawn as the claim checker accepted it, because a page that rewrote its figures would draw a sentence nobody checked. The redesign's plan asked for this and the design correction drew it for the fact strip and the marks alone, so the numbers section printed revenue as 96221000000.00 and margins as 0.749753 until the operator found it on 2026-09-19. (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing) (see: Code owns every number)

**The numbers open on a snapshot of the newest filing with every other filed figure folded beneath it, and the report names no provider** The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, reading HUM's numbers: "If this is supposed to be showing the fundamentals then i think it's better to have a concise snapshot which highlights the important parts like the 2nd image. I want it more readable", then of the segment table and its source line: "i dont want EODHD to be mentioned and this column is repeatative, it needs to be grouped". The section opens on twelve figures of the newest filing, a figure to a row in two columns: the quarter's revenue and net income each with its change on a year earlier as the fetcher computed it from the two filings it names, earnings per share against the estimate, the next report with its consensus, both margins, the market value, the dividend with its yield as the provider states it, both price multiples each with the earnings it is struck on, cash and net debt. The five quarters' table follows under its own heading. Management's passage is folded shut, and opened is a list where the release marks its points with bullets, the passage cut at its own marks and never edited. The balance sheet, the valuation, the dividend and the segments are folded beneath under one heading, each table under its own. The segment table states each group's label once over the lines beneath it, grouped by the table's own groups since two can share a label, and a line the filing sets with a colon and no figure is drawn as the heading it is rather than as a figure not filed. The analysts' target price is not among the twelve, for the reason the facts file carries none (see: The fundamentals row carries the analysts' ratings the provider files, on the newest filing alone). No data provider is named in the report's words: which provider a part came from stays in the store's source column and on the element, as the store's record of where a figure came from rather than something the reader acts on, as the model that wrote a section stays on its element (see: Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section when it was written rather than which model wrote it). (see: A figure is drawn at the places it is read at, and its element carries the stored value whole) (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing)

**A name's page states no listing history, since what an earlier evening's list said is not what the page is read for** Supersedes **A name's listing history states what followed each evening it was listed and forms no rate for the name**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands for any surface that states an evening's result but the card it drew. The operator ruled on 2026-09-26, reading HUM's history: "i dont think this section is doing anything for the reader. We know HUM has never been on the tonight list except on 25th yet we are saying the nights it has been on the list. Overall, this section says entry zones for past dates which are of no use as a reader reading it today", and chose to remove it. Before the switch night an evening's list was every name a reason fired for, 65 to 86 per cent of the index, of which the page drew twenty, so HUM read as listed on nine evenings no page ever showed it on. The name page draws neither the strip nor the evenings, and its contents name no such region. The listings and their forward returns stay stored and are read where a reason's record is measured, since a record is a reason's across every name it fired on and never a name's (see: A reason's record is displayed, beside the reason and never beside the name). The page for an earlier night stays, and the card's evenings were the one place that linked to it, so it is reached by its own address and its walk. (see: A name's page for an earlier night draws what the store held that night and nothing it learned after)

**A pass the page starts is watched until it ends and the page redraws as each section lands** Section 15.12's last step says a pass asked for from the page shows progress and its sections stream in as they are checked and stored. A pass writes a row for each component as it works and its own row last, and the prose writer stores each section as it writes it, so what a pass has done is already in the store while it runs and needs no new write to be watched. The page asks a read address every three seconds for where the pass stands and draws the answer beside the control, and each time one more section has landed it asks for the page again and redraws it in place, keeping the reader where they were. The reply to the press carries the instant the surface took before starting the pass, and the page hands that back with every ask, so the rows read are that pass's and not an earlier one's; a run carrying no rows yet is one that has started, and a run carrying the pass's own row is one that has ended, at which point the page stops asking. It stops in three other cases: the reader leaves the page, the address answers with anything but an answer, and ten minutes pass, which is above the longest pass the run log has recorded. The shape of a pass's run name is stated once, where the worker that writes it and the surface that reads it can both reach it, because the pass's own row, which is the only row carrying the name it was for, is written last. Two alternatives were rejected. Holding the connection open and pushing each section down it would make the read surface keep state per reader and would leave a pass unwatchable the moment the page was reloaded. Writing a progress row from the surface would make the read surface a writer, which it is not (see: The name page's control starts the worker's research verb, and the read API writes nothing it starts). (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing)

**A name's page for an earlier night draws what the store held that night and nothing it learned after** Supersedes **A name's page for an earlier night is what the store held that night**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the listing history it drew beside that night's figures, which the name page no longer draws (see: A name's page states no listing history, since what an earlier evening's list said is not what the page is read for). Section 15.9 gives the name page a second route carrying a date, and what that page draws is the evening's own figures: every read it makes is bounded by that night, so the bars stop there, the bands, the plan and the profile are the ones that night computed, the filings are those filed by then, and a written section is the newest version accepted on or before it. The evening drawn is the newest the listings hold on or before the date asked for, so a Saturday, a holiday and a night that never ran each answer with the evening before rather than with nothing, and the page says which evening it drew and links to tonight's. It offers no control and states no spend: a research pass writes about the company now, not about that evening, and a staleness verdict is a statement about today. How a setup resolved is something the store learned after that night, and the page states none. The walk stays in the evening, each neighbour linked on the same date, so a pass through one evening's list is one pass. Bounding the reads rather than replaying the rules is what keeps the page a read: replaying would be a second implementation of the arithmetic and would produce figures the store never held (see: A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing).

**A screen is read at the width of the screen it is read on** The column every screen is drawn in is the width of the screen, up to a ceiling that is the widest picture a screen draws with the card's own padding and the page's gutter around it. Above that ceiling a line of prose runs past what an eye follows; below it the column is the screen's own width less the gutter, so a narrow screen is filled rather than shown a page laid out for a wide one. A table cannot be narrowed past its columns, so every table a screen draws is read in a box of its own that scrolls sideways rather than moving the page. A picture can be narrowed, and is: the chart and the volume profile beside it are drawn at a size of their own, which is what the ceiling holds, and below the ceiling the pair is scaled down together, each keeping its own ratio and both anchored at the top, so a price is at one height in both at every width. Anchoring them in boxes of a fixed height instead put a price fourteen points higher in the profile than in the chart at a column of 974, which is the fault this rule is written against. A long run of characters with nowhere to break, a document's address among them, breaks instead of pushing the page. The operator found both halves on 2026-09-19: the column was fixed at a width narrower than the screen it was read on, and on a narrow screen tonight's reason totals and the run page's records ran off the right-hand edge with a row of the page behind them. Drawing the chart smaller than the space it is read in was the alternative and was what the page did, at seven tenths of its size, so a year of candles was read at seven tenths on every screen whatever the screen could show. (see: Every region is a card that states where its figures came from and how to read them) (see: Marks are defined once and every screen draws from that list)

**A researched name is one holding an accepted section besides the key under each figure** The researched list, the line under a researched name in the universe table and the masthead search's labels count a name as researched when it holds a section the claim checker accepted other than the key under each figure, dated by the newest such section. The key does not count because the overnight queue writes it again each night for every name (see: The key under each figure is dated by the night whose figures it explains, written for every name each night, and drawn only beside that night's figures), and on the operator's store on 2026-09-19 it stood for 502 names where one name, NVDA, held research. Counting research passes off the run log was the other form, and it would call a name researched on a pass that wrote nothing. Every name is also found from a search box in the masthead of every screen, by its ticker or its company's name, which the operator asked for on 2026-09-19 because a researched name could be reached only by knowing its ticker or finding it in the universe table.

**The queue page states when each request will be written** For every request it lists, the queue page states when its pass will start or started and when it is expected to end or ended, in New York's time with the offset named and UTC beside it. A request asked in a peak window says the instant the window ends; one behind other requests says its estimated start, the median duration of the passes the store holds, stated with how many passes that is over, and a store holding none says it cannot estimate rather than guessing. The operator asked on 2026-09-23 that a reader know fully when a report will be generated, after their first request sat in the queue with no screen saying whether anything would write it.

**A name's group is its industry where at least five other members share it on the session, and its sector otherwise, and every surface that uses it says which and how many** One rule, declared once and used by the move table and the peers table, so the two never disagree about who a name's group is. Five is the operator's proposal of 2026-09-23: an industry of fewer members makes a median of a name or two, which reads as a comparison without being one, so a smaller industry gives way to its sector. The name itself is never in its own group, a group holding nobody says so rather than drawing an empty median, and the floor is a constant section 17 pins from 11.5.

**A large move is shown beside its group's median move over the same sessions** The median of the members' moves from the close before the move's first session to the close of its last session, with members missing either close left out and counted. A median needs no market capitalisation, which the store does not hold nightly, and it is the same shape as the night's own median the volume-against-the-night candidate compares a name with. The move annotator writes it on the move row it already owns, so a move and the median it is read against are one row and cannot drift apart.

**Peers are shown by price alone, ten at most with the name's industry first and then the members whose daily moves followed it most closely** The operator asked on 2026-09-27, reading AAPL's page, for the table to "just list top10 max of its competitors or something otherwise it's just lengthening the page way too much", with each name opening its own page and its chart drawn while the pointer is over it. A table of every member of the group drew a median of fifteen rows and up to eighty-six, AAPL's among them, since its industry holds no other member and its group is its sector. The table draws the name's own row and then at most ten other members of the group its moves are read against, so every row is a member the medians were taken over: the members sharing the name's industry first, being the companies the provider files as doing what it does, then the rest of the group, each part ordered by how closely the member's daily moves followed the name's. The move annotator reads that likeness as the correlation of the two names' daily returns over the sessions both hold along the name's stored year, and a member sharing fewer than sixty such returns is drawn after the rest with its count rather than a figure over fewer. Asked to choose, the operator took this over the ten members most alike whatever their industry and over the ten largest companies, whose size the store does not hold. The likeness is drawn beside each row with whether the member shares the name's industry, so a row placed by a weak figure reads as weak; the order is one of likeness, which says nothing of which company is the better, and the table ranks no company as an investment. Each ticker opens its own page, and holding the pointer over it or giving it the focus draws the member's stored closes with its nearest bands. The columns are otherwise the close, the distance below the stored year's high, the 60-session return, the trend state and the distance row mark, with no multiple and no yield, since the table compares how prices moved and a company's own figures are in its numbers. The readings and the choice are the move annotator's, written on the name's row of a table of their own, on the operator's ruling of 2026-09-23: the indicator engine is one of the sources every candidate evaluator's version pins, and a reading written there would move the versions the candidates stand at. This supersedes the ruling that drew every member of the group in ticker order.

**A page ranks no company as an investment, and the one reading of a company that orders a list is the direction of its reported quarters** Phase 12's second gate reads relative strength, each member's return over 63 and over 126 sessions placed among every member's, and the list draws the names passing by the state their reported quarters give them, improving first and deteriorating last, then by reward to risk, then relative strength, then band strength (see: Tonight's list is the swing filter's with improving businesses drawn first, and an evening is listed and ordered by the rule that listed it). A name's page opened by saying it does not rank the name against any other, and hiding the rank there would hide the input that put the name on the list. The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 that the page draws both returns and each one's place, and that its refusal and section 15.14's say one thing in one wording: no page ranks a company as an investment, and the one kind of rank a page draws is a return's place among the members' returns, which is a fact about the chart. The sentence is held once in the code that draws it, so the page and the two sections cannot come to say different things. The state orders the list without ranking a company as an investment, because it is the direction of the company's own reported quarters read by a stated rule, sales and operating margin against a year earlier, and says nothing of the price, a forecast or what the company is worth (see: Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night by rules the measured split settled, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone). The operator ruled on 2026-09-27 that the list is drawn so, and section 15.14 says it in these terms: no screen orders names by an opinion about the company, and the one reading of a company that orders anything is the direction of its reported quarters, by a stated rule.

Supersedes **A page ranks no company as an investment, and a rank it draws is a return's place among the members' returns**, moved below with its reasoning, whose refusal stands word for word.

**A night's state is read off its own run log rows and its tries, and the pages that state it read that one state** The operator approved on 2026-09-28 a night that tries again from the step that stopped, a notice at the top of tonight's page and the Run page, and a press running the rest of a night, and asked that the Run page's headline and tonight's notice read one source, with a test failing where the two disagree. A night's runs are the ones whose id is a night's, a pass of the overnight queue excepted. A try the night makes again, or a run of the rest of it, carries the id of the run it continues with `-try-` and its number, so the newest run by the instant its id carries is read together with its tries: each stage as the newest of them wrote it, and each try with the step it stopped at and the reason it wrote. Its state is finished where the arithmetic closed; waiting to try again where the newest try stopped before the close and wrote beside the stop the instant its next try starts, until that instant and the night's deadline after it have passed; running where the newest try recorded neither a stop nor a close and its last row falls inside the night's deadline; left unfinished where it stopped with no try left, where a waiting night's next try never wrote a row, or where its last row is older than the deadline and no step said why, which is a process ended from outside, a machine asleep among the causes; and where no run of the night is stored, not yet run until 00:30 UTC on the day after the session, an hour after the scheduled night starts, and never ran from then, which is the instant the operator named, and no session on a day the exchange did not trade. A stop after the close, in the quarters step, the overnight queue or the report, is said beside a finished night and does not unfinish it, since the list was drawn. The four headline figures are read off the same rows: the stocks the close counted, the provider requests every run of the night made, the spend every row of the night recorded, and the tries after the first. The alternative was a state the night writes for itself, and it is not taken, because a night that dies cannot write that it died, and the run log is already what every stage writes when it ends. Only one reader of it exists, `RunScreen.Night`, and both pages are handed what it returns, so the headline and the notice cannot come to say different things. The night's steps are drawn in six groups of the steps they hold, prices and calendar, indicators and levels, plans and moves, readings and the list, the records, and what runs after the close, and every stage a night writes is in exactly one of them.

Supersedes **A night's state is read off its own run log rows, and the pages that state it read that one state**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that a night reading a stop was stopped: a stop now waits to try again, and a night whose tries are spent is left unfinished with each try's reason.

**The market on the Run page is named in one word by a stated rule that moves no gate** The operator asked on 2026-09-28 for the market drawn as a gauge of the share of the index above its 200-day average with the 45% point marked, with a one-word label, "healthy, mixed or weak, by a stated rule: weak below 45% as ruled, and propose the point between mixed and healthy". The word is weak where the night's breadth is below the floor of the market gate the night ran under, read from that version's settings so the word and the gate cannot disagree; healthy at 60% or above; and mixed between. A night whose breadth was not read carries no word and says so. 60% is proposed in section 17, and no study sets it: over the 53 sessions the operator's store read a 200-day average for, from 2026-07-14 to 2026-09-25, the share ran from 47.6% to 75.2% with a median of 68.7%, 41 of them at or above 60% and none below 45%, so the word reads healthy on most of those nights and mixed on the weaker ones. The word moves no gate and lists or leaves off no name; the market gate's floor is the only figure of the rule that decides anything, and it is the gate's own. The breadth line beneath the gauge is the same share over the sessions before the night that the store holds averages for, up to sixty, each counted by the swing readings' own rule over the names holding a close that session, a session on which fewer than half hold an average reading none, so a store holding one year of bars draws about fifty-three and says so.

**Status is drawn in blues with violet for a wait and red for a failure alone** The operator asked on 2026-09-28 for the Run page to show what is happening "in as much visually friendly manner as possible", with green and orange kept for price levels. A night that finished or is running, and every picture of a count on the Run page, is drawn in two steps of one blue; a night not yet run, which is a wait, in one violet; and a night that stopped, was left unfinished or never ran in one red, which marks a failure and nothing else, never a fall in price. Green and orange keep the one meaning section 15.6 gives them (see: Support and resistance own two hues and nothing else uses them), and not yet measured stays a dashed outline (see: Not yet measured is drawn as a dashed outline, never as a pale value). The stylesheet names each of the three with the rule it is held to.

**The news labels alone name the model that wrote them** Every part of a page states where it came from and as of when, and a written section says when it was written rather than which model wrote it, because a reader judges a sentence by its date and its sources and not by a vendor's name. The operator's brief of 2026-09-28 asks for one narrow exception: the labelling model named once, in small type, on the news region of a name's page. A label is not a researched sentence: it is a kind, a direction and a one-sentence reason the model gave for one article, kept by the profile that wrote it and never overwritten, so after a switch two models' labels stand beside each other and a reader asking which model read an article has a fair question with a stored answer. The region names the model that wrote the newest label it draws, once; tonight's counts and the Run page's line name the profile and the model the labeller's own row records; and every other surface keeps the rule above and the test that holds it. Ruled on 2026-09-29 in the 12.6 ruling, as the plan's narrowing of the rule for the news region alone.

**Every page reads one index at a time chosen under Universe, and every figure names its index** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 as part of 15.1's second half: "A universe selector at the top of the page, labelled "Universe", offering S&P 500, S&P 400 and S&P 600, each with its member count. The choice is kept in the URL, defaults to S&P 500, and the same selector appears on Past picks, Run, Universe and Researched." And: "Every figure names its index. The page heading reads "Tonight: S&P 400" (and so on). The night's summary, the breadth line, the market line and every count say which index they are over, for example "1 of 400 S&P 400 members passed" and "the S&P 400's breadth: 52% above their 200-day average". No figure on the page mixes indices." And: "Each card's rule description is drawn from that index's own rule settings, never hand-written, so it cannot drift from the rule. On the 400 and 600 it states what differs from the 500: the minimum price, the daily dollar-volume floor, the profit check, and that trading costs are taken off. The status line reads "Provisional: not yet frozen" or "Live rule since <date>" as the rule is. The drift card on the 400 and 600 carries its evidence line, and the heavyweights card names its design and the index's sector comparison." And: "One trade a stock and the six reports taken in turn work across all three indices, and the "Still open" line and Past picks show which index each trade was listed under." So Tonight, Past picks, Run, Universe and Researched each open with the selector, its three choices each stating its member count, the choice carried in the page's address so a link opens it, and the S&P 500 read where the address names none. A page reads the chosen index alone: its heading names it, and every count, the night's summary, the breadth line and the market line say the index they are over, so no figure on it mixes two. A card's description is written by code from the settings its index's rule holds and never by hand, so a setting changed changes its words, and on the 400 and 600 it states each way the rule differs from the S&P 500's, its minimum price, its dollar volume floor, its profit check and its costs taken off; its status line reads "Provisional: not yet frozen" until the rule's freeze and "Live rule since" the day it registered after; the drift's card on the 400 and 600 carries its line of evidence (see: The 400's and 600's earnings drift lists from the first night with the evidence against it on its card), and the heavyweights' card names its design and the sector comparison its index reads (see: The 400 and 600 each sweep two heavyweight designs and keep the stronger after costs). A trade open on any index's card holds its stock on every other, and the "Still open" line and Past picks name the index each trade was listed under. It narrows, without superseding, the seam the universe was built with, every screen taking the selected universe as a filter (see: One universe now, the seam for more built now). The operator's done condition: "Done when every page is screenshotted with each of the three choices and read back: the heading, every count and each card's description name the right index and come from that index's settings. Add a test that changes a 400 rule's setting and sees its card description change."

**The card warns of a report inside the time three in four of the rule's trades had ended by, and notes one inside the cap alone** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan, and its value on 2026-10-06. The brief asked for the next report date against the rule's longest hold. The caps are 63 sessions for the pullback and the breakout and 60 for the drift, about a quarter, and every company reports once a quarter: over 18,036 S&P 500 member-nights, 36 nights from 2025-10-09 to 2026-06-23, the next report fell within 63 sessions on 96.0 per cent, within 60 on 93.6, within 20 on 31.2 and within 15 on 24.1, so a line warning inside the cap would warn on nearly every card and tell the operator nothing. The line warns where the report's reaction session, taken by the earnings rule's own function, falls inside the sessions by which three in four of the rule's replayed trades had ended, read from its record; notes it without a warning where it falls inside the cap alone; and warns where no report date is on file, saying so. Over 1,916 stored S&P 500 reports the reaction session's close moved a median 1.3 typical moves, and 44.8 per cent moved at least 1.5, the breakout's stop distance, so beside the date the card states the stock's own reactions in the plan's risks.

**The night asks the dividend calendar for each of the next 21 sessions, one request a session** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan as R6, and settled by 16.0's data check. A quarterly dividend is small against most stops, a median 0.13 of the risk at a stop 1.5 typical moves beneath, and 0.36 at the 90th percentile over 398 payers, which is material for a high yielder, so a pick's card names the next ex-date inside the hold. The stored fundamentals carry only the last declared ex-date, fetched when a filing arrived, and the provider's dividend calendar answers forward dates only for one symbol or one day at a time. So the night asks it for each of the 21 sessions after the night, one request a session, the same at fifty names or fifteen hundred: 21 of the day's 100,000 weighted calls, each answer every market's listings going ex-dividend that day, the index's own exchange read alone and every member of the indices the night reads stored under a kind of its own in the calendar. 16.0's check found the calendar carrying every one of the 29 dates the S&P 500's payers had declared inside the window. A calendar that refuses or answers in a form that cannot be read stores nothing and is named on the calendar's row, and the earnings stored beside it stand.

**A pick's next ex-dividend date is the calendar's where it declares one and the last declared date plus the usual interval where it does not** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan as C1, which kept the line on the card whatever the calendar carried. The calendar dated 42 of the S&P 500's 397 payers inside its window, and a quarter on from each payer's last ex-date puts about 30 more inside it undeclared. So where the calendar declares no date inside the hold, the card carries the company's last declared ex-date forward by its usual interval, a year over the count of dividends in its last whole year that paid, to the first date after the night, and draws it as estimated; a date past the hold, a company paying none and one whose answer files no year's count draw none. The forward rate, the last ex-date and each year's count come from the fundamentals answer the quarters fetch already asks for, kept at no further request, and the payment a share is the forward rate over that count, read in the plan's risks, or in per cent of the buy for a rule with no stop.

**Market events inside a hold are read from a committed table of the Fed's and the BLS's own dates, and asked of no provider** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan. FOMC decisions and CPI releases inside a hold belong on the card: the days they are announced carry much higher market returns and Sharpe ratios than other days (Savor and Wilson 2013). The provider's economic calendar comes with its fundamentals plans at one request a night, but each event would be matched by the name the provider gives it, which can change without notice, and a match that stops finding its event reads as a hold with no event in it. The Fed's own page carries the 2026 and 2027 meeting schedules, 2027's announced on 2025-09-05, and the BLS publishes each year's CPI release dates, 2027's not yet. So the dates are a table committed at the repository's root, each row naming the page it was read from, read by the card with no request; a hold running past the table's last date says so rather than reading the absence as no event, and the table is extended by hand as each year's schedule is published.

**A holding with no stop is sized at an equal share of the account across the most holdings its book can hold** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan. The sector heavyweights are held while they lead and sold at a month's first close where the rule would no longer buy them, so they have no stop, the dollars at risk over a stop's distance has no distance, and their record is in percent against their size cut rather than in risks. Such a holding is sized at the account divided by the most holdings its book can hold, 22 on the S&P 500's live rule at 2 leaders in each of 11 sectors, and no more than the position cap allows, and the card says the whole position is at risk, since nothing sells it at a price set in advance.

**The account settings live in a file of their own under the data root and in nothing the store or the logs hold** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan. The brief asked for two private settings, the account's size and the risk a trade in per cent, kept outside the repository, never committed or logged and editable from a small page; the plan adds the position cap, a fifth of the account. They are kept in a file of their own under the configured data root, which the repository's ignore list already covers, and the settings page writes it whole, so a write that fails leaves the file as it was. Not the secrets file, which holds the provider keys a failed write could lose, and not the browser's storage, which a phone and a desktop would hold apart. No store row, log line, run log row or exported report carries them.

**A taken trade's fill is the next session's open once its bar is stored, and the plan's buy marked provisional until then** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05 with the phase 16 plan: "The Taken control's default fill: the next session's open, read from the stored bar once it exists; until then the plan's buy, marked as provisional and replaced by the open when the bar arrives unless the operator entered a price. The follower and the record use the fill as recorded." A pick is listed after the close and bought at the next session's open at the earliest, so the open is the fill a taken trade most often has, and the stored bar is the first place it is known. A price the operator entered is never replaced. Where the open that replaces a provisional fill sits at or under the stop, the trade's risk is read at the plan's distance, the buy less the stop, and the card says the open gapped under the stop, since a risk read from a fill under its stop would be nothing or less.

## Where it runs

**The whole system is a checkout and one database file** Everything the system owns lives in a single database, including the text of every source document it has fetched and the candidate register. There is no directory of loose artefacts to keep in step with it, so moving an installation means copying the code and one file.

**The operator's store is never deleted, and every site that removes a file is stated where a check holds it** The operator asked on 2026-10-01: "Can you make sure to have an entry which ensures that the db is never deleted. My other project deleted its db". The store is the one thing this tool cannot rebuild: bars are kept a year while a record is read over about four, the provider serves no past night's calendar or news again, and every outcome, block and research section in it was written on a night that will not come back. So no code, script, migration, test or session deletes, moves, truncates or replaces the store file under the configured data root, and a migration that has to drop a table, which SQLite forces where a constraint a table was created with must change, copies its rows to the rebuilt table first and renames it back in the same migration. A rule a reader has to remember is the kind that failed elsewhere, so it is held by a check and not by a habit: `store-never-deleted` reads every site in the shipped source, in the scripts and in the suite that removes a file or a folder, moves one or writes one over whatever was there, and holds them to a stated list with what each acts on, so a new one is a line somebody has to choose to add and to say what it removes; none may be handed the store's path, no script's removal may reach the data root, the scratch stores the sweep builds and removes are read as rooted in the machine's temporary folder, and each reader is shown to find a removal of the store over constructed input. What the tree held when it was written: no code and no script deletes the store; the one store a script drops is the one `tools/ci.*` creates under `data-ci`, which was the data root itself until 5.7, when verifying a checkpoint deleted the store the night fills; the night's script removes its own refusal note and build copies under the data root's folder of nights; and two migrations drop a table, `research_request` and `membership`, each a rebuild. A repair that seems to need a fresh store is the operator's decision and not a session's, and a copy of the store is made before anything is touched. The check cannot see a person or a tool outside this repository removing the file, which no rule here reaches; a backup of the store on another disk is the answer to that and is the operator's to rule.

**The store is copied once the night and every process it started have finished and the newest three copies are kept after each is opened and read, and the copy writes a row as it starts and one as it ends** The operator ruled on 2026-10-02: "Backup: yes. After every night finishes, copy the store to <SSD folder, for example E:\EquityBrief-backups>, keep the last 3 copies, check each copy opens and reads before deleting the oldest, and show the newest copy's time and folder on the run page." Asked which disk, since the store is on the spinning one, the operator chose the folder as written, `E:\EquityBrief-backups`, which guards against a damaged file and not against the disk failing. The night starts the copy as its last step, a process of its own as the labeller is, so the night's own rows and deadline are untouched by it. The copy waits while the night holds its lock, for the labeller until its last row or its time limit and ten minutes more, and for the drain, whose lock it then holds so no pass writes beneath it, because "after every night finishes" includes the report pass and the labeller the night starts; it gives up after twenty hours, well before the next night is built. It copies through SQLite's own backup, which reads the store page by page under one read lock and writes a whole file, rather than copying the file's bytes, which a writer could change halfway. A copy is written under a name of its own, named by its instant once written, and opened and read: SQLite's quick check of its pages, and its bars read back against the store's. Of the copies, the newest three are kept, and each is opened and read before any older one is removed; where one of them does not open and read, nothing is removed, so a damaged copy never costs the good ones behind it. The folder is a setting, absolute or under the data root, `backups` under the data root where none is set, since no drive letter is written into the code; the copy's own row names it relative to the data root, since no absolute path is written into a store row, and the run page reads it back against its own data root and draws the newest copy's time and folder beneath anything to worry about. The store itself is never touched beyond the copy's two rows: no site removes or moves it, and every file the copy removes is a copy or an unfinished copy in its own folder (see: The operator's store is never deleted, and every site that removes a file is stated where a check holds it).

Supersedes **The store is copied once the night and every process it started have finished, and the newest three copies are kept after each is opened and read**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but the one row. The operator ruled on 2026-10-08, after two of the three copies the newest row said were kept were found gone from the folder with no row saying so, and the night of 2026-10-07's copy, ended from outside and started again twice, had written no row at all: "find why two copies were removed at about 04:35Z and why the run log says three were kept, restore the copy count the backup ruling set, record a copy row for every night, and add a test that rotation never leaves fewer copies than the setting." A copy that wrote its one row only as it ended left nothing when it was ended before it finished, by a hand, a stop or a machine, so a night whose copy never finished read on the run page exactly like one whose copy had not yet started, and the row before it stood as the newest, naming three kept where the folder held one. So the copy writes a row as it starts, under a stage of its own, since the run log holds one row a run and stage, stamped with its start at both ends and saying whether it waits for the labeller, and a row as it ends as before; a start the run log holds with no end is a copy ended before it finished, which the run page draws as waiting or copying until a copy's longest wait and an hour have passed from its start, and as ended before it finished after, since no copy outlives its wait and its hour. And the ending row names the copies the newest ending row before it kept that are gone from the folder when the copy begins, removed by no copy, because a copy removes only beyond the three it keeps and names each removal, so a kept copy gone unnamed went by another hand, and nothing recorded it; the run page names them beneath its checklist, and the checklist gains two items, the store copied after the night began and every copy the last copy kept still in its folder, so a copy that never finished and a copy gone are seen on the morning after rather than found a month on. Rotation is held by a test over none to five copies standing before one more is made, never fewer than the three kept, or than stood plus the one made under three, with the removed the oldest beyond the three, since the rule a reader has to remember is the kind that fails: the copies that went on 2026-10-08 went by no code of the copy's, which no test of the copy's can see, and what a test can hold is that the copy itself never leaves fewer.

**A store's copy counts and removes only the copies its own rows name, and a test or a rehearsal names a copies' folder of its own** Found on 2026-10-02, the day the copy landed: the folder is a setting every worker reads, and the suite's own night, run through the worker's build beside the checkout, which carries the machine's settings, ended by copying its store into the operator's folder at 17:13 UTC, a file of 1.4 MB named as the operator's copies are. Read by name alone, two more such runs would have removed the operator's copy made by hand at 17:09 as the oldest of four, and that night's copy would have kept two of the suite's beside it. So a copy counts as one of a store's own only where that store's run log names it, made or refused, and only those are kept or removed: a copy another store wrote into the folder is neither one of the three nor ever removed, so no other store's worker can cost the operator a copy, whatever it reads. An unfinished copy is removed only once it is named for an instant further back than a copy waits, since a newer one may be another store's copy still being written. And since another store's copies in the folder would still stand beside the operator's when a copy is chosen to restore from, the suite starts the worker's build only with a copies' folder under its own temporary store's root, and a rehearsal over a copy of the store names one under its own root as it names its data root; a copy is restored from by the name the run page's line gives.

**A writer waits up to ten minutes for another, and a pass stores what it fetched in one write** The night, a research pass started from a name page and the overnight queue all write the one store file, and it takes one write at a time. The driver a connection is opened through waits thirty seconds for another writer before a statement fails, and on 2026-09-18 that failed a night: a pass run beside the overnight queue stored the 7,206 documents it had fetched one to a write, each a disk sync on the operator's spinning disk, which held the store for about twenty minutes with no turn the queue's writes could take, and the queue failed on "database is locked" after writing the key for 207 of the 431 listed names. The other direction was open too: a night's stage holds its write for minutes on that disk, the levels for 218 seconds at the longest, so a pass started during one would have failed on its first write. So a pass and a theme pass store the documents they fetched in one write, as the facts assembler has stored its files since the first night over the index, and every connection the worker and the store open waits up to ten minutes for another writer, longer than any stage the night has measured, except in the sources a ladder rule's code version or a candidate evaluator's version pins: an edit there moves the version the open windows and the registered candidates are measured under, and those sources are the night's own stages, which beside a pass wait for one write. Past the wait a statement fails, naming the lock. The read surface is not a writer and keeps the driver's own wait.

**A night that stops before its close is tried again from the step that stopped, three more times fifteen minutes apart, each try under a deadline of its own** The operator asked on 2026-09-28 for "some retry attempts from the point where it got stuck so that the night can complete", and approved the plan the same day. A step that fails, or that passes the night's deadline, stops the try it is in; the night writes beside the stop the instant its next try starts, waits fifteen minutes, and runs again from that step under a new run id and a deadline of its own, up to three more times. The steps before it are not run again, because every stage reads and writes by the session and not by the run, so what an earlier try stored stands; this is what a night run again for its session already relies on. Fifteen minutes because the failures a try again is for are a provider briefly down, a store held by another writer and a machine that slept, each of which has usually passed by then, and three because four tries cover an hour of waiting, after which the fault is one a person has to look at. A night is not tried again where it was refused before its first step, where the day's allowance of weighted calls is spent, or where the exchange calendar cannot place the day, since each would stop the same way, or after the close, since the list is drawn and what follows is the quarters, the queue and the report, each bounded by its own limit. The process exits non-zero only once no try is left, and the scheduled task's own time limit must allow for four tries and three waits, which is why RUNBOOK raises it to eight hours. Each try is bounded by the night's deadline (see: A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has a two-hour deadline it cannot move), and the deadline itself is not moved by a try.

**A night left unfinished is run to its end from the step it stopped at by a press or a command, and one night runs at a time under a lock file** The operator asked on 2026-09-28 how to restart a night from the page, and approved a "Run the rest of the night" press and `tools/nightly.ps1 --resume`. Both run the newest night on the run log from the first step its tries have not finished, as one more try under that night's id and on that night's session, and do nothing where the night finished; the rest of a night for a session before the one the clock is in runs as a night named for its session does, asking for no quarters and no report, since what those would store is today's answer. The press starts the worker the way the report press starts the drain, from a copy of the build under the data root, so the shipped source still starts a process at one call. A night takes a lock file under the data root before its first step and gives it up when it ends, and the operating system gives it up for a process that ends from outside; a night or a run of the rest of one that finds the lock held is refused and says which run holds it, and the press is refused on the page while it is held. Two nights writing one session at once would each read what the other half wrote, and the store's own writer lock orders statements rather than nights.

**Nothing is written against one operating system** The runtime, the database and every provider call already behave the same on Windows and on macOS. Two things would break that if left implicit, so they are decided here: no path is hardcoded, the data directory being configuration; and the nightly run is a single command that an external scheduler invokes rather than a service that schedules itself, because scheduling is the one genuinely platform-specific piece. The model endpoint is a URL in configuration for the same reason.

**The local and paid boundary is a property of the machine, not of the design** The split in section 13 is set by how much context the local model can hold, so different hardware moves it, and a machine with more memory available to the model can take more of the work locally. The comparison against the fixture is re-run after a hardware change rather than the earlier boundary being assumed to still hold.

**Windows is verified on the operator's machine, and the hosted runners are macOS and Linux** The suite ran on a hosted Windows runner on every push and every pull request from 0.4 until 2026-09-15. By then that leg took from 15 to 33 minutes, against about a minute for the same suite on the operator's Windows machine and two on the hosted macOS runner, and it had grown from about three minutes at phase 4 while the other two stayed flat, so every merge waited on the one leg. The operator ruled it removed on 2026-09-15. Windows stays a supported platform and is verified where the nightly job runs: `tools/ci.ps1` on the operator's machine, green over the tree being merged, and recorded in the checkpoint's PROGRESS entry in those words, which `two-platform` reads. What is given up is a Windows run on a clean image for every push. A fault that shows only on a fresh Windows install, a tool the operator's machine happens to have or a default it has changed, is now found on that machine or not at all, and a change merged without anyone running the script has nothing on Windows behind it, which is why the merge rule names the run. macOS keeps its hosted leg and Linux its case-sensitivity job, and a hosted Windows leg returning is a new decision rather than an edit to the workflow.

**Every entry written since the hosted Windows leg was removed records the Windows run, whatever the entry lands** `two-platform` read the Windows run off the entries that land a checkpoint (see: Windows is verified on the operator's machine, and the hosted runners are macOS and Linux), which left a merge whose entry opens "Not a checkpoint entry" to the merge rule alone, and no check reads the merge rule. Such merges carry code: the 6.0, 7.0 and 7.2 rulings and the 7.0 and 8.0 planning passes each changed files under `src`, 8.0's under a decision of its own, so what an entry was called decided whether its code owed a Windows record.
