The merge rule names the run for every merge, so the check reads every entry written after the last one a hosted Windows leg ran for, a planning pass, a ruling, a correction and a sign-off among them, split out of the record by the reader `DuePoints` lands checkpoints from, so what an entry is cannot be read two ways. It moves no verdict: over the record at 44b7f6d each of the 23 entries written after that one says `tools/ci.ps1` ran green, 3 of them landing no checkpoint. Ruled at the 8.0 correction.

## Process

**A frozen fixture per checkpoint, and the harness decides sign-off** Live data changes nightly and cannot be diffed; the committed fixture can. UNEXAMINED never counts as a pass.

**The fixture is diffed on each stage's serialised output, of which the facts file is one** Prose varies between runs and layout changes constantly, so diffing rendered output would fail for reasons that do not matter and pass over reasons that do. That argument is about the rendered page and holds; naming the facts file as the thing diffed narrowed it to one stage that does not exist until 5.3, leaving every expectation from 1.2 onward with nothing the record said to diff against. Each stage serialises what it produced, the membership set, the stored bars, the refusal record, the indicator rows, and the fixture diffs that. Not the store itself: diffing rows couples every expectation to schema shape, so a migration adding a column breaks expectations about values that did not change, and the fixture stops being a check on behaviour. Supersedes The fixture is diffed on the facts file, not on a rendered page.

**A research pass is recorded per section call, keyed on the canonicalised request** A pass has to replay without a network or no fixture covers research at all, and section 19.2 asked for a recording model endpoint without saying what a recording is. It is what every other outward call here already is: a captured request and response pair committed with the fixture, named in the manifest, and read by the parser the live client uses. Two things about its shape are choices a later session could reverse invisibly. It is keyed on the request rather than on the order the calls were made, because a pass that writes its sections in a different order must still replay, and a sequence-keyed recording passes on the run that produced it and fails on every other. And there is one record per section call rather than one per pass, which is what per-section dating in the research record already requires and what lets one section be run on two models over one evidence set (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole). The key is a hash of the lane, the section, the model identifier, the identifiers of the documents handed in and the prompt text, so a recording answers the request it was made for and nothing else. A replay asked for a request the recording does not hold refuses by name rather than reaching the network, which is the property the recorded feeds already have by holding no client.

**Every phase opens with something to look at** The first checkpoint of each phase renders a page. A report about row counts is not visible output.

**Facts are declared once and cited by descriptive name** Behaviour lives in this document, data shapes in section 16, and no component, decision, invariant or check carries a code number. Citations must match a name exactly, so a paraphrase fails loudly rather than resolving to the wrong thing.

**Specifications are edited clean; the changelog records what they said before** No document carries struck-through text. A superseded decision moves to section 22 with its reasoning.

**A transaction taken for speed is not asserted, and a scan that would need twelve exemptions is not written** Five stages write in a loop and open a transaction, and the question the phase 5 sign-off left open was whether a check should assert that they do. One of the five carries a correctness property and is asserted as one: `ForwardReturnFiller` interrupted partway leaves some horizons written and a base rate belonging to none of them, which is the shape the refetch's atomicity was already ruled on, so a behavioural test induces the interruption and the stage is asserted to leave nothing behind. The other four are speed at a row count the committed fixture cannot reach, and there is no reading of them a text scan can make: all sixteen writers loop, so a scan keyed on the loop reports every one of them and a scan keyed on the transaction reports none, and what distinguishes a stage that needs one is the number of rows it writes, which appears in no file's text. A scan reporting sixteen findings of which twelve are not defects is made green by exempting twelve by name, and an exemption list doing all of a check's work is the under-reporting failure `coverage-reported` exists to catch, arriving inside a check nobody needed. So the check is not written and this entry is the reason, which is the form the uncited decisions were closed on at 3.0: a citation cannot be placed at a rule that does not exist, and a check cannot be written for a property no instrument reaches.

**The pass that plans phase 8 carries the phase 7 sign-off's repairs and its rulings' code** A planning pass builds none of the phase it plans, and this one still edits shipped source: the phase 7 sign-off carried repairs that are a line of code each, and four of the rulings 8.0 owes are a behaviour rather than a sentence. The operator ruled on 2026-09-15 that they land in the planning pass rather than being spread across the loop's checkpoints, because a repair parked in a checkpoint that does not need it is a checkpoint doing two jobs, and a repair left to a later pass runs on every night until then. The exception is named here so a later session reading that planning a phase builds none of it, against a pass that touched shipped source, finds the rule rather than an inconsistency. What it does not license is building a checkpoint of the phase in the planning pass: no checkpoint of phase 8 is built by 8.0, and the pass that makes these edits may not sign the phase off.

**Code a pass that lands no checkpoint carries into shipped source meets the done conditions a checkpoint's code meets** The pass that planned phase 8 carried the phase 7 sign-off's repairs and four rulings' code under a decision that said what that exception did not license, building a checkpoint, and not what the code still owed (see: The pass that plans phase 8 carries the phase 7 sign-off's repairs and its rulings' code). Its entry opened "Not a checkpoint entry", so its code owed no derived expectation and no Windows record any check read, and three of the phase 8 sign-off review's findings arrived that way: a prompt still asking for a quarter over the twelve-month figures the pass began carrying, a branch of the sentence the pass wrote for a name with no facts file that no test asserted, and the worker's reading of a named session against its store that no test ran. So code a planning pass or a ruling carries into shipped source is held to what a checkpoint's code is held to, and its entry records the same: the suite green with its count, the Windows run, a derived expectation added to the fixture or a row carried by name, a mutation chosen by a rule stated before its run and shown red, and the entry written before the run that verifies it. A correction to that code is labelled for the checkpoint the pass belongs to, as `Phase 8 / 8.0 - correction` is. What such a pass lands does not change: a planning entry still opens "Not a checkpoint entry" and lands its checkpoint and never its phase. Ruled at the 8.0 correction.

**A verdict note states no count of the row's own parts** A note in the harness's placement map says what the check asserts about a row, and until 8.0 some of them also counted the row's cells, the stores a component declares or the columns a table has. A count typed into a note is read against nothing: the phase report prints it whatever the row holds, which is how the read API's note said twelve cells read over a row the series state read had made thirteen, and how two notes said all thirteen cells blank over rows that had grown. So a note states no such count, the count lives in the row where it cannot go stale, and a guard refuses a number standing next to cells, stores, columns or reads in any note. Read over all 346 notes when the rule was written, nine stated such a count, and 61 others carry a number that is a rule figure the row itself states or a population of the fixture, which the claim's own check reads. Ruled at 8.0.

**A written rule never blocks a real fix** If a rule in this document prevents correcting a genuine defect, the rule is the defect and is amended in the same pass, with the change stated plainly.

**A due point names a checkpoint that exists wherever its phase has been detailed** The reader that decides whether a due point is in the plan accepted the phase alone, so any point inside a phase the plan has passed whether or not its checkpoint was written. That fallback is argued for and stays where it is argued: a later phase gets its checkpoints only at the previous phase's sign-off, so requiring a checkpoint by name would forbid the check roster from naming anything past the phase in hand. The argument does not reach a phase whose checkpoints are already written. Inside a detailed phase the point exists or it does not, and a due point naming one that does not is an obligation owed at a place nobody created and a claim out of scope until a checkpoint that will never land. Found at the phase 5 sign-off, where "5.8" read as in the plan before 5.8 was written and the suite stayed green on a checkpoint nobody had created. Detail is read from the plan itself, being any checkpoint heading inside the phase, rather than from a list of detailed phases kept beside the reader, which would be a second statement of the same fact and would go stale the moment a phase is planned.

**Phase 6's carried items are built as phase 7, and the improvement loop is phase 8** The phase 6 sign-off carried three items to 7.0, which was then the improvement loop's planning pass, and the first of them, the operator's ruling on a suspect name's own surfaces, was committed under 7.0 on 2026-09-14 by the clause in CLAUDE.md that files work under the checkpoint that owes it, while that phase had not started. The operator read the label as saying phase 7 had begun, and ruled the same day that the carried items are phase 7: that ruling at 7.0, the due-point reader's repair at 7.1 and the phase report at 7.2. The improvement loop moves to phase 8 with every checkpoint, due point, placement, roster row and line on a page that named it. The plan moved to meet the label rather than the label to meet the plan, because history already on `main` is not rewritten, and a label between two phases is not one the plan can carry: 6.5 already names the staleness judge. This rules phase 7 and changes no convention; the clause stands.

**The solution file takes the SDK's current format** The .NET 10 SDK writes `.slnx` and no longer writes the older `.sln` by default, so the solution is `EquityBrief.slnx`. Pinning the older format means every session that regenerates the file has to remember a flag to get it, and a format the tool has stopped writing drifts out of support with nothing announcing it. The corpus names the file in the layout block and in the Commands table and the suite finds the checkout by looking for it, so a format change is a rename in three places and the third one fails loudly if the other two are missed.

**The report generation the operator asked for is built as phase 9, and phase 8's sign-off is owed after it rather than before it** The operator asked on 2026-09-20 for three things a name's page cannot do: ask for a report from the row on tonight's list rather than from the name's own page, a request that outlasts the press rather than a process started beside the read surface, and a statement at the head of the page of which lane would write one. None of the three is a defect and no checkpoint owes any of them, so they are a phase rather than a correction (see: Phase 6's carried items are built as phase 7, and the improvement loop is phase 8). The merge section owed a phase's sign-off before the next phase's plan, and the operator ruled the same day that phase 9 opens first. What moves is the order of the two and nothing else: the sign-off stays owed on phase 8 as a whole, is named as outstanding in the ruling's own entry, and is discharged by nothing phase 9 does. The order moves because a sign-off needs a session that has committed no code while building does not, so holding the work behind it spends a session at the point the operator wants the page working, and the sign-off reads the same record whether it runs before phase 9 or after. The cost is named rather than left to be found: phase 8's defects stay unfound for as long as the sign-off waits, and one phase 9 builds on top of is dug out of more code than it would have been.

**Setup families are built as phase 13 beside phase 12's sign-off, which runs in a fresh session over what merged on 2026-10-01** The operator ruled on 2026-10-01: "This is Phase 13: setup families, and it starts now", with phase 12's sign-off running "in a fresh session over what is merged today, beside this work". The merge section owes a phase's sign-off before the next phase's plan, and this moves the order for one phase, as the ruling of 2026-09-20 moved it for phase 9: the sign-off stays owed on phase 12 as a whole, is named as outstanding in the ruling's own entry, and is discharged by nothing phase 13 does. What the sign-off reads is fixed by the ruling at what had merged on 2026-10-01, so the session that signs reads a tree phase 13 has not touched and the session that builds phase 13 does not sign phase 12. The order moves because the operator wants each family's picks on screen every night and a sign-off needs a session that has committed no code, which building does not; the two do not wait on each other. The cost is the one the earlier ruling named: a defect of phase 12 found by its sign-off is dug out from under whatever phase 13 has built on it by then.

**The sign-offs of phases 15 and 16 run between phase 17's pull requests, never beside one** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-08 with phase 17's plan, answering its brief's "beside this phase": the sign-offs of phases 15 and 16 are owed to fresh sessions the operator starts, and a review's branch and its mutations share the one checkout the build runs in (see: Each night is built from a clean copy of the main checkout's own commit and never from its working tree, and refuses only a checkout off main or ahead of the remote's main), so neither can run while a phase 17 pull request is open in it. When the operator starts a review session, phase 17 finishes the pull request it has open, returns the checkout to main, and pauses until the review hands back; the queue ruling in the 17.0 entry records the order. Phase 14's sign-off was taken the same way before phase 17 was planned, and a session that has committed code still may not sign that code off.

**Each night is built from a clean copy of the main checkout's own commit and never from its working tree, and refuses only a checkout off main or ahead of the remote's main** On 2026-09-29 an edit meant for a worktree beside the repository landed in the main checkout and was reverted at once. The night built and ran from that checkout's working tree, so a leftover edit would have run as live code, and a project compiles every source file beneath its folder, so a file never added to git is built as surely as an edit to a tracked one. Of the two ways offered, refusing and recording through a worker command built from the same tree, or never building from the working tree, the operator ruled the second on 2026-09-29 with its conditions. Each night exports the checkout's own commit, read with no fetch, into a folder of its own under the data root, one a commit, builds it once in Release and runs it; a copy already holding the worker is run as it is, so every try of a night, the rest of one and the drain it starts run the build the night made and never a newer one, and a copy no night has used for a week is removed. The script refuses only a checkout off main or holding a commit the remote's main does not have, before any worker exists, writing the instant and the reason to a file under the data root that tonight's page and the Run page read as the night's state, because an uncommitted or untracked file is never built and so costs no night; a checkout behind the remote's main runs and says so, since a merge fetched and not yet pulled is sometimes the safe state, and refusing on it would refuse the night whenever one stood. The data folder and the store are the checkout's by absolute path, so no night finds, creates or writes a store inside the copy; the secrets file is read from the checkout by a path the script names, over the settings beside the build and under the environment, and never copied into the copy; and the night records the commit on the run log under a stage of its own, which the Run page shows. The check could not live in the worker, which is built from the tree it would check, and the read surface on its port is outside this, started by hand from the working tree as it stands.

**A pull request of documents alone runs the checkpoint script alone, and one changing code runs the phase report as well** The operator ruled on 2026-10-02: "Documents-only pull requests run tools/ci.ps1 alone. Amend CLAUDE.md's merge rule and the check that disagree, so the rules stop conflicting; the reason, that code changes need both gates, stays." The merge rule asked for `tools/ci.ps1` on every pull request and nothing asked for the phase report at a merge, while the practice from 2026-09-29 ran both scripts over code and the operator's word of 2026-10-01 asked for neither over a plan. A pull request of documents alone runs `tools/ci.ps1`, which carries every corpus check that reads them, and `two-platform` already reads that run off every entry. One that changes shipped source, the suite, a fixture or a setting runs `tools/verify-phase.ps1` as well, because a change there can move a claim's verdict, and the phase report is the one run that gives every claim in scope a verdict from what the checks did and is green only with none unexamined, where a green suite says only that nothing it ran failed. No check asked for more or less than this, so none moved.

**The seventh swing family candidate leaves off a member whose reported quarters read deteriorating, and no live rule removes a stock for its state** The operator's fundamentals item of 2026-09-27 ruled three things together: absent is never a failure and no gate reads a fundamental, so a member with no fundamentals or too few quarters is judged and listed as any other; the state orders tonight's list and removes nothing from it; and one candidate over the live filter skips the members whose state is deteriorating, scored in shadow beside the others, the family becoming seven, each tested at 0.05 over the distinct trials. The candidate is the live filter's own evaluator with one more parameter rather than an evaluator of its own, so it is a whole rule like each variant: a registration that leaves a deteriorating business off fires where every gate read passes, no exclusion applies and the state the night's fundamental readings stored for the member reads anything but deteriorating, or nothing, so a member whose quarters the night did not read is judged as any other member is; every other registration states the parameter off and reads no state. The state is handed to the family's shadow by the filter, from the readings the night stored for the session, and read by no gate, so the live list changes nothing by this, and whether a deteriorating business is worth leaving off is answered by the candidate's record against the live filter's and never assumed. Its code moves the filter's pin and every evaluator's version, so it lands with the freeze's one remedy, which retires the six and registers the seven at one instant, and not before (see: A rule correction taken before the family's first scored night opens a filter version and registers the family again at one instant, and is no shape acceptance).

---

## Previously decided

**A holding's code is kept only where its closes move in step with the fund's values a share from one quarter end to the next** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-05: "(A): keep a ticker for a holding only where its quarter-end closes move in step with the fund's reported value per share for that holding, quarter after quarter, as proposed. Before any of the eight wrong-price tickers is left unmatched, try the company's correct delisted listing (for example BBBYQ for Bed Bath & Beyond) under the same check, and report which recovered." After the remedy of 15.3's correction, eight codes the provider files under a holding's own ISIN were found carrying another security's prices, over 61 holdings: COR_old, filed under CoreSite's ISIN, carrying AmerisourceBergen's; EE_old, FG_old and GOV at a few cents; and ARCH_old, BABY, BBBY_old and RPT_old. An ISIN names a security, and a match by it read no price, so nothing refused them. So every code a holding is matched to, by its ISIN or by its name, is read over the quarter ends it is matched at: the value a share the fund filed over the code's close, as the provider sends the close or with the splits it files after undone, whichever sits nearer the same ratio at the quarter end before, holds within 5 per cent from one quarter end to the next, or steps to a level it holds at the next quarter end as well, as the provider's closes divided down for a corporate action step and then hold. A code read at a single quarter end stands within 5 per cent of its close there, as a name match must, and a code read at none, which only an ISIN can match, is kept. A code failing it is matched to none at those quarter ends, and the holding's codes by its name, its ISIN set aside, are read in its place under the same test, a delisted listing the pulled history holds no close of asked of the provider once a quarter, each code kept at the quarter ends it holds a close at. Over the stored holdings before this rule, the eight wander from one quarter end to the next, BBBY_old's ratio in the S&P 600's fund running 0.373, 0.206, 0.370 and 0.440; six codes step at a spin-off to a level they hold, XPO's at 2.893 for eight quarter ends, 1.683 for five and 1.000 for fifteen, and VGR's, ZD's, BERY's, ENSG's and SITC's once each; and every other code read at two quarter ends or more holds within 1 per cent at every step. A code of the holding's own company whose earlier closes the provider adjusted for a corporate action, standing at a constant distance from the value a share, moves in step and is kept where the 5 per cent at one quarter end refused it.

Superseded on 2026-10-08 by **A holding's code is kept only where its closes move in step with the fund's values a share, and a match by name is held to the level at each quarter end as well**. The in-step reading stands; the last sentence kept a name's code at a steady distance from the value a share, which is what GrubHub's match to GRUB at a ratio running about 6.5 over five quarter ends was, and the superseding rule keeps such a code only where the distance is one ratio at every quarter end, as the company's own adjusted closes hold it.

**The store is copied once the night and every process it started have finished, and the newest three copies are kept after each is opened and read** The operator ruled on 2026-10-02: "Backup: yes. After every night finishes, copy the store to <SSD folder, for example E:\EquityBrief-backups>, keep the last 3 copies, check each copy opens and reads before deleting the oldest, and show the newest copy's time and folder on the run page." Asked which disk, since the store is on the spinning one, the operator chose the folder as written, `E:\EquityBrief-backups`, which guards against a damaged file and not against the disk failing. The night starts the copy as its last step, a process of its own as the labeller is, so the night's own rows and deadline are untouched by it. The copy waits while the night holds its lock, for the labeller until its last row or its time limit and ten minutes more, and for the drain, whose lock it then holds so no pass writes beneath it, because "after every night finishes" includes the report pass and the labeller the night starts; it gives up after twenty hours, well before the next night is built. It copies through SQLite's own backup, which reads the store page by page under one read lock and writes a whole file, rather than copying the file's bytes, which a writer could change halfway. A copy is written under a name of its own, named by its instant once written, and opened and read: SQLite's quick check of its pages, and its bars read back against the store's. Of the copies, the newest three are kept, and each is opened and read before any older one is removed; where one of them does not open and read, nothing is removed, so a damaged copy never costs the good ones behind it. The folder is a setting, absolute or under the data root, `backups` under the data root where none is set, since no drive letter is written into the code; the copy's own row names it relative to the data root, since no absolute path is written into a store row, and the run page reads it back against its own data root and draws the newest copy's time and folder beneath anything to worry about. The store itself is never touched beyond the one row: no site removes or moves it, and every file the copy removes is a copy or an unfinished copy in its own folder (see: The operator's store is never deleted, and every site that removes a file is stated where a check holds it).

Superseded on 2026-10-08 by **The store is copied once the night and every process it started have finished and the newest three copies are kept after each is opened and read, and the copy writes a row as it starts and one as it ends**. What this entry ruled stands but the one row: a copy ended before it finished left no row, so a night whose copy never happened read like one whose copy had not started, and copies gone from the folder by another hand stood as kept on the newest row.

**The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03 as the fourth family, on provisional settings until its sweep proposes the ones its freeze registers: "the largest companies in each sector that are outperforming their sector, held while they keep leading. A months-long rotation, not a swing trade, so it needs its own entry, exit, scoring and record." Its universe is the index's members on the session, each in the GICS sector the provider files (see: A company's sector on a session is the GICS sector the provider files, with the fourteen moves of 2023-03-17 read by date), and its size cut a sector's five largest companies by value as it stood (see: A company's value on a session is the newest share count filed before it times the session's close on the count's split basis) (see: Companies are ranked by CIK with one listing held, the class that traded the more dollars over fifty sessions). Its look-back is counted in sessions, three, six and twelve months read as 63, 126 and 251: twelve months is one session short of a year of sessions, so a return over it reads no close older than the year of bars the store keeps, and six months, 126 sessions, is the provisional setting. It rebalances on the first session of each month, where the month's new leaders are bought at that close, and the book reads a rebalance on the first night of a month it runs, so a month whose first night did not run rebalances on the night after rather than not at all, and its first night reads one so it holds from then. A month rather than a week because a rotation among the largest companies turns slowly, and the sweep tries both. Its card is drawn after the swing families', with its holdings, the last rebalance's entries and exits, the next rebalance's date and held while leading in place of a stop and a target, and the night asks for a report on what a rebalance buys, a buy point of the night after every swing family's picks, and never on a holding carried from an earlier month, on the operator's words of 2026-10-03: "A new entry is eligible for the night's reports under the cap of six; a holding carried over is not." (see: The night asks for a report on the first six names its page draws). No model reads, chooses or orders anything it holds (see: Rules choose the stocks, and the AI writes the reports).

Superseded on 2026-10-07 by **The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need**. A return over 251 sessions reads the close 251 sessions before the night, so it needs 252 closes, and the store's year holds 251 on any night whose date a year back was no session: on 2026-10-05, the four registered books' first rebalance, every return, beta and lead read none and no sector had a leader. What this entry ruled stands but that premise.

**A heavyweight's beta is read over 251 daily returns against the index** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03 for the variant the sweep tries: "Beta: not required. High-beta stocks earned less for their risk (Frazzini and Pedersen 2014), so beta of at least 1 is a variant, not a rule." Where a setting reads it, a stock's beta is the slope of its daily returns on the index's over the 251 sessions to the rebalance, the most the year of bars the store keeps holds, and a stock holding fewer has none and is bought by no setting that asks for one.

Superseded on 2026-10-07 by **The sector heavyweights hold the largest companies leading their sectors, rotated on the first session of each month whose stored year holds the closes their readings need**. A beta over 251 daily returns reads 252 closes, which the store's year does not hold on a night whose date a year back was no session, so 251 is not the most it holds on every night. The beta's span and its floor stand.

**A screen reads and renders, and computes nothing** No screen calls a model, makes a network request, or works out a level, a stop or a rate. Every number on every screen is already in the store. A rendering layer that computes is a second implementation of the same arithmetic, and two implementations of one rule disagree eventually. It also means a screen can be rebuilt or restyled without any chance of moving a number.

Superseded on 2026-10-06 by **A screen reads and renders, and computes only the plan in the operator's money and a pick's open trades in its sector**. What this entry ruled stands for every number but two: a pick's plan in the operator's money and its sixth line, which no store may hold and one function in the core works out where the card is drawn.

**The local lane loads its model at the context its settings name and waits for it to load, one model at a time** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-06: "Dont forget for it to wait for the model to load and not just fail while it's loading." A model the runtime has not loaded answers no section until it is, and asked for by name it is loaded on demand inside the call: Gemma 4 took 19.8 seconds so on this machine, at the runtime's own context of 32,000 tokens rather than the 50,176 its profile names, so a call bounded by a section's timeout fails on a model about to answer and one that does answer reads a context the lane did not plan its prompts for. So before a pass's first call the lane reads the runtime's own list of its models and, where its model is listed and not loaded, loads it at its profile's context and waits for the load to answer that it is loaded, which LM Studio sends only once it is, within the allowance the profile states; a load measured at 15.3 seconds against an allowance of 600 is the allowance bounding a runtime that has hung rather than one loading. Any other model loaded is unloaded first, so the runtime holds one model at a time, which is the operator's standing word on the machine's memory. A model already listed is loaded or loading, since the list names an instance from the moment its load starts, so it is not loaded again and its first call carries the load's allowance beside its own timeout; a runtime that answers no such list, being another runtime or one not answering it in time, is called the same way. A model the runtime does not hold, a load it refuses and one past its allowance are the local model unavailable, so the pass leaves every section of the lane unwritten with why rather than failing them one call at a time.

Superseded on 2026-10-06 by **The local lane loads its model at its profile's context and waits for the load before its first call of a night or a pass**. What this entry ruled stands for the load at the profile's context, its wait and its allowance; what moved is that the list is read once for a night's queue as for a pass, that every other model loaded is unloaded whatever its kind, that a failed load's reason is kept for the rest of the pass or the night rather than the load being tried again each round, and that an address the runtime cannot be asked at and a bound past a day are settings the lane cannot read.

**The local lane loads its model at its profile's context and waits for the load before its first call of a night or a pass** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-06: "Dont forget for it to wait for the model to load and not just fail while it's loading." A model the runtime has not loaded answers no section until it is, and asked for by name it is loaded on demand inside the call: Gemma 4 took 19.8 seconds so on this machine, at the runtime's own context of 32,000 tokens rather than the 50,176 its profile names, so a call bounded by a section's timeout fails on a model about to answer and one that does answer reads a context the lane did not plan its prompts for. So before its first call the lane reads the runtime's own list of its models and, where its model is listed and not loaded, loads it at its profile's context and waits for the load to answer that it is loaded, which LM Studio sends only once it is, within the allowance the profile states; a load measured at 15.3 seconds against an allowance of 600 is the allowance bounding a runtime that has hung rather than one loading. The list is read once for a pass on demand and once for a night's overnight queue, whose passes share one lane, so a night does not read it again between names. Any other model loaded, whatever its kind, is unloaded first, so the runtime holds one model at a time, which is the operator's standing word on the machine's memory. A model already listed is loaded or loading, since the list names an instance from the moment its load starts, so it is not loaded again and its first call carries the load's allowance beside its own timeout; a runtime that answers no such list, being another runtime or one not answering it in time, is called the same way. A model the runtime does not hold, a load it refuses and one past its allowance are the local model unavailable, so the pass or the queue leaves every section of the lane unwritten with why rather than failing them one call at a time, and the reason is kept for the rest of the pass or the night: a later round of the pass answers with it at once rather than loading again, since a load that failed once would otherwise be waited out again round after round. An address the runtime cannot be asked at, and a timeout or an allowance past a day, are settings the lane cannot read, as a missing value is.

Superseded on 2026-10-07 by **The local lane loads its model at its profile's context before its first call of a night or a pass, and loads again a model held at a smaller one**. What this entry ruled stands whole but for one case: a model listed at a smaller context than its profile's is unloaded and loaded again at the profile's rather than called as it stands, read off the context the runtime's list states for each instance.

**The local model answers at an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, and the lane calls the one model its settings flag as the default** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-06: "I want us to be able to use gemma4 in LM studio from now on", with every setting of the lane "load from settings", each model "a setting provision" of its own and "a flag maybe like isDefaultModel" to switch between them, "for now default it to gemma4", and the models switched by hand to compare them a day at a time. The local model is reached over HTTP at an OpenAI-compatible chat completions endpoint on the operator's own machine, which is the interface LM Studio, `llama.cpp`'s own server and Ollama all serve, so the runtime is the operator's choice and not the build's. Each model the operator runs is a profile of its own under the local lane's settings, named by a word and carrying its base address, its model identifier, the per-call timeout, the context it is loaded with and how long its load may take, and the lane calls the one profile flagged as the default. No value has a default in the code, where the address and the model measured on the machine this was first built for once stood: a model the code names is one the operator did not choose, and a switch that left a stale default in place would call it without saying so. So settings naming no profile, flagging none or flagging two refuse at startup in a line naming the profiles, and so does a flagged profile missing a value or a key the lane read before the profiles, which a lane that passed over it would leave silently not the model called. Gemma 4 is the default from 2026-10-06, with Qwen 3.5 9B, the model this was first built for, kept beside it (see: The local and paid boundary is a property of the machine, not of the design). The identifier is stored on every section the model wrote, which is what makes a comparison between two models a reading of the record rather than a memory of which was running, and why a switch needs no command of its own. A run over a capture calls the profile its recordings were made under, from the capture's own models file, so a switch of the shipped default moves no recorded test. A local call needs no key, and a key configured for the local lane, at the lane or in a profile, is refused rather than sent, because a local endpoint that authenticates is an endpoint on somebody else's machine and the queue's zero-cost property rests on this lane being free.

A local call needs no key, and a key configured for the local lane is refused rather than sent, because a local endpoint that authenticates is an endpoint on somebody else's machine and the queue's zero-cost property rests on this lane being free.

**A reason's record is displayed, beside the reason and never beside the name** Once a reason has enough resolved setups it shows the share that reached the target before the stop, the number resolved, and the break-even those setups demanded, always as the three together. That tells you which of tonight's reasons has a track record while you are reading. It is a property of the reason across every name it has ever fired for, so it is not placed next to a ticker, where it would be read as that stock's chance of going up.

Superseded on 2026-10-06 by **A rule's record is drawn under the rule's own heading, beside its reason or on a pick's card and never as the stock's own**. **The night asks for a report on the first six names its page draws** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: "the night writes at most 6 reports across all families, by the page's order, and the rest are written on the report press". After its close and its overnight queue the night writes one request for each of the first six names its page draws, in the page's order, the first family's picks before the next family's, each marked as asked by the night, a name holding a request outstanding or being written getting none, and the passes they lead to are the drain's own, paid at the off-peak rate as every pass is (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC). Six rather than one, since the page now draws up to five a family, and six rather than the whole page, since a report is written when a reader asks for it and the night's are the ones a reader is likeliest to open first. Measured on 2026-10-01 over the ten first reports the operator's store held, a report took 199 to 504 seconds to write, 302 at the median, and cost about five cents on the research profile, so six in a row end about half an hour after they are asked for at the median and about fifty minutes at the slowest, and cost about thirty cents a night against a day's cap of ten dollars. The night makes no model call for them and no request: it writes rows and starts one process, and the number of paid passes a night is bounded by the count and never by the index's size. The count is a constant section 17 pins. It replaces the ruling of 2026-09-23, which asked for the first name alone and is kept under Previously decided.

**The universe is the S&P 500, and membership is fetched, not maintained** A published index is a fixed list rather than a filtered one, which keeps this a reporting tool rather than a screen. Membership is stored with join and leave dates so a name added last month is not shown as present in an older window.

Superseded on 2026-10-05 by **The universe is the S&P 1500's three indices with each member tagged by its index, and membership is fetched**. **A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has a two-hour deadline it cannot move** Every feed interface accepts a cancellation token and nothing supplied one, so a night that hung on a socket hung until somebody looked. At most three attempts per request, the first two failures waited out for two seconds and then four, and each attempt bounded by a thirty-second timeout. Thirty seconds because the bulk file for a whole exchange is the largest thing the night fetches and is a few megabytes: far outside a healthy fetch and far inside the night. Three attempts because the failure a retry is for is a transient one, and a fourth attempt is a slower way of learning what the third said. What is retried is a refused connection, a timeout, a rejected rate and the provider's own fault statuses; what is not retried is a rejected key or a route the provider does not have, because a wrong key is wrong three times and the retry only delays the message that says so. The night as a whole carries a deadline of two hours, which is three times the forty-minute wall clock the limits table states for the three indices' members, so the deadline stops a night that has hung rather than one that is merely slow. At the deadline the night stops, names the step it was on, and exits non-zero. The operator ruled both figures on 2026-10-04 with phase 15's plan, "Wall clock 40 min, deadline 2 hours": the last four scheduled nights over the S&P 500's 503 names reached the close in 580, 616, 640 and 701 seconds, which scale by 1,506 over 503 to about 29 to 35 minutes over the three indices. The figures stood at twenty minutes and an hour from the operator's settlement of 2026-09-28 over ten nights of the S&P 500 alone, and the first five nights over the three indices settle the forty minutes.

Superseded on 2026-10-06 by **A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff and the night's news query waits ninety seconds a try, and the night has a two-hour deadline it cannot move**. What this entry ruled stands: three attempts with a doubling backoff, each bounded by thirty seconds, what is retried and what is not, and the deadline at three times the wall clock. What changed is the bound on each attempt of the night's news query, from thirty seconds to ninety, after the provider answered it in forty-five on a night whose news step it stopped.

**A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has an hour's deadline it cannot move** Every feed interface accepts a cancellation token and nothing supplied one, so a night that hung on a socket hung until somebody looked. At most three attempts per request, the first two failures waited out for two seconds and then four, and each attempt bounded by a thirty-second timeout. Thirty seconds because the bulk file for a whole exchange is the largest thing the night fetches and is a few megabytes: far outside a healthy fetch and far inside the night. Three attempts because the failure a retry is for is a transient one, and a fourth attempt is a slower way of learning what the third said. What is retried is a refused connection, a timeout, a rejected rate and the provider's own fault statuses; what is not retried is a rejected key or a route the provider does not have, because a wrong key is wrong three times and the retry only delays the message that says so. The night as a whole carries a deadline of an hour, which is three times the twenty-minute wall clock the limits table states for five hundred names, so the deadline stops a night that has hung rather than one that is merely slow. At the deadline the night stops, names the step it was on, and exits non-zero. The operator settled both figures on 2026-09-28, reading ten nights that ran on the schedule over the whole index, which took 363 to 665 seconds to the close with a median of 494: "how does it matter how long it takes. It's running locally." The wall clock had stood at a proposed five minutes, which every one of those nights ran past, and the deadline at fifteen, which the store's spinning disk had brought within four minutes of a night that was only slow.

Superseded on 2026-10-05 by **A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has a two-hour deadline it cannot move**. What this entry ruled stands: three attempts with a doubling backoff, each bounded by thirty seconds, what is retried and what is not, and the deadline at three times the wall clock. What changed is the wall clock, from twenty minutes over the S&P 500's members to forty over the three indices', so the deadline moves from an hour to two.

**The wider universe is not adopted, since no swing family's edge improves on it even on survivors** Answered on 2026-10-04 by the first test's run from main's own build, under the operator's rule for it (see: A wider universe is tested first on today's members, and widened only where a family's edge improves even so and holds on membership as it stood): today's 1,003 members of the S&P 400 and 600, 878 of them never held by the S&P 500, read beside the S&P 500's history over the 1,949 nights from 2019-01-02 to 2026-10-02. The pullback's base made an edge of 0.100 over 1,903 trades on the 500 alone and -0.017 over 5,354 on the 1,500, lower in every one of the 8 years; the breakout 0.082 over 1,009 and 0.067 over 2,454, higher in 3 of the 8 and 1 of the last 3; and the earnings drift 0.121 over 2,243 and -0.222 over 3,809, lower in every year. None passes the ideas' run's test, where luck alone would pass about 0.40 of the three tries. The plain result a trade fell on the 1,500 for all three as well, 0.224 to 0.168, 0.138 to 0.066 and 0.278 to 0.044, though a history of survivors flatters that figure most directly. So the widening is dropped: the universe stays the S&P 500, the provider's add-on for membership as it stood is not needed, and today's members and their history stay in the pulled history's tables under the pulls that wrote them, read by no night and removed whole only on the operator's command.

Superseded on 2026-10-04 by **The S&P 400 and 600 join the universe with rules of their own, since the first test showed only that the 500's settings do not carry to them**. What this entry recorded stands as the test's record, and every pulled table stays; what it concluded, that the universe stays the S&P 500, does not, on the operator's ruling with phase 15's brief that the test answered whether the 500's settings carry to the 1,500 and not whether the 400 and 600 can give good picks.

**A setup family's sweep replays its own rule over the stored history and proposes the best edge among the settings meeting its floors** Ruled with phase 13's plan on 2026-10-01, each family going live on provisional settings and swept after. A family's sweep reads the history the pullback's sweep reads, read-only, and replays the family's own rule over every scored session at each setting of a small grid around its provisional values, the market check at the live filter's floor closing every list, the nights walked in order as the page lists them, in the family's own order with its names a night and one open trade a stock, each trade bought at the close under the family's own exit and read as an edge over the same plan entered on every member that night. A grid holds tens of settings, so each is read whole, and the best edge among the settings meeting the pullback sweep's own floors is proposed, ties to the fewest dials moved from the provisional setting, with its one-step neighbours as its variants; no plateau is searched, since there are not millions of settings to fit. A family no setting of which meets the floors is set aside with its figures and keeps its provisional settings. The report states the test the family's checkpoints read from its freeze before anything is frozen, and nothing is registered until the operator's go.

Superseded on 2026-10-05 by **A setup family's sweep replays its own rule over the stored history and proposes the best edge among the settings meeting its floors, or brings the strongest where none does**. What this entry ruled stands but that a family no setting of which meets the floors is set aside: on the operator's ruling of 2026-10-05 no family is set aside by a test result without their word, and the report brings them the strongest settings and what could be tried next.

**A family runs on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: each new family goes live with provisional settings taken from the published evidence, "marked as not yet frozen", and its sweep over the stored history then proposes the starting point that replaces them at its freeze, on the operator's go. Until a registration stands for a family its card says, in the ruling's words, "provisional: not yet frozen; its record starts at the freeze", its picks are listed, scored and recorded like any other, and no trade of it counts toward any checkpoint, since a rule nobody registered was never pre-registered and its settings were chosen before any sweep. A family whose live rule stands registered draws the day that registration was made and the variants registered beside it, read off the register as it stood at the night's end. The freeze registers the family's rule and its variants at one instant, as the swing family's registration does, and a family whose sweep finds nothing steady is set aside with its figures, its card saying so.

Superseded on 2026-10-05 by **A family lists on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint**. What this entry ruled stands but its last clause: a family whose sweep finds no setting meeting its floors keeps listing on its provisional settings until the operator rules, on their ruling of 2026-10-05.

**A registration names an evaluator the code carries, and its version is the pin of every source its evaluation runs through** A registered candidate carries the name of an evaluator, the parameters it will be run with, and that evaluator's version, so the row says what will run rather than describing it. A rule written only in prose is re-implemented by whoever scores it, and what they implement is whatever they read the words to mean, which is the thing pre-registration exists to stop: the registration and the thing tested come apart with nothing able to say they have. The version is a pin rather than a number somebody raises, because a number is a second statement of the same fact and drifts the first time a parameter is tuned in a hurry, where a pin cannot. It is taken over the evaluator's own source and every source that decides a value the evaluator reads or the verdict it returns, from a stored bar's text to the verdict: the money and statistic crossings, the indicator arithmetic and the stage that stores its readings, the builder that hands an evaluation a name-night's readings and names the session before, the shadow column that runs it, the catalogue that resolves its name, and the shared evaluator code that reads a registration's parameters. They are listed beside the version in `CandidateEvaluator`, and `register-append-only` holds the list to the pin, to every shipped file that computes a reading or runs an evaluation, and to the types an evaluation calls. What decides only whether a name-night is evaluated at all, being the register's standing rule, the gap stop and the staleness read, is outside it, because a change there produces a skip or a different set of name-nights, each named on its row, and never a verdict under a rule the register does not name. A pin over the evaluator's own file left the reading of the session before, the parse of a registration's parameters and the arithmetic of the readings free to change what a registered rule fires on while the row still matched; the ladder rules' code version covers every source a replay runs through, as the operator ruled on 2026-09-16, and a registered candidate is held to the same reach. The pin is taken with line endings normalised to LF and any leading byte order mark removed, so the same code pins the same on both machines and on a runner that checked the tree out with either ending, and less the one line carrying the version, because a pin of a file including its own pin never settles. What this costs is that every change to any of those sources, down to a comment, moves every evaluator's version, and each standing candidate is skipped and named as a failure on the listings stage's run log row until it is retired and registered again under the version the code carries, under the same name or another. That is the cost being bought: a row naming code that has moved is a row naming a rule nothing runs, and a night that evaluated it would write evidence about a different rule under the registered one's name. Ruled at the 8.3 correction.

Superseded on 2026-10-04 by **A registration names an evaluator the code carries, and its version is the pin of every source its evaluation runs through but the catalogue**. What this entry ruled stands but one source: the catalogue that resolves an evaluator's name left the sources every evaluator pins, on the operator's ruling with phase 14's plan, in the move that added the sector heavyweights' evaluator.

**The night asks for the index's and the VIX's daily closes once a series, and keeps them apart from the members' bars** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-03 with the two variants that read a market switch every night the families are evaluated, the index's close against its 200-session average and the VIX's close against its close ten sessions before (see: The breakout and the earnings drift each register a variant listing only on nights its market switch is open, each family registered again whole and its records replayed). The night's fetch step, after the day's bulk file, asks the provider's historical endpoint once for each series, `GSPC` and `VIX` under its index exchange, over the 400 days before the night's session, two requests at a weight of one a night whatever the index's size, and stores each session no night has stored in `market_bar`, its open, high, low and close as sent and kept as text, with the run that first stored it; a session held keeps its first row, and the table is kept whole, two rows a session. A table of its own and not the bar store, because neither series is a member's and nothing that reads a member's bars should find them; and not the pulled history, which no night reads and a purge removes whole. Only the family evaluator reads it, handing each registered rule the two series' closes on the store's own sessions, so a day the VIX is quoted on that is no session is passed over as the ideas' run passed it. A series the provider refuses, does not answer in time, sends nothing for or answers in a form that cannot be read stores nothing, is named on the stage's own row and stops nothing: a switch reading it is closed on a night it cannot be read, as a gate fails on an absent value, and the night goes on, since only the registered rules that state a switch read either series. The first hard rule says so, and the night's cost still does not grow with the universe.

Superseded on 2026-10-04 by **The night asks for the market series' daily closes once a series, and keeps them apart from the members' bars**. What this entry ruled stands for the index and the VIX. The sector heavyweights' freeze added the eleven sector funds, whose held sessions are written again where the provider restates a close for a dividend, and the heavyweights' book as a second reader.

**The market check closes every family's list together** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01. The night's breadth, the share of the members closing above their own 200-day average, is read against one floor, the open filter version's, and a night it falls below that floor lists no stock under any family: every card is drawn and says the check closed every list, with the breadth and the floor. One check for every family rather than one a family, because the evidence it rests on is about the market a trade is opened in and not about a kind of setup, and a page on which one setup is open and another shut on the same breadth would be read as a view on setups the night does not hold.

**A stock holds one trade across every family, and one qualifying under two is listed once under the first in the page's order** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01: "One stock, one trade, across all families: a stock with an open trade from any family is listed by none. A stock qualifying under two families on one night is listed once, under the first in the page's order, carrying both labels, and counted once." A trade is the one the page listed: bought at that night's close on the listing family's plan, and open until that plan's exit or its sessions run out, read by the rule that already reads an open trade, a stop's night included and a missing outcome read as open until its cap's sessions have passed (see: A stock holds one open trade on each rule's list, and it is free the night after its trade ends). The lister reads every trade a list made on an earlier night, the pullback's from the nights the swing filter listed before the families among them, and lists no stock whose trade is still open, storing for each family that passed it a row naming the family and the night that listed the open trade, which that family's card says in a note. A stock two families pass on one night is listed under the first of them in the page's order that has a place left, its row carrying the other's label; the later family stores it as listed under another and gives it none of its own five places, so the night's count of buy points counts it once. A stock past the five of an earlier family is not listed by that family and may be listed by a later one it qualified under, since it is on no card. What this rules is the page's list. A family's own record, the one its checkpoints read, counts every trade its own rule would have made, the ones the page gave to another family among them, each rule walking its own open trades: a record that depended on what another family listed would move every family's record whenever one family's rule changed.

**The breakout and the earnings drift each register a variant listing only on nights its market switch is open, and each family is registered again whole to add it** The operator ruled on 2026-10-03, from the three reports of 2026-10-02 and 03: "register two variants through the remedy script, stating what restarts: breakout: only on nights the index closes above its 200-session average (edge 0.082 to 0.100, held without the five largest, cut 2022's loss); earnings drift: only on nights the VIX closes under its close ten sessions before (edge 0.121 to 0.221, and 0.159 without the five largest). Both failed only on yearly totals because they sit out nights, which the live record will weigh." Each variant is its family's live rule at the frozen settings with one market switch and nothing else moved, read as the ideas' run on the frozen families read it: the index's close above the average of its 200 closes ending on the night, and the VIX's close under its close 10 sessions before, each on the store's own sessions and closed on a night it cannot be read. A switch is a setting of the registration, a count of sessions and nought where the rule states none, read after the family's own gates as a gate of its own, so every registration of either family states both, and the fourteen rules the freezes registered are the same rules stating none. The night asks for both series to read it (see: The night asks for the index's and the VIX's daily closes once a series, and keeps them apart from the members' bars). The switch moves both families' evaluators, so each family is registered again whole at one instant, its seven retired and eight registered, the seven under their names and settings and the eighth the switched variant, as the pullback's family was to add its ninth rule. What restarts is stated: a rule's record counts from the first session on or after the day it registered, so every rule of both families counts from 2026-10-05 in place of 2026-10-02, the one night the freezes' rules were scored on; the breakout's seven kept no trade that night, and two drift variants kept one each, which stay stored and leave their rules' blocks. Each family's rules are tested at 0.05 over eight in place of 0.05 over seven, and no pullback rule, filter version or clock moves. The ideas' test had failed each on more than its totals: the breakout's switch was better in two of the eight years and the same in five, and its 963 trades fell under the 1,000 the test asks, which the family as frozen clears by nine; the drift's switch lowered its plain result a trade from 0.278 to 0.241, its edge rising because the benchmark on the nights it kept fell from 0.157 to 0.020. The live record weighs each as it weighs every variant.

**Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most eight a family** Ruled with phase 13's plan on 2026-10-01 and binding from the freezes. Each setup family is a rule of its own with its own record and checkpoints, tried over its own question, so the correction for the chance that one of several rules clears its test by luck divides by the rules that family tests together and by no other family's: the divisor, the distinct trials Holm's first step counts and the graph the level passes along are each read over the candidates registered under the family's own evaluator, the pullback's being the swing filter's. Eight stood for the whole register while one family was registered, and stays the most a family registers, its live rule and its variants together. This narrows the candidate family's maximum and Holm's first step to one setup family and supersedes neither, since within one family both read as they always have (see: The candidate family is at most eight and the threshold is divided by it) (see: Holm's level passes between the candidates by a graph fixed when they are registered, and its first step is 0.05 over the distinct trials read at a look or still running).

Superseded on 2026-10-02 by **Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family**. Each family counting its own rules alone stands whole. What changed is the most a family registers, nine in place of eight, on the operator's ruling registering the night's best three as the pullback's ninth rule.

**The candidate family is at most eight and the threshold is divided by it** The maximum family size is a convention and no count of resolved setups measures it, so it is decided rather than carried: with five worthless candidates the chance that one clears an ordinary test is about 23 per cent and with twenty it is about 64, and eight is the size at which the correction stays a correction rather than a bar no candidate could clear. The six live reasons are a family of their own, registered by section 11 before the first listing night, and registered candidates are the other; a candidate promoted to live joins the live family, which restarts the window the live reasons are measured over. Ruled at 8.0.

Superseded on 2026-10-02 by **Each setup family's correction for luck counts its own rules alone, at most nine a family**. The maximum as a convention, the threshold divided by the family and the live reasons as a family of their own stand whole. What changed is the size, nine in place of eight, and the family it is counted over, each setup family's own, which phase 13 had ruled beside this entry.

**A sector leader is a stock in the top quarter of a top three sector, bought at the pullback's buy point** Ruled by the operator on 2026-10-01 as the fourth setup family: "sectors ranked by median 126-session return, top 3 of 11, stocks in the top quarter of their sector, entry, stop and target as the pullback's". Read as written, the rule has no buy point: about 33 stocks qualify every night and the same ones the next, measured over the pulled history, so a card of them would be a standing list and not a night's setups. So "entry, stop and target as the pullback's" is read to include the pullback's buy point, which was put to the operator with the plan and built on their word to start: a member passes where the market check is open, its sector is among the top 3 of those ranked, its own return is in the top quarter of its sector's, and the swing filter's setup, trigger and trade gates passed for it that night as the filter stored them. Sector leadership stands in place of the filter's trend and strength gate and of nothing else, so a leader the filter's own trend gate failed is passed, and one the pullback passes too is listed under the pullback with this family's label. Over the 8 stored gate nights that lists 0, 0, 1, 1, 3, 5, 2 and 2, all but 2 of those 14 stocks ones the pullback does not list. The sectors are ranked on the median of their members' returns over the 126 sessions the swing readings already store, highest first and the sector's name where two tie; a sector holding fewer than 5 members with a return is not ranked, a floor that binds on none of the eleven the membership names; and the top quarter is the first of every 4 of a sector's members by return, rounded up, the ticker deciding a tie. The names passing are ordered by the sector's rank and then by the stock's own return. Its trade is the pullback's plan as the filter's row stored it under the open version's trade input, so it is scored on that row and no second outcome is written for it, and it carries the exclusions the filter stored for that plan, earnings inside the holding window among them, less the pullback's own open trade, which is the page's to hold back. The evidence is Moskowitz and Grinblatt (1999), the top three of twenty industries by six-month return beating the bottom three and industry momentum explaining much of single-stock momentum; the 3 of eleven, the quarter and the buy point are judgement, and every setting is provisional until the family's freeze (see: A family runs on provisional settings until its freeze, and nothing before the freeze counts toward a checkpoint).

Superseded on 2026-10-02 by **The sector leaders are a variant of the pullback's starting point and not a family of their own**. The reading of the rule stands whole: sectors ranked by their members' median long return, a member in the top quarter of one of the top 3, at the pullback's buy point with leadership in place of the trend and strength gate. What changed is where it lives: its sweep found its one qualifying setting isolated, so it is registered as a variant of the pullback and is no family with a card of its own.

**Each print's reaction is read from the nightly calendar and the stored bars, and reaches no reason, gate or plan** The calendar is fetched for the whole index in one request (see: A calendar event is fetched once for the whole index, and the calendar holds provider events only), which makes it the bulk source the fundamentals ruling says would be a new decision, so this is recorded as a new decision and not a reopening of that ruling (see: No reading of the fundamentals fires a reason, gates a tranche or draws a panel). A print with no filed estimate shows its actual and says no estimate was filed, and is never counted as a meet. A print's reaction session is the one the earnings rule takes, read by calling the rule's own function one print at a time, so a print whose timing the provider left unstated is handled exactly as the earnings rule handles it and no pinned source is edited to share the reading. The earnings rule itself keeps its two prints. The operator ruled it on 2026-09-23, one of two additions an SNPS report asked for.

Superseded on 2026-10-01 by **Each print's reaction is read from the nightly calendar and the stored bars, and the earnings drift is the one rule that reads it**. How a reaction is read stands whole. What changed is who reads it: the operator ruled a setup family that buys a beat with a strong reaction, so the record reaches one rule's gates and its plan, where this entry kept it to display.

**The night asks for a report on the first name of its list** After its close the night writes one request for the first name drawn on its list, marked as asked by the night, and the pass it leads to is the drain's own, paid at the off-peak rate as every pass is (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC). One name rather than the whole list, on the operator's ruling of 2026-09-23, "just the top 1st name for now": the name a reader is likeliest to open holds its report when they open it, and any other is asked for. A pass cost between $0.04 and $0.05 over the three the operator's store held that day, so the count is set by what a reader needs rather than by the cap. The count is a constant section 17 pins from 11.4.

Superseded on 2026-10-01 by **The night asks for a report on the first six names its page draws**. **A starting point is proposed from the centre of a plateau of the stored history and never its best variation, and nothing is registered before the operator approves it** Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-29, in their words: "I am not backtesting with the goal of finding a winning plan and fix it indefinitely. I want to backtest with numerous variations, as many as possible, to find a starting point. And from this starting point we start the evaluation on live data for the future without overfitting to the past." The sweep replays the swing filter over every session the store and the pulled history hold from 2019-01-02, the year before it warm-up for the twelve-month return, across 46,656 designs, each a set of structural choices, and nine dials. Stage 1 reads every design at three values of each dial, 918,330,048 variations, and ranks the designs by the share of their settings that beat both their break-even and no skill in at least 6 of the 8 years with at least 300 trades; stage 2 reads the five strongest over a fine grid of 2,016,000 settings each. Among that many variations some do well over one history by chance, and the best single one is the likeliest of them to be that, so no variation is chosen for its own record. A plateau is a region where a setting and at least three quarters of its one-step neighbours beat both, and the proposal is the setting on a plateau whose neighbours' median result is highest, holding four floors: beating its break-even in 6 of the 8 years and with its best year removed, 300 trades in 22 of the 30 blocks of 63 sessions, and a stock listed on 60% of nights. A variant moves one setting to a value on the plateau, is open in history with neither side winning more than 5 of the 8 years, picks at least a quarter of its stocks where the starting point does not, and holds 30 trades in every year; up to six are proposed, spread across the filter's parts. The history holds only the departures the provider still serves bars for, and the missing ones are mostly names that failed, so the report states the share of index-nights with no bar served beside its figures and, above 3%, says plainly that the results read better than the market was. The trigger's arrival is its event's alone, as the live trigger reads it, so no dial feeds it and it is one reading per design. The sweep proposes and registers nothing: the starting point, the variants and the four floors are put to the operator in its report, and the freeze that registers them is one registration at one instant on the operator's word.

Superseded on 2026-09-30 by **A starting point is proposed from the deepest setting of a plateau on the edge and never its best variation, and nothing is registered before the operator approves it**. **A retry names each thing the check refused, and a second draft repeating one is left out** A section's one retry is told each thing its first draft was refused for, a line each, with what to do about it, and never the refused draft itself. Until the operator's research template of 2026-09-29 a retry was told the checker's reason in one sentence, and ECL's short version of that day was refused for one date, then written again carrying six figures the facts file does not hold, several of them from other sections' refused first drafts, and left out. So the refused draft is read again by the checker's own rules over the facts file and the source list it was checked against, which gives the findings the checker stored without a column to keep them in, and each finding becomes a line naming the figure, date, citation or sentence, the sentence it sits in, the rule, and what a second draft does: a figure or a date removed or replaced by one the facts file lists, a sentence citing nothing removed or ended on the marker of the document stating it, each other rule its own instruction. The paid lane, the local lane and the theme pass retry this way. A second refusal leaves the section out, as it did, and where the second draft carries a thing the first was refused for, by the same rule over the same text read without case, its reason says the retry repeated it, so the name page and the run page tell a writer that ignored the line from one that failed on something new. The bound is one retry a pass, unchanged. (see: A claim is a sentence, every sentence in a researched section names the document it rests on, and a window written in words is read as its number)

Superseded on 2026-09-30 by **A retry names each thing the check refused, and a section refused on its third retry is left out**. **A pass the model could not be reached for on a section of the report is not the day's report** The day's report, which a second plain open and a second regenerate on the same day are both held to, is a pass that ran to the end, and a pass the cap paused or the model did not answer never counted as one. A pass the model answered for most of its calls and could not be reached for on one or more of the report's own calls counted until 2026-09-30, because its outcome read as written. That day the connection to the provider was cut on every answer taking longer than a minute (see: A paid model on the OpenAI format is asked to stream its answer, because a connection silent for a minute is cut), and BDX, MO and IT each lost the two cases, BDX its risks as well, while each pass wrote the rest. Told that the rule refused a second report on each until the next day in New York, the operator chose to lift it for the three. It is lifted by what the three share rather than by their names: a pass whose run log holds a call of the report's own that could not be reached is not the day's report, so a regenerate or a plain open the same day runs, and a report left with a section lost to the connection is never the one the day keeps. A call a trial or a review made is no part of the report, and one that could not be reached leaves the pass the day's. A section refused by the claim checker, declined for lack of sources or cut at its budget is the model's answer, and the pass stays the day's report, because asking again the same day would pay for the same answer twice, which is what the rule is for (see: A regenerated report is written whole by the paid model from the company's figures as they stand on the day it runs, once a name a day) (see: A name opened again on the day its research pass ran starts no second pass unless the page asks for one).

**The run page draws how each report's sections came out and each section's rates over the newest twenty reports** Ruled in the same pass, from the brief's item 5: the operator could not see from the run page which sections keep failing. A report is a research pass whose paid model answered at least once, and the run page draws, in its detail, one row for each report of the seven nights the research region reads, with a cell for each section: passed first time where its accepted version was the pass's first draft, passed on retry where it was the retry, left out with the line the pass's row names it by or the checker's refusal as the name page words it, and not warranted where it stood from an earlier day. Each cell carries what that section's own calls cost, every round, and the report what all its calls cost; a trial's calls are in neither, being spend beside the report. The two cases' cell is marked where any of the pass's drafts carried a figure on both sides, the first as well as the retry, since what is counted is what the ask produced and not what the checker let through, and the page states how many of the newest twenty reports that drafted the two cases carried one, which is the count the carried obligation reports at. Each section's share passed first time and share left out are read over the newest twenty reports that warranted it. Twenty is a display window and not a measured number: one report moves a rate by a twentieth, and a change to a section's model or ask shows within a few weeks of reports. Where fewer have been written the count read is stated beside each rate. The sections a trial asked for are drawn beneath, each with both models' outcomes, rounds and costs and the two drafts side by side, and nothing is drawn while no trial has written a row.

Superseded on 2026-09-30 by **The run page draws how each report's sections came out and each section's rates over the newest twenty reports, and no trial's drafts**.
