**A trial asks a second profile for named sections after a report and records its drafts beside the report, never in it** Ruled in the same pass. Choosing a model for a section wants the two models' drafts over the same request side by side, and a trial that wrote into the report would change what the page draws on the strength of a draft nobody had yet compared. So a trial names a profile, the sections it is asked for, the reports it runs over and the day it counts them from. After a pass writes one of those sections in its paid lane at the first draft, the trial asks its profile with the request the pass built for that draft, the same facts file and documents and, for the short version, the sections the pass had accepted, and checks the answer in memory by the claim checker's own rules, told once what was refused as a pass's retry is. It writes no research section; it writes a run log row a section under the pass's run holding each round's draft, verdict and price, the outcome and the cost, and its calls stand under a round of their own, which the run page leaves out of a report's count and cost and the spend caps count as they count every call. It stops by itself once it has run over the reports it names, so its cost is bounded by that number rather than by attention: as shipped, Claude Sonnet 5.5 with thinking off for the short version and the two cases over three reports, at most four calls a report. After a pass writes the two cases or the risks at the first draft, a review asks the profile it names, as the addendum puts it a second call to the model that wrote the draft, with the request the pass built and the draft handed back, to read it a sentence at a time: a reason rather than a restated figure, the size of a change weighed, specific to this company, and for a risk a confirmation that is what the risk coming true would look like; and to answer with the whole section revised in the form the section asks for. The revision is read by the claim checker's own rules in memory, told once what was refused as a retry is, and written beside the report on the run log and never into it, as a trial's drafts are, under a stage and a round of its own that the run page leaves out of a report's count and cost. Superseded on 2026-09-30 by **A review asks a section's model to check its own draft against the section's rules, beside a stated number of reports**. What this entry ruled stands but the profile it shipped naming: the operator reviewed the addendum's pull request and ruled the review on, naming DeepSeek.

**A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has a deadline it cannot move** Every feed interface accepts a cancellation token and nothing supplied one, so a night that hung on a socket hung until somebody looked. At most three attempts per request, the first two failures waited out for two seconds and then four, and each attempt bounded by a thirty-second timeout. Thirty seconds because the bulk file for a whole exchange is the largest thing the night fetches and is a few megabytes: far outside a healthy fetch and far inside the night. Three attempts because the failure a retry is for is a transient one, and a fourth attempt is a slower way of learning what the third said. What is retried is a refused connection, a timeout, a rejected rate and the provider's own fault statuses; what is not retried is a rejected key or a route the provider does not have, because a wrong key is wrong three times and the retry only delays the message that says so. The night as a whole carries a deadline of fifteen minutes, which is three times the wall clock the limits table states for five hundred names, so the deadline stops a night that has hung rather than one that is merely slow. At the deadline the night stops, names the step it was on, and exits non-zero.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A feed is tried three times with a doubling backoff, and the night has an hour's deadline it cannot move**. What this entry ruled stands but the deadline's length: the operator settled the wall clock at twenty minutes and the deadline at an hour on 2026-09-28 from ten nights that ran on the schedule.

**The trailing stop is the higher of the band beneath and the last swing low** In a range the stop is the range floor, which is the low edge of the next support band beneath the tranche; in an uptrend it trails, and what it trails to is the higher of that band and the most recent swing low beneath the tranche; in a downtrend there are no tranches. Kaminski and Lo, Journal of Financial Markets, 2014, show a stop reduces expected return under a random walk and can add value where returns trend, so one stop rule for both cases is wrong in one of them. Taking the higher of the two rather than the swing low alone is what keeps the trailing half meaningful. Read literally, trailing the last higher low puts the stop wherever that low happens to be, which on a name that has run a long way is far below the band beneath and is looser protection than the range rule gives, and a trailing stop that can sit below the range floor is not trailing anything. The committed fixture holds the case: MSFT's third tranche has its last swing low a hundred points under the band, and the literal reading would put the stop there.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **The trailing stop is the higher of the band beneath and the last swing low, and a stop inside a support band moves to that band's low edge**. What this entry ruled stands but where the stop lands when the swing low it trails to sits inside a support band, which the operator ruled on 2026-09-26 moves to that band's low edge.

**A ladder rule's version is measured beside that rule's live window, and both count against the bound** A version of a ladder rule is opened only while that rule's live window is open, under exactly the parameter names the rule is replayed from, and a live window is not closed while a version of its rule stays open. The live window is the one the night compares against the build's own hash, and a version replays through the same code the live rule runs through, so a version opened with no live window beside it is a measurement whose subject can move with nothing to stop the night: the code changes, the version's scores become scores of something else, and nothing says so. A parameter name the rule does not read is refused because the replay falls back to the live value for a missing name, which would store the live rule's plans under a version's name. Every open window counts against the caps, live ones included, and the caps are per rule: two windows of the merge distance, whose versions replay the level stage at 148 seconds on the night of 2026-09-14, and four of each other rule, whose versions replay the ladder stage at 5. That puts the fullest register the caps admit at one level replay and twelve ladder replays, 208 seconds on a 495 second night, inside the deadline of 900, where the bound as 8.0 wrote it and 8.6 built it, four windows of every rule and fourteen at once with no live window required, admitted four level replays and ten ladder replays, a night of 1,137 seconds, and stated 688 as though it were the end. The alternative was a bound on projected seconds rather than on windows, which would move every time the night's stage durations did and leave a person unable to say in advance whether a version would be admitted; a count of windows can be read off the verb's own list. The caps are the operator's ruling in the third round of the phase 8 plan's review, approved on 2026-09-15: two windows of the merge distance, four of where the stop sits and four of the near-exit skip, each rule's live window counted and no extra replay, ten at once and a night of 673 seconds, with four of every rule rejected at 969. The zone edges rule arrived after that round, from 8.0's ruling on a tranche zone's edges, and carries the cap of the other rules that replay the ladder stage alone, which made fourteen; the trend rule arrived at 10.4 on the same terms and makes eighteen (see: The trend rule is a fifth ladder rule a version replays, and none of its three versions is live). The caps per rule are the ones ruled in 2026-09-15 and the sum is arithmetic over them, so a rule joining the set moves the total and settles nothing here. Three windows of the merge distance would put the night at 836 seconds, 64 inside the deadline on a projection from one night's stage durations, and the bound's operating row is what sets the caps from the scorer's own nights. A version is opened only beside its rule's live window because that ruling counts the live window inside each cap and the plan's enforcement hashes each live rule against its open window. The caps are ruled by the operator on 2026-09-15; the zone edges cap and the live window requirement were written here at the 8.6 correction.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A ladder rule's version is measured beside that rule's live window, and both count against a bound of eighteen**. What this entry ruled stands but the deadline of 900 seconds and that the scorer's nights would set the caps: the operator settled the caps and their sum on 2026-09-28 with the deadline at an hour.

**The ladder rules' code version pins every source a live ladder rule or its replay runs through** The code version hashed into every window is the pin of every source file the compiled code runs through from where each side reads a ladder rule's inputs and applies it: the level builder's bands, the ladder builder's plan, the scorer's replay and the reads it replays from, and the hash the night compares. A test derives that set from the build's call graph and symbols and holds the scorer's list to it, so a rule or a helper that moves into a file the list does not name fails the check rather than leaving the code a window measures unpinned. The merge distance is one constant the level builder applies and the scorer hashes, so the value a live window carries is the value the night applies. A file is pinned whole wherever the walk reaches a method in it, which pins the swing and trend arithmetic that share files with the records both sides read; the indicator engine, the swing finder, the volume profile builder and the trend classifier are not reached and are not pinned, because they write the stored rows both sides read, and a change there moves the live rule's inputs and every version's alike, so a window still compares two rules over one set of inputs. The cost is stated rather than narrowed: an edit to any pinned file, a comment or a decision citation included, is a new code version, and every night with a live window open then stops at the rule versions step, before the close and the queue, until each open window of every rule is closed and opened again, because one code version is hashed into every rule's window. A pin blind to comments would be a second pinning rule beside the evaluator's, and a pin narrowed to the arithmetic would miss the reads that decide what a replay is given, so the cost is made visible instead: `version --list` names each live window the build no longer hashes to, and the runbook says to close the windows before such an edit merges. Ruled by the operator on 2026-09-16.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **The ladder rules' code version pins every source a live ladder rule or its replay runs through, its comments and blank lines aside**. What this entry ruled stands but that a comment or a decision citation is a new code version: the operator ruled on 2026-09-28 that all three pins read past comments and blank lines, which makes a comment-blind pin the one rule rather than a second.

**A paid job names its model profile in one word, and a profile is priced at its configured rates at its call's own timestamp** The operator ruled on 2026-09-28 that each job calling a paid model names the profile it uses by one word, and that changing the word is the whole of switching its model. The shipped configuration holds one profile per model under `EquityBrief:Models:Profiles`: its wire format, the address its provider answers at, the model, the section of the secrets file its key sits under, any options the provider takes with a request, the provider's rates, and the earliest date the provider publishes for retiring the model with the day that was read. Each job's own section holds `Use`, the one word naming its profile, beside the job's answer budget and how long one of its calls may take, which belong to the job because a label and a report section need budgets of their own. The research job names `claude-sonnet` from 2026-09-29, on the operator's answer that research moves to Claude Sonnet 5.5 and stays there. None of it is in code, and the feed that sends a request is named for its wire format rather than for anybody's model. A call's price is its response's own counts at its profile's rates: the prompt tokens read from the provider's cache, those neither read nor written, those written to it where the provider charges apart for a write, and the output, multiplied where the call's own instant falls in a peak window the profile names, because the provider's clock decides what it bills and this machine's does not. DeepSeek's profile names two windows and Claude's none. The instant is the creation time the OpenAI format carries in its body, and the `Date` header of Claude's interface, whose body carries none. Reasoning is billed as output and both formats count it inside the output, so nothing is added for it. A model with no prices is refused at startup rather than called, because a call recorded as costing nothing is spend the cap cannot see, and a response carrying no counts is refused for the same reason. The identity a section records is the model with the options it was asked with, because the same model asked two ways is two writers and the recording key has to tell them apart. DeepSeek's rates are its page as read on 2026-09-13 at 14:57 UTC, and Claude's are https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing as read on 2026-09-29 at about 02:45 UTC; when a provider moves a price, the profile is what a change edits, and a call already made keeps the price its run log row carries. The scan that keeps a model off the night reads every shipped source file for the provider's name and for each format's wire, and finds them in the feeds alone. (see: Code owns every number) (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC) (see: A paid model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, and a job never falls back from the profile it names)

Supersedes **The research model is named only in configuration, and a call is priced at the configured rates its own timestamp falls in**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that one research model is all configuration names.

**A local model writes prose from numbers and each paid job names its hosted model in configuration** A local model on the operator's GPU writes prose from numbers, which is small and free. A hosted model does research, which is a large-context job a local model does badly. Which hosted model a paid job uses is the profile its configuration names, so another is chosen by changing one word and not by superseding an entry here (see: A paid job names its model profile in one word, and a profile is priced at its configured rates at its call's own timestamp). The shipped configuration named DeepSeek V4 Flash for research from 6.7, chosen on cost at a few cents a pass. On 2026-09-29 the operator moved research to Claude Sonnet 5.5 and kept it there: a pass priced from the last six DeepSeek passes' own token counts at Sonnet 5.5's rates comes to 0.84 to 1.30 dollars against the 0.04 to 0.05 they cost, an upper figure since DeepSeek counts its reasoning as output, and the first five reports' actual costs are recorded against it (see: A report costing more than two dollars is named on the run page the morning after it was written). Every section records the model that wrote it, so a change of model shows on each section written after it and on none written before (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole).

Supersedes **Two models for two jobs, and which research model answers is configuration**, moved below with its reasoning, all of which stands but that the shipped configuration names DeepSeek V4 for research.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A local model writes prose from numbers and each paid job names the hosted model the operator chooses in configuration**. What this entry ruled stands but that research was kept on Claude Sonnet 5.5: the operator moved it back to DeepSeek the same day, and the first five reports on Claude Sonnet 5.5 are counted over whichever days research names it.

**Every paid call is made through the spend cap, which holds the research model** The decisions above say research pauses at the cap and how the cap is counted, and neither says where the check sits, which is the choice that decides whether the cap can be passed at all. Checked by each runner before it calls the model, the cap holds only while every caller remembers to ask, and the runners are two components written at two checkpoints. So the cap is a component, and it is the only one that holds the research model: a runner hands it a request and gets back an answer or a refusal, and has no model of its own to call instead. Before a call it reads what the run log says was spent today and this month and judges the call by the most it could cost; after it, it writes what the call did cost on a run log row of its own, under the pass's run, one row per section asked. The ledger the cap reads is therefore the rows it wrote, measured off the store rather than a figure a runner kept, and a pass stopped at the cap is a row the run page shows rather than a pass that quietly did nothing. The alternative of a check inside each runner was rejected for the reason a feed holds its client: the rule belongs where the thing it governs happens. (see: The spend cap is a stop, not an allowance) (see: The spend cap counts a UTC day and a UTC month, and refuses a call that could take spend past either)

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **Every paid call is made through the spend cap, which holds each paid job's model**. What this entry ruled stands but that the one model the cap holds is the research model's: each paid job's calls go through a cap holding the model its profile names, all counted against the same two caps.

**The research model is named only in configuration, and a call is priced at the configured rates its own timestamp falls in** A cap denominated in money needs a price for every call, and the price is the provider's arithmetic rather than this system's: its published rates, per model, for cached prompt tokens, uncached ones and output, multiplied inside the peak windows it fixes. The decision this replaces put the provider's name, its models, its rates and its wire in one feed file, which made changing model a change to code and made a price the provider moves a constant somebody edits. So none of it is in code. The shipped configuration names the wire format, the address the provider answers at, the model, any options the provider takes with a request, the answer's budget, and the rates: dollars per million cached prompt tokens, uncached prompt tokens and output tokens, the UTC hours and the days in which they are multiplied, and the multiple. A different provider or model is different values in that file or in the secrets file beside it, and the feed that sends the request is named for its wire format rather than for anybody's model. A call's price is its response's own counts at those rates, multiplied where the response's own creation instant falls in a peak window, because the provider's clock decides what it bills and this machine's does not. Reasoning is billed as output and a provider in this format counts it inside the completion, so nothing is added for it; a cached prompt is read from the provider's own field where it sends one and from the format's where it does not. A model with no prices is refused at startup rather than called, because a call recorded as costing nothing is spend the cap cannot see, and a response carrying no counts is refused for the same reason. The identity a section records is the model with the options it was asked with, because the same model asked two ways is two writers and the recording key has to tell them apart. The shipped values are the provider's page as read on 2026-09-13 at 14:57 UTC, for the model the shipped configuration names; when the provider moves a price, the configuration is what a change edits, and a call already made keeps the price its run log row carries. The scan that keeps a model off the night still reads every shipped source file for the provider's name, and now finds it in none. (see: Code owns every number) (see: Queued work runs off-peak, and every schedule is written in UTC) (see: The research model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, chosen by configuration and never falling back)

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A paid job names its model profile in one word, and a profile is priced at its configured rates at its call's own timestamp**. What this entry ruled stands but that configuration names one research model: it names a profile per model and each paid job's one word, the prices are the profile's with a rate for a cache write where the provider charges one, and the instant of a Claude answer is its response's `Date` header.

**Two models for two jobs, and which research model answers is configuration** A local model on the operator's GPU writes prose from numbers, which is small and free. A hosted model does research, which is a large-context job a local model does badly. Which hosted model is a setting rather than a decision, so another is chosen by changing configuration and not by superseding an entry here (see: The research model is named only in configuration, and a call is priced at the configured rates its own timestamp falls in). The shipped configuration names DeepSeek V4, chosen on cost: a research pass is a few cents rather than a few tens of cents, which puts a year of normal use in the tens of dollars. The tier, Pro or Flash, is left to the fixture comparison rather than decided here, because the difference between them is a few dollars a year and the only thing worth choosing on is the quality of the two-cases section; the shipped configuration names Flash until that comparison runs. Every section records the model that wrote it, so a change of model shows on each section written after it and on none written before (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole).

Supersedes **Two models for two jobs, and the research model is DeepSeek V4**, moved below with its reasoning.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A local model writes prose from numbers and each paid job names its hosted model in configuration**. What this entry ruled stands but that the shipped configuration names DeepSeek V4 for research: the operator moved research to Claude Sonnet 5.5 on 2026-09-29, and a paid job other than research names its own profile.

**The research model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, chosen by configuration and never falling back** The endpoint was already configuration and that is not enough, because two research providers do not share a request shape, so the choice of provider is a code path even where the address is not (see: Two models for two jobs, and the research model is DeepSeek V4). The research model is one interface with one implementation per wire format, and configuration names which provider answers. It is resolved in the one place the on-demand feeds are resolved, refusing in both directions, and it never falls back from one provider to the other. A fallback would make the model that wrote a section unknowable from the fact that the section exists, and every section is already dated and stamped with the model that wrote it for exactly that reason (see: A research record is written and dated per section, not as a whole). A provider named in configuration with no key refuses at startup rather than at the first pass, which is the shape the credential path has carried since 2.1. The switch is also what makes the lane boundary measurable rather than asserted, because the recording is keyed on the canonicalised request, so one section over one stored evidence set through two providers is two records a reader can set side by side.

Superseded on 2026-09-29 by **A paid model is one interface with an implementation per wire format, and a job never falls back from the profile it names**. **A night's state is read off its own run log rows, and the pages that state it read that one state** The operator asked on 2026-09-28 for the Run page to open on how last night went, and for a notice on tonight's page saying whether the night is running, waiting to try again or left unfinished; the page must read "the same state the retry notice is being built on, never a second source". A night's runs are the ones whose id is a night's, a pass of the overnight queue excepted, and the newest by the instant its id carries is the one read. Its state is finished where that run closed its arithmetic; stopped where it recorded a stop before the close, with the step and the reason it wrote; running where it recorded neither and its last row falls inside the night's deadline; left unfinished where its last row is older than the deadline and no step said why, which is a process ended from outside, a machine asleep among the causes; and where no run of the night is stored, not yet run on the session the clock is in, never ran on a session before it, and no session on a day the exchange did not trade. A stop after the close, in the quarters step, the overnight queue or the report, is said beside a finished night and does not unfinish it, since the list was drawn. The four headline figures are read off the same rows: the stocks the close counted, the provider requests every run of the night made, the spend every row of the night recorded, and the stops earlier runs of the night wrote before the one read. The alternative was a state the night writes for itself, and it is not taken, because a night that dies cannot write that it died, and the run log is already what every stage writes when it ends. Only one reader of it exists, so the Run page and the notice cannot come to say different things. The night's steps are drawn in six groups of the steps they hold, prices and calendar, indicators and levels, plans and moves, readings and the list, the records, and what runs after the close, and every stage a night writes is in exactly one of them.

Superseded on 2026-09-28 by **A night's state is read off its own run log rows and its tries, and the pages that state it read that one state**. **Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and scored in shadow before they are shown** The register is an append-only table naming each candidate, its rule, its test and the date it was registered, with a stated maximum family size. Rows are never updated or deleted: a retirement is a new dated row naming what it retires, which is the same convention this document uses for superseded decisions. What pre-registration needs is that you cannot quietly change what you registered after seeing the result, and append-only with an insert timestamp gives that without keeping a fact outside the store. A candidate is evaluated and stored nightly like a live condition and appears nowhere until it has cleared the minimum evidence and the corrected threshold. Nothing is adopted on the strength of the idea, and nothing is chosen after seeing which way the data went.

Superseded on 2026-09-28 by **Candidate conditions are registered before they are scored, and a candidate's picks are shown on the Run page while its outcomes wait for a look**. **The swing filter's results are replayed for the sessions before its first stored night, for the trigger's arrival alone** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, after version 2 opened at a 45% market floor with 2026-09-24 the only night the filter had stored. A pullback's trigger arrives where it first fired within the arrival window, read off the stored results of the sessions before the night, and the sessions before the filter's first stored night held none, so every pullback on the list's first nights failed its trigger for want of them. The `filter-history` verb replays the filter for sessions before its first stored night, from the bars, bands and plans the store holds as of each session and under the open version's settings, through the replay the filter counts run, and stores each member's result under the version `replayed`, which is no filter version. The trigger's arrival reads a replayed result as it reads a night's, and nothing else reads one: no clock counts a replayed session, the shape clock keying its window on the open version and the edge clock and the near misses on theirs, no page draws one, and the filler scores no replayed plan. A session that already holds results and the night's own session are refused whole, and the night that draws a session replaces whatever the session held with its own. Rejected: writing a trial copy's seeded results into the store, which no declared writer wrote; and running the older nights again, which the night refuses because its corporate action refetch would cut a refetched name's later bars (see: Arrival is a trigger that first fired within the last three sessions, and the trade is read from tonight's close).

**The dated screens open from the swing filter's first night, and an evening before it is not drawn** The operator ruled on 2026-09-25, reading the evening of 2026-09-23 drawn with the 340 names its reasons fired: "Lets start from 24th as real night", with the evenings before it made unavailable on tonight's page and on the run page. **Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone** The operator asked on 2026-09-27 for a business state beside each name on tonight's list and a summary of what the numbers say at the head of the report's numbers, and approved the plan with five corrections. Every night, after the swing readings and before the listings, the fundamental reader works out four readings for every member, by arithmetic and no model, from the quarters its newest fetch before that night stored, so a night run again later never reads a quarter from its future. The trajectory is sales against the same quarter a year earlier and the operating margin against a year earlier, over the two newest quarters. The record is the actual earnings a share against the analysts' estimate over the eight newest quarters carrying both, an actual within a cent or 1% of the estimate, whichever is larger, reading as met, stated where four or more count; a quarter with no actual has not reported and is never read as met. Earnings quality is operating cash flow over net income, summed over the four newest quarters: below 0.8 earnings ran ahead of cash, 0.8 to 1.2 is in line, and above 1.2 they were more than backed by it, and a total at or below nought is named a loss and not compared. The valuation position is tonight's multiple placed in the thirds of the range of the quarters' own multiples, stated where eight or more carry one. The state is one word read from the trajectory alone: improving where sales stood above a year earlier and the margin wider in both quarters, deteriorating where sales stood below and the margin narrower in both, steady otherwise, not enough quarters where the trajectory cannot be read, and no fundamentals yet where no quarter is stored. A year earlier, so the seasons fall out; the operating margin, because it is the part of profit the business itself runs, before interest, tax and one-off items; two quarters, so one quarter's one-off cannot move the word; and both signals, so the word says something only where sales and margin agree. The record, the quality and the valuation are drawn beside the state and never move it, so improving can stand beside earnings that ran ahead of cash, and the heading says so in its own words: "What the numbers say: {state}, read from sales and operating margin alone". The report's numbers open with that heading and one sentence per reading, written by code from the fixed patterns section 4 places, each describing the business and none advising a trade. The state orders tonight's list and gates nothing: no gate reads it, a member reading none is judged and listed like any other, and absent is never a failure. The cent is the smallest gap a figure filed to the cent can show, and the provider restates per-share figures to the cent after a split, so a restated figure's cent is a larger share of it: over NFLX's committed capture an exact comparison read 6 beats in eight quarters, where the tolerance reads 2 beats, 4 meets and 2 misses, its quarter to 2025-06-30 reading 0.72 against 0.71 after its 10:1 split. The same capture reads sales 13.4% above a year earlier in the quarter to 2026-06-30 and 16.2% in the quarter before, the margin 33.4% against 34.1% a year earlier and so narrower in the newest quarter and wider in the one before, which is steady, and operating cash flow 0.87 of net income, in line. No study sets the tolerance, the quality's cut points or the state rule, and each was proposed in section 17 to be settled from the members' measured split once every member held quarters or was marked absent. This superseded the ruling that no reading of the fundamentals fires a reason, gates a tranche or draws a panel, whose reason was the path rather than the merit of the readings: the fundamentals were fetched only when a name was opened, so a reading of them covered the names the operator happened to open. The quarters are fetched for every member on the night after it reports, so every member is read by the same rule on the same night, and the state reaches no reason, gate or plan: it orders the list and draws the summary.

Superseded on 2026-09-27 by **Four readings of a member's reported quarters are worked out every night by rules the measured split settled, and its state is read from sales and operating margin alone**. **No reading of the fundamentals fires a reason, gates a tranche or draws a panel** The four readings 6.1 asked about, being a computed trajectory, a guide record, earnings quality and a valuation position, are not computed, and no fundamental state reaches a listing or a plan. The reason is the path rather than the merit of the readings: fundamentals are fetched when a name is opened and by nothing the night runs, so a reason or a gate reading them would either read a store whose population is the names the operator happened to open, which puts two names in the same state on different rows and leaves a shadow candidate unevaluated on most name-nights, or fetch them for every member nightly, which is the per-name request the nightly rule forbids at ten weighted calls each. What is given up is stated: no fundamental state can inform tonight's list or tonight's plan, and the numbers table goes on showing the reported quarters for a reader to weigh. A bulk source covering the index in one request would be a new decision and not a reopening of this one. Ruled at 8.0.

What changed is the path its reason rested on, the quarters now fetched for every member on the night after it reports rather than only when a name is opened and asked for again on the reporting calendar rather than every night, so the four readings are computed, the state they give orders tonight's list, and the report's numbers open with what they say.

**Tonight's list is the swing filter's, and an evening is listed by the rule that listed it** The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 that tonight's list becomes a swing filter, and on 2026-09-25 opened its first version on the swing trade's own plan. From the first night whose filter step records its session, tonight's list is the names the swing filter passes: every gate passed, the trigger arriving inside its window and no exclusion applying, ordered by the trade's reward to risk, higher first, then relative strength, then the setup band's strength, then the ticker, each a fact about the chart or the plan and none a judgement about the company. Each row carries its gates with the values that decided them, and the six reasons are still computed and stored for every member every night and stand beside each row as context. A night the market gate closed draws no row and one line with the breadth and the gate's floor, and a night no name passed draws one line with how many reached each gate, so an empty list says why it is empty. The night records the rule for its session in the swing filter's step, once the filter has stored its rows and before any later step can stop the night, and only where the filter stored rows; the night close is its only writer. An evening with no such record was listed by any of the six reasons firing, which is every evening before the switch, and it is drawn as it was listed, in the order it was drawn in (see: Tonight's list breaks a tie in fired count by the plan's reward to risk, and a row with none is drawn after every row with one and says why). A night run again over a session draws that session again and records its rule, so a session is listed by the rule of the night that drew it last. Every surface that says whether a name was on the list reads each evening by its own rule and names it: tonight's list and its header, a name's page, its listing history and its walk, the universe's last evening and strip, the run page's overlap, the facts retention, the overnight queue, which takes the filter's names first in its order, and the night's own request, which asks for the first name the filter passed and for none on a night none passed. This narrows three entries rather than superseding them, since each still describes code that runs: the list is still built from stated conditions and not a score, the five gates being the conditions (see: Tonight's list is built from stated conditions, not a score); the page still draws twenty and states the true count, the count the filter listed (see: The page shows twenty and states the true count); and the fired-count order still draws every evening the reasons listed. The ruled filter passed a median of three names a night over the stored year, so the list will rarely approach twenty, and that is its selectivity rather than a fault.

What changed is the order the names passing are drawn in, by the state their reported quarters give them first and the filter's own order within a state, and that a night storing no readings keeps the filter's own order wherever it is drawn.

**A page ranks no company as an investment, and a rank it draws is a return's place among the members' returns** Phase 12's second gate reads relative strength, each member's return over 63 and over 126 sessions placed among every member's, and the list orders by reward to risk, then relative strength, then band strength. A press is a request carrying the ask, and the drain answers it with a pass writing every section again rather than only what is missing or stale, at the off-peak rate every pass waits for. **A written section is broken into parts only where its own prose says where each part ends** A model's prose is drawn as the claim checker accepted it, and a section a reader has to search is drawn as one they can scan wherever the prose itself says how. Two sections are drawn as parts. The two cases are two labelled halves where the section is the two paragraphs the writer is asked for. Measured on 2026-09-20 over every sample the repository can reach: the paid lane's risks are ten risks in one paragraph each opening on an ordinal for one name and five paragraphs for another, and the local lane's two answers for that second name are four risks in one paragraph opening on none, which is the section drawn whole. **A name's page for an earlier night is what the store held that night** Section 15.9 gives the name page a second route carrying a date, and what that page draws is the evening's own figures: every read it makes is bounded by that night, so the bars stop there, the bands, the plan and the profile are the ones that night computed, the filings are those filed by then, and a written section is the newest version accepted on or before it. Superseded on 2026-09-26 by **A touch is one visit to a band, counted only where the band holds no swing or retracement from that visit, and it never creates a band**. **A candidate's verdict is read only at looks fixed when it is registered** A fixed-level test repeated as setups accumulate promotes a worthless condition far more often than its level says: at a 35% break-even and a third of 0.05, one read at 250 setups has a size of 1.28%, while reading after every three resolved setups reaches 4.9% by 500, 7.4% by 1,000 and 9.9% by 2,000, and three worthless candidates read nightly have about a 21% chance that one is promoted by 1,000. So each register row carries its looks at registration: at 8, 12 and 16 non-empty blocks, information fractions 0.5, 0.75 and 1.0, triggered by the block count alone and never by where the figure stands, with the level spent across them by a Lan-DeMets function of the O'Brien-Fleming type. The boundaries are derived on the sign-flip null by simulation over stored listings, with the method, the draw count and the seed pinned, because independent increments are not established under overlapping windows. Under independence at 500, 750 and 1,000 setups the crossing win shares would be 42.0%, 39.6% and 38.4%, overall size 1.52% and power 79.6% against a 4.6-point excess. The first look cannot promote: the level released at a fraction of 0.5 is 0.00071, below the floor of 1/256 that 8 blocks allow, so it can only retire; the earliest promotion is at 12 blocks, 756 sessions, about three years, and the maximum at 16, 1,008 sessions, about four years, each plus up to 63 to resolve. Evidence: Armitage, McPherson and Rowe (1969) on repeated significance tests on accumulating data; Lan and DeMets (1983) on spending functions chosen before monitoring; O'Brien and Fleming (1979); FDA (2019) guidance on adaptive designs, that analysis times chosen from results inflate error and that the maximum is not extended. Judgement: the three looks and their spacing.

Superseded on 2026-09-22 by **A candidate's verdict is read only at looks fixed when it is registered, with each look's boundary found over every sign vector its blocks allow**. What changed is how a look's boundary is found, from a simulation over stored listings to an enumeration of every arrangement of the blocks the look holds, which is exact at sixteen blocks and assumes nothing about what the setups inside a block did to each other.

**Nothing a candidate or a version is registered with changes while it runs, and settling a proposed number is a new registration** The calibration's method, inputs and cap, the cost and its sensitivity, the spending function, the looks, the graph, and the boundary simulation's method, draw count and seed are fixed in the register row or pinned in code before the registration command runs, because the running figure is shown every night and a choice made after it has been seen is a free parameter. A proposed number is settled only by a new registration or a new version, with its own window and counted in the lifetime count, and any reading that motivates it happens at a look. The trend confirmation's two nights may be settled from flip-back counts, which are labels and not outcomes, and a changed count is a new version. The margin by which version A must beat version B is fixed at 5 points before scoring and has no settling row. Every threshold phase 10 chose was chosen on per-night counts, with no forward return and no setup outcome read, which is what keeps three an accurate count of what was tried. Evidence: Harvey, Liu and Zhu (2016) that significance hurdles must rise with how much was tried; Sullivan, Timmermann and White (1999).

**Guidance is stored as management's own prose and never parsed into a figure** Section 4 asks for the guided quarter from the earnings release exhibit, and what the exhibit carries is a sentence. No filing carries it in a structured field, which is why it is read from the exhibit at all. The rule this corpus already holds is that a figure is computed from stored data or copied from a payload with the filing date it came from, and a range extracted from a sentence by pattern is neither: it states no population and the same pattern that finds it in one filer's wording misses it in the next (see: Code owns every number). So the archive stores the exhibit's passage, its heading, the document it came from and the date it was filed, and the numbers section shows management's own words rather than a figure. **A claim is a sentence, and every sentence in a researched section names the document it rests on** The two decisions above say every number in prose exists in the facts file and every researched claim names a stored source, and neither says what a claim or a number is when a checker reads a paragraph, so a later session could draw either line anywhere and nothing would show it. A claim is a sentence. Every sentence of a researched section carries a citation, written as a D and the document's position in the section's own source list in square brackets, and the citation has to resolve to a stored row admissibility admitted. A position rather than an id, because a model copying a thirty-two character id into prose is a model given the chance to get one character wrong. The key under each figure is the one section held to the number rule alone, because the lane table says it is a fixed explanation over known values, and asking it to cite a document would be asking it to invent a source for arithmetic.

A figure is a rounding of a value the facts file holds, at the precision the prose itself states: half a unit at the last decimal the prose writes, scaled by the prose's own unit word, so $109.4 billion is a rounding of 109417000000 and $110 billion is not. A percentage is also tried against a value stored as a fraction, which is how the margins are held. The match is on magnitude and not sign, because the sign of a move is carried by the words around it and a signed match would refuse every "fell 3.2%" written from a stored fall, and it is on existence rather than attribution. Some numbers are not figures. A four-digit year standing alone carries no claim about a value. A quarter or half label, a fiscal year, a time of day and an ordinal from first to tenth say which period or which place, and a wrong period is caught by the figure beside it failing to match. The length of a window, the 200 of a 200-day average, has to be a number the facts file carries in the name of a figure or as a count it holds. A date has to be one the file holds. The S&P 500 and the twelve months a filing reports over are named as the two phrases whose digits and words are neither. A number written in words from eleven up, or a scale word standing on its own, is refused as a figure nothing can match, while one to ten are ordinary language and are not read, which is a stated cost: "two segments" where there are three passes.

The checker writes two outcomes and two waypoints. A first refusal is `rejected` and the writer may try once more as the next version; a second consecutive refusal on the same as-of date is `fallback` and the section is left out with both attempts' offending text kept on their own rows. A researched section whose source list holds nothing admitted goes to `fallback` on its first check with the reason that no admissible source was found, because a rewrite cannot create a source. A version following a fallback is a fresh first attempt.

The reader was measured before anything rested on it, over the four captured articles, the two filed releases and 411 articles from one live news request, being 15,412 sentences and 11,727 figures, and two findings from that measurement are the reason this entry states its limits in numbers. The sentence reader holds: of the 15,412, 700 are three words or fewer and the ones read are real short sentences and page furniture rather than splits at an abbreviation. The measurement found three misreads and each is repaired and asserted: a percentage before the word week read as a window, a time of day read as two figures, and a strength of 2 matching 200% through the fraction reading. And the number rule is a strong test for a figure stated with decimals and a weak one for a whole number. Against AAPL's facts file of 38 values, 1,392 of the 11,727 figures, written by nobody with that file in mind, pass by coincidence: 1,301 of 7,939 whole numbers, 76 of 2,091 at one decimal, and 14 of 1,547 at two, some of which are AAPL's own figures. A model inventing "revenue grew 12%" passes where any value near twelve exists. That is not repaired here, because what would repair it is attribution, a figure tied to the fact it rounds, and the prompt that could require prose to name its facts is written at 6.6, which is where the first model-written sections exist to measure it against (owes: A whole-number figure tied to the fact it rounds, measured against written sections). (see: Every number in written prose must exist in the facts file) (see: Every researched claim must name a stored source document)

**A registration names an evaluator the code carries, and its version is a hash of that evaluator's own source** A registered candidate carries the name of an evaluator, the parameters it will be run with, and that evaluator's version, so the row says what will run rather than describing it. A rule written only in prose is re-implemented by whoever scores it, and what they implement is whatever they read the words to mean, which is the thing pre-registration exists to stop: the registration and the thing tested come apart with nothing able to say they have. The version is the hash rather than a number somebody raises, because a number is a second statement of the same fact and drifts the first time a parameter is tuned in a hurry, where a hash cannot. It is taken over the evaluator's source with line endings normalised to LF and any leading byte order mark removed, so the same code hashes the same on both machines and on a runner that checked the tree out with either ending, and over that source less the one line carrying the version, because a hash of a file including its own hash never settles. What this costs is that every change to an evaluator, down to a comment, is a new registration retiring the old one rather than an edit, and that is the cost being bought: a row naming code that has moved is a row naming a rule nothing runs, and `register-append-only` fails one rather than letting a night evaluate it.
